# Direct3D 11 renderer — plan

A second renderer for the Windows build that draws through Direct3D 11
instead of OpenGL. It follows the plan of the Android build's Vulkan renderer
(`port/android/VULKAN.md`), which followed the Switch's deko3d renderer
(`port/switch/DEKO3D.md`): phases, each written out in detail before it is
handed over, each tried on the machine before the next, every result in
"Progress", every phase audited against the source before it is committed.
Read those two plans, and their "Progress", before this one: most of what is
decided here was decided there first, and most of what will go wrong here
went wrong there first. Where this plan differs from them it says so, and
why.

Status: **phase 0 is worked on this PC and committed on `d3d11-backend`**: the probe
(`port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_probe.c`, `ninja windows_d3d11_probe`), its reports on the Adreno and
on WARP, and the OpenGL baseline. The emulated x86 process reaches the Adreno X1-45's own Direct3D 11
driver, at feature level 11_1. The four runs were made again with the debug layer, and the layer
reported nothing, and the agent has seen `present`'s picture through every transition on both
drivers. **Phase 0 is done**: nothing is left for the user. See "Progress". The user decided phase 0's proposals (applied to "Decisions"), and
then the two rows phase 1 depends on (the copies with one shared change; two executables): no row is left to confirm.
**Phase 1 is worked on this PC, committed on `d3d11-backend` and audited**: `ninja windows` makes
`halo.exe` and `halo_d3d11.exe`, whose Direct3D 8 device (cut from `d3d8_gl.c`) draws nothing, whose
backend makes the Direct3D 11 device on the Adreno or WARP, with or without the debug layer, and whose
window has no OpenGL context. It runs the menus and a10's opening into the cryo room with a black
window on both drivers, with no debug-layer message and no GL stub call, and exits clean. The audit
reproduced the object check and found nothing blocking (see "Progress": the audit, the summary and 20
deviations). **Phase 2 is worked on this PC and committed on `d3d11-backend`**: the game's clears on the
screen through Direct3D 11 on the Adreno and on WARP, with the swap chain, the render targets, the
letterboxed present, the marker, screenshots, the clears' self-test and the lost device; the debug layer
silent; the object check and the symbol difference as phase 1's. Open: the Adreno's GPU was reset by
Windows twice in a10 with no frame limit (a driver timeout also seen once on this PC without the
renderer); the user stopped the testing before WARP's a10 and the frame-rate runs. See "Progress": the
summary and 14 deviations. **Phase 2 is audited**: the record is true to the code and nothing is blocking ("Progress",
"Phase 2 — audit"); the user ended the testing where the summary says, and accepted it. **Phase 3 is worked on this PC and
committed on `d3d11-backend`**: every draw's vertices and indices are copied into Direct3D 11 buffers at the draw (the stream
and index rings, the mirror's segments through an upload ring), from `d3d8_gl.c`'s bookkeeping, on the game's real traffic,
with the KB mirrored and streamed a frame as `halo.exe`'s for the same scenes; the memory self-test `ok` on the Adreno and on
WARP, with and without the layer; the layer silent; nothing skipped; no lost device. Not run, by the user's decision: a10 on
WARP. See "Progress": the summary and 14 deviations. **Phase 3 is audited**: the record is true to the code and nothing is
blocking ("Progress", "Phase 3 — audit"); the memory self-test, the menus and a10 on the Adreno were run again and agree with the
summary. **Phase 4 is worked, committed on `d3d11-backend` and audited**, run without the user from
`HANDOFF-d3d11-phase4-5.md`: the generators' copies emit HLSL, and every shader of the corpus (the PC and Xbox menus, a10 to the
cryo room with and without per-pixel lighting: 33 vertex and 240 pixel shaders) compiles with `d3dcompiler_47.dll` and is its
GLSL twin with the dialect's changes and nothing else; the shader self-test is `ok` on the Adreno and on WARP, with and without
the layer. The audit reproduced the checks and found nothing blocking; its one code finding (the high-res override's checks in
the key) is left for phase 6. **Phase 5 is worked, committed on `d3d11-backend` and audited**: every draw asks the shader
service for its shaders (kept on the disk), its input layout and its four state objects, none bound; a cold start of the menus
and a10 compiles 239 shaders in 4.2 s of `D3DCompile`, a second start reads them in 43 ms, a10 makes 31 layouts and 22/9/3/19
states, the four self-tests are `ok` on both drivers and the cryo room runs at 122.9 frames a second (phase 3: 121.9). The audit
found nothing blocking; its code findings (the attribute slot's elements always four floats, four small ones in the service) and
the worker's (a10's blend by the constant's alpha and colour at once) are for phase 6's "Step 0". **Phases 4 and 5 are
audited by the user's session as well** ("Progress", "Phases 4 and 5 — the user's audit"): the reading and the runs were made
again and agree with the records; nothing is blocking and nothing is added to phase 6's "Step 0". **Phase 6 is written out in detail**
("Phase 6 — draws, textures and render targets") with its brief, `HANDOFF-d3d11-phase6.md`, to run without the user as
phases 4 and 5 did. **Phase 6 steps 0 to 3 are worked and committed on `d3d11-backend`**, to be audited: the game's draws
go through Direct3D 11 (`win32_d3d11_draw.c`), its textures through a copy of the texture cache
(`xbox_textures_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11_textures.c`), and the PC and Xbox menus look as under `halo.exe` by screenshot on
the Adreno and on WARP, with the GL stub count 0, the layer silent and `halo.exe` changed by the accessors alone
("Progress", "Phase 6 — summary"). Steps 4 to 6 (a map, render targets, visibility tests) follow.

---

## Why

On Windows the game renders through OpenGL 4.5 (`port/linux/src/d3d8_gl.c`,
the renderer shared with Linux). On Windows that is the second-class API:

- **On Windows on ARM there is no native OpenGL.** The Snapdragon X's Adreno
  GPU has Direct3D 11, Direct3D 12 and Vulkan drivers; OpenGL comes from
  Microsoft's OpenCL and OpenGL Compatibility Pack, which is Mesa's GL
  translated onto Direct3D 12. The game, an x86 executable, already runs
  under emulation there; its graphics then go through a second translation.
  Whatever that layer gets wrong or slow, the game gets wrong or slow.
- **On every other Windows GPU, Direct3D 11 is the driver's first API**: the
  one the driver was written for, tested against and tuned, where OpenGL is
  often a thinner or older path (Intel's integrated GPUs most of all).

So the renderer exists to draw the game on the API every Windows driver
does well, first on Windows on ARM. That decides three things:

- **Correctness first, speed as the result.** Each phase is accepted on what
  it draws, compared with the OpenGL renderer by screenshot, and nothing is
  done for speed alone. Speed is measured once, in phase 7, against OpenGL
  on the same machine: the number that says whether the renderer achieved
  its purpose. The OpenGL renderer's structures that exist for speed (the
  mirror of the game's memory, the state cache, the index extent cache) are
  kept in the copy, because they are proven and free.
- **The test machine is this PC**: a Snapdragon X Plus (Windows 11, Arm64)
  with a Qualcomm Adreno X1-45, running the x86 build under emulation. Every
  phase is tried here, on the GPU and on WARP (below).
- **The OpenGL renderer stays, untouched, selectable for good** and the
  comparison in phase 7 is against it.

Two things this does not assume: that the OpenGL path's faults, if any, are
the translation layer's (some may be in `d3d8_gl.c`'s use of GL, and a
renderer copied from it will carry them: a fault seen on the GPU and on WARP
is ours), and that the emulated x86 process reaches the Adreno's Direct3D 11
driver at all, rather than Microsoft's software adapter. Phase 0's first
question is which adapter the process gets.

---

## Decisions

Taken from the Vulkan plan unless the row says otherwise.

| Question | Decision |
|---|---|
| What the renderer is for | **Correctness on the driver's own API, for x86 Windows machines first** (Intel, AMD and NVIDIA GPUs under no emulation); Windows on ARM is the second tier (the user's decision after phase 0). This PC, a Snapdragon running the game under emulation, is the only machine to test on, so it stands in for both. Speed measured in phase 7, not pursued before (see "Why"). |
| Shared code with the OpenGL renderer | **Copied**, not extracted, as for Vulkan and deko3d: `d3d8_gl.c`, `xbox_textures.c`, `nv2a_vsh.c`, `nv2a_psh.c`, `xgpu_post.c`, `gl_functions.c` stay untouched so Linux and Android keep working; Direct3D versions are copies under `port/windows/d3d11/`. Files under `port/linux/src` change only by read-only accessors behind a guard (as the deko3d and Vulkan work added behind `HALO_SWITCH` and `HALO_ANDROID`), by rows in `port_config.c`'s settings table, and by **one real change: `sdl_platform.c` learns to make a window with no OpenGL context** (below, "The platform layer"), behind `_WIN32` and a flag the Direct3D device sets, so Linux and Android compile the same code. (The user confirmed both, the copies and the one shared change, before phase 1.) |
| Where the renderer lives | **In the game's process**, as the OpenGL one does: no guest and host, no command stream, no hand-over. It is split in two halves by a header boundary the Windows build already has (below, "Where the renderer lives"): the Direct3D 8 device in the Xbox SDK's world, the Direct3D 11 backend in the Windows SDK's, calling each other directly at every draw. |
| How the renderer is chosen | **Two executables** for the length of the plan: `build/windows/halo.exe` (OpenGL, as now) and `build/windows/halo_d3d11.exe`, the same objects with the renderer's replaced, as the Android build links two guest images. `display.renderer` is not a Windows setting until phase 8, which decides whether the two merge (one executable choosing at start, or the OpenGL one starting the other). (The user confirmed the two executables "for now", before phase 1: phase 8 revisits.) |
| The game's memory | **Copied by Direct3D calls at the draw** (`Map` with no-overwrite into a ring; the mirror's pages through `STAGING` buffers), never read in place: Direct3D 11 cannot read the process's memory from the GPU, and the OpenGL renderer copies at the draw too. **The mirror is kept**: the contiguous window's pages in `DEFAULT` buffers per segment, uploaded when first drawn from or after the game wrote them (`memory_watch`, `port/windows/src/win32_memory_watch.c`, already the Windows build's), with its volatile-page rule as `d3d8_gl.c` has it. **The pages go through a buffer and `CopySubresourceRegion` into the segment**, not `UpdateSubresource`: phase 0 measured 1.7 ms of CPU a frame for a whole 4 MB segment that way against 4 ms (the user's decision, for performance on any machine). Phase 0 proposed a ring of `STAGING` buffers, one a frame in flight; phase 3 refines that to one `DYNAMIC` upload ring written with `Map` `NO_OVERWRITE` (a `STAGING` buffer's `Map` waits for the copies still queued from it, and the mirror writes dozens of runs a frame; see phase 3). No busy tracking: `IsBusy` false, locks return at once, as now. |
| Shader compiler | **`D3DCompile` from `d3dcompiler_47.dll`** (a component of Windows 10 and 11; present here in `System32` and `SysWOW64`), HLSL for shader model 4.0 (feature level 10's model, enough for every shader the generators make; 5.0 only if phase 0 finds a reason). **The bytecode is cached on disk**, so each shader is compiled once per machine and generator version. **Compiled at the draw that needs it, synchronously**, as the OpenGL renderer compiles its GLSL: a first-sight hitch of a few milliseconds, the same as now. Phase 0 measured 8.5 to 26 ms for a game's pixel shader and 24 to 146 ms for a vertex shader on this PC, but under x86 emulation, which the target machines do not have: the user's decision is to assume the compile is fast on them and keep it synchronous. `vs_4_0`/`ps_4_0` at `D3DCOMPILE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL3` (5.0 is no faster and its bytecode larger); the DLL loaded with `LoadLibrary`. The Vulkan plan's compile thread, with the draw skipped, stays the fallback if testers' x86 machines or Windows on ARM show hitches. Phase 5 measured the first run here: the menus and a10 to the cryo room compile 239 shaders in 4.2 s of `D3DCompile` in all (17.7 ms each on average, under emulation), spread over the scenes' first seconds (at most about 1 s of compiles in a 5 s span, at the opening and the cinematic); a second start reads them from the cache in 43 ms. |
| Pipeline state | Direct3D 11's immutable state objects (blend, depth-stencil, rasterizer, sampler), one per distinct state, made the first time and kept by hash; input layouts one per (vertex declaration, vertex shader signature). Nothing is "not ready" but a shader being compiled. |
| Validation | **The Direct3D 11 debug layer** (`D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_DEBUG`; needs the "Graphics Tools" optional feature of Windows, which the user installs once) in debug builds from phase 2 on, its messages to the log and counted, run at the end of every step. A machine without the layer runs without it, logged. |
| The second driver | **WARP**, Microsoft's software rasterizer (`D3D_DRIVER_TYPE_WARP`), as the Vulkan plan used Turnip: every phase is tried on the GPU and on WARP. A fault seen on both is the renderer's; a fault seen on one is the driver's. `display.d3d11_adapter` chooses: empty for the first hardware adapter, `"warp"`, or part of an adapter's name. |
| Feature level | **11_0 required, nothing of 11_1 used** (the Adreno and WARP offer 11_1; 11_0 keeps more of the testers' GPUs): BC1 to BC3, exact occlusion queries, every format the renderer relies on. A machine without 11_0 gets a log line and no picture; the OpenGL executable is its renderer. |
| The 16-bit colour formats | **Decoded to BGRA8**, as most formats are decoded now. On the Adreno, B5G5R5A1 and B4G4R4A4 are sampled but are not render targets and have no mip autogen, and the Custom Edition caches' channel order is fixed in the decode anyway. B5G6R5 is complete on both drivers and may go native later if phase 7 shows the decode costs time. |
| Presenting | **`DXGI_SWAP_EFFECT_FLIP_DISCARD`, two buffers, `B8G8R8A8_UNORM`, `DXGI_SCALING_NONE`**, on SDL's `HWND`, with `MakeWindowAssociation(DXGI_MWA_NO_ALT_ENTER \| DXGI_MWA_NO_WINDOW_CHANGES)`. `DXGI_SWAP_CHAIN_FLAG_ALLOW_TEARING` when the factory supports it, and `DXGI_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING` with sync interval 0 when `display.vsync` is false. **Fullscreen is SDL's alone** (borderless and SDL's display-mode fullscreen); DXGI's `SetFullscreenState` is never used. `ResizeBuffers` with the same flags on every `SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_PIXEL_SIZE_CHANGED`. (Phase 0: every transition presented again within 110 ms on both drivers; a minimised window presents `S_OK`.) |
| What players share | **Nothing.** Each machine compiles what it meets and keeps it. |
| Devices | This PC, on the Adreno and on WARP, is what each phase is accepted on, standing in for the x86 machines the renderer is for. Other GPUs (Intel, AMD, NVIDIA, under no emulation) are tried in phase 7 by testers. |

---

## Where the renderer lives

The Windows build compiles two kinds of unit (`tools/windows_build.py`,
`port/windows/README.md`): the game and the Xbox-facing platform units see
the Xbox SDK's headers (`port/include/xdk`), and the Windows-facing units
(`port/windows/src/win32_*.c`) see the Windows SDK's. The two define the
same names differently, so no unit sees both. Direct3D 11 is the Windows
SDK's (`d3d11.h`, `dxgi.h`, `d3dcompiler.h`); the Direct3D 8 device is the
Xbox SDK's (`D3DDevice_*`, `D3DSurface`, `D3D__RenderState`).

So the renderer is split at the draw, as the Vulkan and deko3d renderers
are, but by a header and not by an address space:

- **The device** (`d3d8_d3d11.c`, Xbox SDK): the `D3DDevice_*` entry points,
  the state they keep, the vertex declarations, reading the state back at
  each draw, the shader keys and the HLSL generation, the texture cache and
  decoding, the mirror's bookkeeping.
- **The backend** (`win32_d3d11.c`, Windows SDK): the device, the swap
  chain, buffers, textures, state objects, shaders, queries and draws, as
  plain C functions the device calls at every draw, taking fixed-width
  integers, floats and `const void *` to the game's memory.

The boundary header, `d3d11_backend.h`, includes neither SDK: `<stdint.h>`
and `<stddef.h>` only, so both halves include it. A pointer to the game's
memory crosses it as it is (one process, 32 bits), and the backend copies
what it points at during the call (`UpdateSubresource`, `Map`): nothing of
the game's memory is read later.

Direct3D 11 from C is COM through its C macros (`COBJMACROS`:
`ID3D11Device_CreateBuffer(device, ...)`), which `d3d11.h` supports; the
backend stays C like the rest of the port.

### The platform layer

`sdl_platform.c` (shared with Linux and Android) makes the window with
`SDL_WINDOW_OPENGL`, creates the GL context, loads the GL entry points and
swaps at each present. Under Direct3D 11 there is no host to stand in for
it, so it gains a mode of its own: the window made without `SDL_WINDOW_OPENGL`,
no context, no entry points loaded, no swap: the device's `Present` calls
the backend's present itself (phase 2) and then `platform_video_swap`, which
in this mode only paces (the frame limiter as now; `display.vsync` is the
present's sync interval once there is a present, and the display's refresh
rate as the limiter's interval before that). Behind `_WIN32` and a flag the
Direct3D device sets before it asks for the window, so the OpenGL executable
takes the path it takes today. This is the one change to shared code that
is not an accessor, and the Decisions table says so; phase 1 writes it out.

The GL calls left in shared files the Direct3D executable links
(`hud_hires.c` 7, `text_hires.c` 8, `menu_files.c` 2, `sdl_platform.c` 1,
and `xbox_textures.c`'s 15 until phase 6 replaces it) go to
`gl_functions.c`'s pointers, which are null without a context: a call is a
crash, not a no-op as on Android. The Direct3D executable links a generated
`gl_stubs.c` in its place: every entry point of `gl.h`'s desktop list points
at a stub of its real signature (`__stdcall` on x86: the callee pops the
arguments, so the signature must be right) that counts the call and returns
nothing, and the count is in the statistics line (phase 1), to be zero by
the end of phase 6 as the Vulkan plan's was.

### Clip space, the one place this is simpler

`d3d8_gl.c`'s desktop branch already draws in Direct3D's clip space
(`glClipControl(GL_UPPER_LEFT, GL_ZERO_TO_ONE)`: depth 0 to 1, row 0 at the
top), and its vertex shaders carry Direct3D 8's half-pixel offset (`+ 0.5`),
which Direct3D 11 needs as well (its pixel centres are at half-integers, as
OpenGL's are; the Xbox's were at integers). So the HLSL is the desktop
branch's without a flip, the front face is the desktop branch's, and the
viewport is the game's: none of the orientation questions the Vulkan plan's
phases 4 and 6 had to settle. Phase 6 step 1 checks the picture anyway.

### What Direct3D 11 does not have, checked against the game

- **Point size and point fill.** The game draws no point lists
  (`D3DPT_POINTLIST` and `D3DRS_POINTSIZE` appear nowhere in `source/`) and
  never asks for `D3DFILL_POINT`: `gl_PointSize` and `GL_POINT` go, nothing
  replaces them.
- **Texture view swizzles** (`GL_TEXTURE_SWIZZLE_*` in `xbox_textures.c`:
  Custom Edition caches' channel order, `docs/custom_edition_caches.md`).
  The channels are reordered in the decode instead, where the texels are
  copied anyway (a BC texture whose order is not the Xbox's is decoded to
  BGRA8 and reordered: rare). The Vulkan and deko3d copies solved this for
  their caches (commit `f0218328`); the Direct3D copy does as they do.
- **A blit with scaling** (`glBlitFramebuffer` at present, letterboxed): a
  full-screen draw with a linear sampler. A blit without scaling (the mip
  composite) is `CopySubresourceRegion`.
- **Line width** is 1, as the Vulkan plan found enough. **Logic ops** are not
  used.

Direct3D 11 has, where Vulkan had not: any border colour on a sampler,
exact occlusion counts, `GenerateMips`, and a device that creates resources
and shaders from any thread.

---

## Files

New, Windows only, under `port/windows/d3d11/` (the build compiles
`win32_*.c` there against the Windows SDK and the rest against the Xbox
SDK, as it does `port/windows/src`):

| File | From | What |
|---|---|---|
| `d3d8_d3d11.c` | `d3d8_gl.c` | entry points and state kept; GL calls replaced by backend calls |
| `xbox_textures_d3d11.c` | `xbox_textures.c` | format decoding and the cache kept; textures made and filled through the backend; channel order in the decode |
| `nv2a_vsh_hlsl.c`, `nv2a_psh_hlsl.c` | `nv2a_vsh.c`, `nv2a_psh.c` | HLSL (phase 4) |
| `win32_d3d11_post.c` | `xgpu_post.c` | FXAA and SMAA as HLSL (SMAA's source is HLSL first; phase 6), as a backend unit: every piece of a pass is a Direct3D object |
| `hlsl_shaders.h` | — | constant buffer layouts, semantics, the generators' prototypes; fixed-width types only |
| `d3d11_backend.h` | — | the boundary: what the device calls, with neither SDK's types |
| `win32_d3d11.c` (and what it splits into as it grows: `win32_d3d11_present.c`, `_memory.c`, `_shaders.c`, and in phase 6 `_draw.c`, `_textures.c`, `_post.c`) | — | the Direct3D 11 backend |
| `win32_d3d11_probe.c` | — | phase 0's probe, kept as the reporting tool |
| `build/windows/gl_stubs.c` (generated by `tools/windows_gl_stubs.py`) | `gl.h`'s desktop list | the counted stubs for the GL calls shared files still make, one per entry point with its real signature (phase 1) |

Changed:

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `tools/windows_build.py`, `port/windows/port.json` | the second executable: the Direct3D files in place of the OpenGL renderer's; `d3d11.lib`, `dxgi.lib` (and `d3dcompiler.lib`, or `LoadLibrary`); phase 6: `xbox_textures.c` left out of it as `d3d8_gl.c` is |
| `port/linux/src/sdl_platform.c` | the no-context mode (above) |
| `port/linux/src/port_config.c` | the settings' rows, `_platform_windows` |
| `port/linux/src/hud_hires.c`, `text_hires.c`, `menu_files.c` (and their headers) | the read-only accessors' guards widened to the Windows build (they exist for the Switch and Android already; phase 6) |
| `port/windows/README.md` | the executable, the settings, how to report a problem |

Unchanged and shared: `d3d8_resources.c`, `win32_memory_watch.c`, the rest
of the platform layer.

---

## Phase 0 — spike

A probe, a program of its own built from one Windows-facing unit, that
answers the questions the rest of the plan depends on before any of the
renderer is written, and a baseline: the OpenGL build on this PC, measured
and photographed, which every later phase is compared against. The Vulkan
plan's phase 0 (`port/android/VULKAN.md`, part A, and
`port/android/host/host_vk_probe.c`) is the model: the report format, the
guard that leaves a crashing step out of the next run, the timing rules.
Read that section and that file's head (its first 60 lines, `rep`,
`guard_begin`, `step_table`) first.

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far. The
results go into "Progress" before phase 1 starts; the probe stays in the
tree as the way a tester's machine is reported later (phase 7).

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** the probe (`port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_probe.c`), the build
  changes that make it (`build/windows/d3d11_probe.exe`), its reports for
  the Adreno and for WARP in `port/windows/probe/reports/`, and the
  baseline's numbers and pictures.
- **It is not** the renderer. No file under `port/linux/src` changes. No
  Direct3D 8 device, no `halo_d3d11.exe`, no setting in `config.toml`. The
  probe is written to measure, not to be the backend: phase 2 may copy
  pieces (the swap chain's handling, the adapter choice) once they are
  proven here.
- **The OpenGL build does not change.** Its executable is built here only
  to be measured.

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_probe.c` | the probe: the five steps below |
| `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` | one paragraph: what the folder is (the Direct3D 11 renderer's, this plan) and how to build and run the probe |
| `tools/windows_build.py` | the `windows_d3d11_probe` target (below) |
| `port/windows/probe/reports/` | the reports (below) |
| `port/windows/probe/*.hlsl` | the four hand-converted shaders and the trivial pair (step 3) |
| `port/windows/README.md` | a line under "Find problems" (or the nearest section) naming the probe |

### The probe's shape

- **A program**, not a mode of the game: `d3d11_probe.exe [steps]
  [--adapter hardware|warp|<part of a name>] [--shaders <folder>]
  [--report <file>] [--sync 0|1]`. `steps` is `all` (the default) or a
  comma list of `caps,present,compile,memory,queries`, run in that order
  whatever order they are written in. `caps` always runs, because every
  step needs what it finds (the Vulkan probe's deviation 7).
- **Windows-facing.** The unit sees the Windows SDK only (`<windows.h>`,
  `<d3d11.h>`, `<dxgi1_6.h>`, `<d3dcompiler.h>`, `<SDL3/SDL.h>`) with
  `COBJMACROS` and `WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN`, so it is compiled with the build's
  `WIN32_FLAGS` like `win32_*.c` is. C, `-std=gnu11`, as the rest of the
  port. Whatever else the headers need to compile under clang's
  `i686-pc-windows-msvc` target (a define, a warning silenced) is written
  into "Progress" as the build's first finding: phase 1's backend is
  compiled the same way.
- **The report.** Every result is a line `section.key: value`, one fact
  each, written to the report file (default `d3d11_probe.txt` in the
  current directory; `--report` names another) and to standard output with
  the prefix `d3d11 probe:`. The file is opened at the start and flushed
  after every line. The first lines: the build (the git hash and
  `+changes` if the tree is dirty, taken at build time into
  `build/windows/d3d11_probe_build.h` by a small generated header, as the
  Android build's `tools/android_build_stamp.py` does; copy its approach),
  the machine (`GetNativeSystemInfo`'s architecture, `IsWow64Process2`'s
  native and process machines: the process is x86 under an Arm64 host
  here, and the report must say so), the OS version (`RtlGetVersion`), the
  steps asked for and the adapter asked for.
- **The guard.** A step, or a risky sub-step, writes its name to
  `d3d11_probe_running.txt` beside the report before it runs and clears it
  after; a run that finds the file at start appends that name to
  `d3d11_probe_skip.txt`, says so in the report (`guard.<name>: skipped (it
  killed the probe once; delete d3d11_probe_skip.txt to try again)`) and
  leaves it out. The Vulkan probe's `guard_begin`/`guard_end` with its
  part-B fix (`guard_end` restores the step's own name, not nothing).
- **Errors.** Every `HRESULT` that is not `S_OK` is reported with its hex
  value and, where DXGI or the device has one, its name (a small table of
  the common ones: `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED`, `E_INVALIDARG`,
  `DXGI_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED`, `DXGI_ERROR_NOT_FOUND`, `E_OUTOFMEMORY`). A
  step that fails reports `step.<name>: failed (<where>, <hresult>)` and
  the next step runs. Nothing is called through a null interface.
- **The debug layer.** The device is made with `D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_DEBUG`
  first; if that fails with `DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING` (the
  Graphics Tools feature is not installed) it is made without, and the
  report says which (`debug_layer: yes|no (<hresult>)`). With the layer,
  the `ID3D11InfoQueue` is read after every step and every message is
  reported (`debug.message: <severity> <id> <text>`), with a count at the
  end; a step whose messages name a mistake in the probe is fixed, not
  explained away.
- **Timing** is `QueryPerformanceCounter` for wall time and
  `GetThreadTimes` for one thread's CPU time; GPU time where a step asks
  for it is a `D3D11_QUERY_TIMESTAMP_DISJOINT` pair. Every timing is
  several runs: the first on its own, then the minimum and median of the
  rest.
- **The adapter.** `--adapter hardware` (the default) takes the first
  adapter `IDXGIFactory6::EnumAdapterByGpuPreference` lists for high
  performance that is not the software adapter, or the first adapter at
  all if that interface is missing; `warp` is `D3D_DRIVER_TYPE_WARP`; a
  name takes the first adapter whose description contains it. Feature
  levels asked for: 11_1, 11_0, 10_1, 10_0 in that order, so the report can
  say what the adapter offers even below the plan's minimum.
- **When it ends**, every object is released in reverse order (with the
  layer on, `ReportLiveDeviceObjects` with `D3D11_RLDO_SUMMARY` says
  whether anything leaked; the count goes into the report), the report says
  `probe.done: yes`, and the program exits 0; a failed step makes it exit 1.

### The build

- **Configure with `--portable`.** `-march=native` asks clang for this
  machine's processor, which is an ARM one, so the default flag is wrong on
  this PC (and a portable build is what emulation runs). If `configure.py`
  without `--portable` happens to work here, do not rely on it; the
  commands in "Progress" say what was used.
- **The probe target**, `ninja windows_d3d11_probe`, in
  `tools/windows_build.py` after the game's own emit: the one unit compiled
  with `win32_cflags` (the Windows SDK's headers come through clang's MSVC
  environment, as they do for the `win32_*.c` units today), linked with the
  `windows_link` rule into `build/windows/d3d11_probe.exe` with `SDL3.lib`
  (the fetched `SDL3-devel` package's `lib/x86`, as `halo.exe` links it),
  `d3d11.lib`, `dxgi.lib`, `d3dcompiler.lib`, `user32.lib`, `shell32.lib`
  and whatever else the link asks for; `SDL3.dll` beside it is `windows`'s
  already. A console program (`/SUBSYSTEM:CONSOLE`), so its output is seen.
  It is **not** an input of the `windows` phony target: the CI's build is
  unchanged.
- The build stamp header above, generated by a rule of its own so it is
  written at every build (the Vulkan probe's lesson: a stamp taken at
  configure time goes stale).

### Step 0: the baseline (the OpenGL build on this PC)

Not a probe step. The number and the pictures every later phase is held
against.

1. `python configure.py --portable --release` and `ninja windows`: the
   OpenGL game builds on this machine. The build's log is the first thing
   "Progress" records if it does not, and the phase waits there.
2. A `config.toml` beside `build/windows/halo.exe` with `paths.data`
   naming the folder that holds `maps\` (the user says where),
   `debug.gpu_stats = true`, `debug.telnet_console = true`,
   `debug.screenshot_directory` set to a folder and `debug.screenshot_every
   = 300`. Run `halo.exe` from a console. The log's `OpenGL <version> on
   <renderer>` line (`halo.log` beside the executable in a release build,
   the console in a debug one) goes into "Progress" word for word: it names
   what GL stack the emulated process gets (expected: Mesa's GL over
   Direct3D 12 naming the Adreno; if it names Microsoft's software
   renderer, say so, loudly).
3. **The scenes**, each 60 seconds once settled, the `debug.gpu_stats`
   line's presents a second read from the log as the frame rate (minimum,
   median and maximum of the sixty one-second lines), and one screenshot
   each, kept as the reference picture:
   - the main menu, idle;
   - the first campaign map's opening, standing still: start the map from
     the script console (`debug.telnet_console`: connect to 127.0.0.1:2323
     and send `map_name a10`, the first campaign map; the Linux README's
     "developer console" section has the commands), wait for the cinematic
     to end, then `debug.test_input = "look:1"` set beforehand turns and
     looks without moving;
   - the first campaign map's opening cinematic, from the same start: the
     same frames on every run, so the steadiest heavy scene to compare
     (the user's decision after phase 0, in place of the first fight played
     by hand, which differs on every run).
   With `display.vsync = false` for the numbers (and `display.max_fps` set
   to no limit: `frame_interval_ns` in `sdl_platform.c` says what value
   that is), so the frame rate is the renderer's and not the display's. The
   resolution, the window mode and every setting that changed from the
   defaults are written with the numbers.
4. **The shader dump** for step 3: `debug.gpu_dump_shaders` naming a folder,
   through the same scenes; the folder's file count and total size into
   "Progress". The folder is not committed; four files from it are (step 3).

### Step 1: what the adapter is (`caps`)

Logged in full, so that the rest of the plan can be decided from the report
and not from memory:

- **Every adapter** DXGI lists: `Description`, `VendorId`, `DeviceId`,
  dedicated and shared memory, `DXGI_ADAPTER_FLAG_SOFTWARE`, the driver's
  version (`IDXGIAdapter::CheckInterfaceSupport` with `IID_IDXGIDevice`
  gives the user-mode driver's version). The one the probe chose, and why.
- **The first finding of the plan**: with `--adapter hardware`, is the
  device on the Adreno (the description names it; Qualcomm's `VendorId` is
  0x5143 on this driver, 0x4D4F4351 on older ones) or on Microsoft's Basic
  Render Driver? And the **feature level** the device came up at,
  `D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_DEBUG` accepted or not, the runtime's
  `ID3D11Device5`/`ID3D11DeviceContext4` interfaces present or not (the
  version of Direct3D 11 the runtime offers), and `IDXGIFactory6` present
  or not.
- **The options** from `CheckFeatureSupport`: `D3D11_FEATURE_THREADING`
  (concurrent creates, command lists), `D3D11_FEATURE_D3D11_OPTIONS`
  (`MapNoOverwriteOnDynamicConstantBuffer`, `MapNoOverwriteOnDynamicBufferSRV`,
  `ConstantBufferOffsetting`), `D3D11_FEATURE_D3D11_OPTIONS2`
  (`MapOnDefaultTextures`), `D3D11_FEATURE_D3D9_OPTIONS1`, and
  `D3D11_FEATURE_D3D11_OPTIONS1`'s `TiledResourcesTier` for the record.
- **Formats:** for each DXGI format the renderer could map a texel kind of
  `xbox_textures.c` to, `CheckFormatSupport`'s bits for `TEXTURE2D`,
  `TEXTURE3D`, `TEXTURECUBE`, `SHADER_SAMPLE`, `MIP_AUTOGEN`,
  `RENDER_TARGET`, `BLENDABLE`, `DEPTH_STENCIL`, `MULTISAMPLE_RENDERTARGET`,
  `MULTISAMPLE_RESOLVE`, `DISPLAY`, and `CheckMultisampleQualityLevels` for 2,
  4 and 8 samples on the colour and depth formats. At least:
  `B8G8R8A8_UNORM`, `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`, `B5G6R5_UNORM`, `B5G5R5A1_UNORM`,
  `B4G4R4A4_UNORM`, `R8_UNORM`, `R8G8_UNORM`, `A8_UNORM`, `R16_UNORM`,
  `R16G16_UNORM`, `R8G8_SNORM`, `R16G16_SNORM`, `BC1_UNORM`, `BC2_UNORM`,
  `BC3_UNORM`, `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`, `D32_FLOAT_S8X24_UINT`, `D16_UNORM`,
  `D32_FLOAT`, `R16_UINT` (indices), and the vertex formats
  `attribute_format` in `d3d8_gl.c` maps to (`R32G32B32A32_FLOAT` and the
  shorter floats, `R16G16_SINT`/`SNORM` and their four-component forms,
  `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`, `R32_UINT`), each with `IA_VERTEX_BUFFER`.
- **The limits** the plan relies on: constant buffer size (4,096 float4 on
  every feature level; the vertex constants are 192), sixteen input slots
  and elements, the texture dimensions of the feature level, the maximum
  anisotropy, the `D3D11_REQ_*` constants named for the record.
- **The 32-bit address space**: `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`'s available virtual
  memory before and after the device is made (step 4 adds a figure after
  64 MB of buffers).

### Step 2: a frame on the screen (`present`)

Run last, because it waits for the tester. What phase 2's present will be:

- An SDL3 window (`SDL_CreateWindow` with `SDL_WINDOW_RESIZABLE |
  SDL_WINDOW_HIGH_PIXEL_DENSITY`, **without `SDL_WINDOW_OPENGL`**), its
  `HWND` from `SDL_GetPointerProperty(SDL_GetWindowProperties(window),
  SDL_PROP_WINDOW_WIN32_HWND_POINTER, NULL)`; a swap chain on it from
  `IDXGIFactory2::CreateSwapChainForHwnd`: `DXGI_SWAP_EFFECT_FLIP_DISCARD`,
  two buffers, `B8G8R8A8_UNORM`, `DXGI_SCALING_NONE`, no MSAA; then
  `MakeWindowAssociation(hwnd, DXGI_MWA_NO_ALT_ENTER |
  DXGI_MWA_NO_WINDOW_CHANGES)` so DXGI leaves the window to SDL. Each frame
  clears the back buffer to a colour that cycles over a few seconds, draws
  a small built-in triangle over it (a vertex and pixel shader compiled
  with `D3DCompile` at the start: the step's own proof that the compiler is
  reachable from this process) and presents with the sync interval
  `--sync` gives (1 by default; the step is run twice, 1 then 0).
- **Resizing and modes.** On `SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_PIXEL_SIZE_CHANGED` the
  back buffer's view is released and `ResizeBuffers(0, 0, 0,
  DXGI_FORMAT_UNKNOWN, 0)` is called; every resize is logged with the new
  size and the result. The tester's script (below) resizes, makes the
  window fullscreen both of SDL's ways (`SDL_SetWindowFullscreen` with a
  NULL mode: borderless; and with the display's current mode: exclusive),
  restores it, minimises and restores, and presses Alt+Enter (nothing
  should happen). Each transition: did presenting go on, did `Present`
  return anything but `S_OK` (`DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED` while minimised is
  expected; what else?), how long the transition took.
- **Pacing**: the interval between presents measured at the CPU after
  `Present` returns: minimum, median, maximum and the count more than 1.5
  times the median, over each ten seconds; the display's refresh rate
  beside it (`SDL_GetCurrentDisplayMode`). With `--sync 1` it should be the
  refresh; with `--sync 0` whatever the GPU gives. Also
  `DXGI_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING` with sync 0 if the factory says it is
  supported (`CheckFeatureSupport(DXGI_FEATURE_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING)`):
  supported or not goes into the report.
- **SDL beside Direct3D**: the first gamepad opened and every button press
  logged by name; keyboard events logged; a one-second tone through an SDL
  audio stream when the step starts.
- **The tester's script**, printed when the step starts: watch the colour
  cycle; resize the window by its edge; press F11 (the probe toggles
  borderless), F10 (exclusive), Escape (windowed); F9 cycles three window
  sizes; minimise and restore; Alt+Enter; A on a gamepad or Enter if the
  picture looks right, Y or Backspace if not, B or Escape twice to end.
  With no input for 120 seconds the step ends on its own and says so. The
  agent can run this step itself by sending the keys to the window
  (PowerShell's `SendKeys` after `AppActivate`) where the user is not there
  to; the user does it once by hand before the phase is accepted.

### Step 3: the shader compiler (`compile`)

**Which shaders.** From step 0's dump: the largest vertex shader, the
largest pixel shader (most texture stages and combiner stages), and a
typical one of each (the median size). Convert each to HLSL by hand,
changing only what HLSL needs and keeping the bodies: `#version` and
`precision` gone; the loose uniforms into one `cbuffer` per stage with
every member a `float4` or an array of them (`c[192]` stays `float4
c[192]`); `in`/`out` into a struct of `TEXCOORDn` semantics with
`SV_Position`; `texture()` into `Texture2D` with a `SamplerState` and
`Sample`; `mix`, `fract`, `mod` into `lerp`, `frac`, `fmod`; `discard`
stays. Add a trivial pair (a pass-through) for the fixed cost. Commit them
under `port/windows/probe/` with a comment at the top of each naming the
dump file it came from and the build that made it. This conversion is not
phase 4: it is four shaders by hand, to time the compiler on shaders of the
right size.

**What is measured**, for each shader (`--shaders` names the folder; the
committed folder by default), ten times: `D3DCompile` with the target
`vs_4_0` or `ps_4_0`, `D3DCOMPILE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL3`, no debug; then
`CreateVertexShader` or `CreatePixelShader` from the blob. The first
compile of the first shader on its own (it pays the DLL's load), then per
shader the minimum and median of the rest, wall and thread CPU time, and
the bytecode's size. Then the same shaders at `vs_5_0`/`ps_5_0`, for the
difference in time and size. And whether `precise` on the position output
compiles for `vs_4_0` (the `invariant gl_Position` the generators need;
`D3DCOMPILE_IEEE_STRICTNESS` is the fallback to try if not). Every blob is
written to `d3d11_probe_shaders\` beside the report, so it can be looked at
with `D3DDisassemble` later.

The numbers decide Decisions' "compiled at the draw, synchronously": a
game-sized shader at a few milliseconds is what GLSL costs today; at tens
of milliseconds phase 5 adds the compile thread.

### Step 4: buffers (`memory`)

The costs the renderer pays every frame, and the proof that a copy is
made when the call is made:

1. **The stream ring**: a `D3D11_USAGE_DYNAMIC` vertex buffer of 32 MB
   (the OpenGL build's `STREAM_BUFFER_SIZE`), written with `Map`
   `D3D11_MAP_WRITE_NO_OVERWRITE` in 64 KB pieces, 4 MB a frame, `DISCARD`
   at the wrap, 300 frames with a draw from each piece into an offscreen
   16×16 target: CPU time of the maps and copies per frame (minimum,
   median), GPU time of the frame.
2. **The mirror's segment**: a `D3D11_USAGE_DEFAULT` buffer of 4 MB
   updated with `UpdateSubresource` in 4 KB pieces (a page), 1,024 pieces a
   frame (the whole segment) and then 64 a frame (a quiet frame's stale
   pages), 300 frames each, drawn from each frame: the same timings. And
   once with a `D3D11_USAGE_STAGING` buffer plus `CopySubresourceRegion`
   for the comparison.
3. **Rewrite seen.** A buffer of three vertices written red and drawn,
   rewritten green through the same path and drawn again in the same
   frame, both draws into their own target, read back through a staging
   texture: the first must be red and the second green, for the ring's
   path and the mirror's path. This is the Vulkan plan's data self-test,
   run before the renderer exists.
4. **A texture by `UpdateSubresource`**: a 512×512 BC1 texture's level 0,
   and the same as BGRA8, 300 times: the time per upload. And a 2,048×2,048
   BGRA8 once (a high-res HUD bitmap's size).
5. **The address space** after 64 MB of buffers are alive
   (`GlobalMemoryStatusEx`), reported with step 1's figures.

### Step 5: queries (`queries`)

- 64 `D3D11_QUERY_OCCLUSION` queries, each around a draw of a known
  coverage (a square of n×n pixels into a 256×256 target), ended, and
  `GetData` with `D3D11_ASYNC_GETDATA_DONOTFLUSH` polled once a frame,
  never waited on: how many frames until each answers (minimum, median,
  maximum) and whether the count is exactly n², on the GPU and on WARP.
  Then the same with `Flush` after the end of the frame, for the
  difference. And `D3D11_QUERY_OCCLUSION_PREDICATE` for the record.
- 4,096 queries made at once (the game's slots): creation time and whether
  the device objects.

### Testing on this PC

1. Step 0 as written, release build, the numbers and pictures into
   "Progress".
2. `ninja windows_d3d11_probe`; `build\windows\d3d11_probe.exe all` (the
   Adreno, the debug layer if installed), then `--adapter warp`, then each
   again with `--sync 0` for `present`. The reports copied to
   `port/windows/probe/reports/` as
   `snapdragon_x_plus_adreno_x1_45_<adapter>_<sync>.txt`.
3. If the debug layer is missing, the user installs the Graphics Tools
   feature (Settings, Optional features, "Graphics Tools"; or an elevated
   `DISM /Online /Add-Capability /CapabilityName:Tools.Graphics.DirectX~~~~0.0.1.0`)
   and the runs are made again; both sets of reports are kept.
4. A step that crashes is left out by the guard, the rest run, and the
   crash (the running marker's name, the exit code, the console's last
   lines) goes into "Progress" as a result, not a blocker.

### Acceptance

- The reports in `port/windows/probe/reports/` for the Adreno and for
  WARP, with and without the debug layer where it could be installed.
- **A table in "Progress"**: the adapter the emulated process gets and its
  feature level; the runtime's interfaces; the options; the formats that
  matter (BC, the 16-bit colours, the depth formats, the vertex formats, the
  MSAA levels); presenting's behaviour under SDL (each transition, the
  pacing, tearing support); the compiler's times and sizes at 4_0 and 5_0,
  and `precise`; the buffers' costs and the rewrite test; the query latency;
  the address space figures.
- **The baseline**: the GL stack's line, the three scenes' frame rates and
  their screenshots (the pictures kept in `port/windows/probe/reports/` as
  PNG, converted from the game's BMP, at most a few hundred kilobytes each).
- **Proposals, written into "Decisions" marked (to confirm)** for the user:
  the shader compiler (synchronous at the draw, or a thread); the 16-bit
  formats (decoded, or native where supported); the swap chain's shape
  (buffers, scaling, tearing) and how fullscreen is handled; the feature
  level (11_0 or 11_1), from what the Adreno and WARP offer; and, if the
  emulated process does not reach the Adreno, what the plan does instead.
- `port/windows/README.md` names the probe; `port/windows/d3d11/README.md`
  exists.
- The probe's code audited against the Direct3D 11 and DXGI documentation
  for what it calls (the debug layer's run is part of that, not all of it),
  then committed on `d3d11-backend`.

### What needs the user

- Where the game's data is on this PC (`paths.data`), or the ISO for the
  Linux README's extraction route.
- The Graphics Tools feature (elevation).
- (Phase 0 also listed the first fight played by hand and a human run of
  step 2's script. The user dropped the fight for the cinematic, and had the
  agent run the script and look at the picture.)

## Phase 1 — the Direct3D executable

A second executable whose Direct3D 8 device draws nothing yet, and the
backend brought up to a device and the decision. The Vulkan plan's phase 1
(`port/android/VULKAN.md`, "Phase 1 — the Vulkan image", and its "Progress"
entries for phase 1) is the model, and the Vulkan device as it was first cut
from `d3d8_gl.c` (`git show b5038d02:port/android/guest/d3d8_vk.c`, 1,138
lines; its head says what it kept) is the reference for what such a device
keeps. Three of that phase's lessons are built into this one: the cut lost
the memory watch's start and phase 6 had to put it back; the stubs wrote no
output parameters, so every caller had to be replaced later; and the copy
drifted from `d3d8_gl.c` while the phases went on, so the copy's commit is
recorded and the drift is checked at each phase.

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far. The
source facts it relies on (line numbers in `d3d8_gl.c`, `sdl_platform.c`,
`port_config.c`, `tools/windows_build.py`) are from `main` at `d071462e`;
read the files, not the numbers, if they have moved.

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** `build/windows/halo_d3d11.exe`: the game linked with a Direct3D
  device that keeps every piece of state `d3d8_gl.c` keeps and draws none
  of it; a Direct3D 11 backend that makes the device on the adapter the
  settings name, with the debug layer when asked, and says in one log line
  what it got; the platform layer's window made without an OpenGL context;
  the shared files' GL calls counted; the two settings rows; both
  executables from `ninja windows`.
- **It is not** drawing or presenting: the window stays black. No swap
  chain, no render targets, no buffers, no shaders (phases 2 to 6). No
  `display.renderer` setting and no merging of the executables (phase 8).
- **`halo.exe` does not change in what it does.** Its objects are the same
  but for `port_config.o` (the two rows), `sdl_platform.o` (the mode it
  does not take) and `win32_crash.o` (the crash line's name, in "Files"
  below; the audit added it to this list); the acceptance checks that by
  comparing the objects.
- **Nothing is copied that phase 1 does not need.** `xbox_textures.c`,
  `nv2a_vsh.c`, `nv2a_psh.c`, `d3d8_resources.c` and the shared files stay
  as they are in both executables; the Direct3D executable only leaves
  `d3d8_gl.c`, `xgpu_post.c` and `gl_functions.c` out. Their copies come in
  the phase that needs them (`xbox_textures_d3d11.c` in phase 6, the
  generators in phase 4, `xgpu_post_d3d11.c` in phase 6).

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d8_d3d11.c` | the device: `d3d8_gl.c`'s desktop branches with every GL call taken out (below) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11.c` | the backend: the factory, the adapter, the device, the debug layer's messages, the log line (below) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d11_backend.h` | the boundary between the two: `<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only (below) |
| `tools/windows_gl_stubs.py` | writes `build/windows/gl_stubs.c`: a counted stub for every function of `gl.h`'s desktop list (below) |
| `tools/windows_build.py` | the Direct3D units, the stubs' rule, the second link, `halo_d3d11.exe` in `windows` |
| `port/linux/src/sdl_platform.c`, `sdl_platform.h` | the no-context mode, behind `_WIN32` (below) |
| `port/linux/src/port_config.c` | `display.d3d11_adapter`, `debug.d3d11_debug`, both `_platform_windows` |
| `port/windows/src/win32_crash.c`, `tools/symbolize_crash.py` | the crash line names the executable it runs as, and the script accepts either name |
| `tools/ci_build.py` | `halo_d3d11.exe` and `halo_d3d11.pdb` in the Windows outputs |
| `port/windows/README.md`, `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` | the executable (marked in development), the two settings, the units |
| `tools/windows_build.py` (`windows_configure_inputs`) | `port/windows/d3d11` among the folders whose change re-runs configure |

### The device: `d3d8_d3d11.c`

A copy of `d3d8_gl.c` (4,897 lines, 239 GL calls, 61 conditionals all on
`HALO_ANDROID`) made in this order, so the audit can follow it:

1. **Copy the file whole** and write its header: what it is, the commit of
   `d3d8_gl.c` it was copied from (`git log -1 --format=%h --
   port/linux/src/d3d8_gl.c`), and that changes to `d3d8_gl.c` are not
   followed automatically. Later phases copy decisions from `d3d8_gl.c` as
   it is then, and say in "Progress" if the copy already holds an older one
   (the Vulkan plan's rule).
2. **Take the desktop side of every `HALO_ANDROID` conditional** (the
   `#else` and `#ifndef` sides; the desktop branch has no macro of its own)
   and drop the Android side, the ES shims (`d3d8_gl.c` 56–74) and
   `xgpu_capabilities`, which only Android declares.
3. **Take out every GL call** (`\bgl[A-Z]\w+\(` finds them; the audit greps
   for zero) and what exists only to make them. What goes:
   the GL state cache, the program and fragment caches and `program_*`,
   the samplers, the render targets' GL side (`render_target_*`, the
   framebuffer cache, resolve, the mip composite), multisampling, the
   anti-aliasing passes and `xgpu_post_*` calls, the mirror's GL buffers
   and the stream and index rings' GL side, `write_screenshot`, the query
   buffers, `gl_initialize`'s GL queries of limits, the pointer's GL draw,
   and the tracing that prints GL names. **What stays, as it is**: the
   screen's width and scale and everything `halo_screen_*` exports; the
   `D3D__*` arrays and every `SetRenderState_*`/`SetTextureState_*`; the
   transforms, the viewport and its reserved constants; the vertex shaders
   and their declarations (`struct vertex_element`), `CreateVertexShader`
   through `SetVertexShaderConstant`; `SetStreamSource`, `SetIndices`;
   immediate mode's accumulation (`Begin`, `SetVertexData*`, `End`); the
   surfaces and render targets *as the game sees them* (`SetRenderTarget`,
   `GetBackBuffer`, `GetDepthStencilSurface`, the back and depth buffers
   from `d3d8_surface_initialize`); `GetDeviceCaps`'s constant answer; the
   vertical blank thread, `flip_count`, `pending_flips`, `d3d_find_flipcount`,
   `SetVerticalBlankCallback`, `BlockUntilVerticalBlank`; `InsertCallback`;
   the debugging settings; the statistics; and every function another
   object calls (the table in "The link is the check", below).
4. **What drew, cleared or uploaded keeps its state and returns**:
   `DrawVertices`, `DrawIndexedVertices` and `End`'s draw count the draw
   in `stats` and return; `Clear` counts; `SetTexture`, `SetPalette`,
   `SetPixelShaderProgram` keep the pointer or the program; `LoadVertexShader`
   and `SelectVertexShader` keep what they keep now; `SetRenderTarget` keeps
   the surfaces. Nothing is uploaded, so `xgpu_texture_get` and
   `xgpu_texture_cache_begin_frame` are not called (phase 6's work).
5. **The answers the game reads back** are the OpenGL build's where they do
   not depend on the GPU, which is all of them but one: `IsBusy` is `FALSE`
   as now, `KickPushBuffer` is empty, `InsertCallback` calls at once,
   `GetDeviceCaps` answers the same constants, `GetBackBuffer` returns the
   same surface, `PersistDisplay` returns `S_OK`, the locks are
   `d3d8_resources.c`'s and return pointers into the game's memory as now.
   The one that depends on the GPU, `GetVisibilityTestResult`, returns 0
   samples and `S_OK` with `time_stamp` 0 (never `D3DERR_TESTINCOMPLETE`),
   until phase 6 step 6 counts: lens flares stay dark (the Vulkan plan's
   phase 1 saw a lens-flare warning in the log that its GL build logs
   too; if this build logs one, "Progress" says so).
6. **Device creation** (`Direct3D_CreateDevice`, `d3d8_gl.c` 1734–1812) keeps
   its initialisation of the device's state, then, where it calls
   `platform_video_initialize` and `gl_initialize`:
   - `platform_video_without_gl()` (the platform layer's new call, below),
     then `platform_video_initialize(width, height)` as now; `debug.null_renderer`
     still skips the window, with the same log line.
   - `d3d11_backend_create(adapter, debug, line, size)` with
     `display.d3d11_adapter`'s string and `debug.d3d11_debug`'s boolean; the
     line it returns is logged with `platform_log`, whatever it says. The
     device's `backend_ready` is its result: with 0 the game runs with a
     window and no picture, as `debug.null_renderer` does today, and the
     player's way back is `halo.exe`.
   - `memory_watch_initialize()`, where `gl_initialize` calls it (1601):
     **this is the call the Vulkan cut lost.** Nothing uses the watch in
     phase 1, but phase 3's mirror and phase 6's textures expect it running
     from the start, and the Windows one (`win32_memory_watch.c`) is the
     same object in both executables.
   - the debugging settings read as `gl_initialize` reads them (1602–1605).
7. **`Present`** (`d3d8_gl.c` 4771–4894) keeps: the frame count, the
   statistics line (its form in this phase, below), `platform_pump_events`,
   and the vertical blank block as it is (two frames queued without
   interpolation, `flip_count` with it). It calls `platform_video_swap()`
   where the OpenGL one does, after the blit it no longer has: in the
   no-context mode that call paces the frame (below) and presents nothing.
   `debug.screenshot_every` is read and, if set, logged once as not
   available until phase 2.
8. **The exports the shared files call** stay defined:
   `xgpu_gl_state_invalidate` as an empty function (`xbox_textures.c`,
   `hud_hires.c` and `text_hires.c` call it); `halo_ui_pointer_update` and
   `halo_scoreboard_pointer_update` as the desktop versions with their GL
   draw taken out; `d3d8_renderer_description` answering `"Direct3D 11"`
   where it answers `"OpenGL"` now (`menu_functions.c` prints it). `xgpu_compile_shader`, `xgpu_link_program` and
   `xgpu_render_target_find` are not defined: their only callers are
   `xgpu_post.c`, which the Direct3D executable leaves out, and the Android
   and Switch hosts.

**The link is the check**, as the Vulkan plan put it: every symbol
`d3d8_gl.o` defines that another object uses must be defined by
`d3d8_d3d11.o`, and none may be a stub that changes what the game sees. The
list, from the source: the four `D3D__*` arrays (`progress_bar.c` reads
`D3D__RenderState` directly; everything else goes through `xdk_d3d8.h`'s
inlines); the 49 `D3DDevice_*` entry points; `Direct3DCreate8`,
`Direct3D_SetPushBufferSize`, `Direct3D_CreateDevice`; `halo_screen_width`,
`halo_screen_pixel_scale`, `halo_shadow_map_scale`, `halo_screen_ui_offset`,
`halo_screen_commit`, `halo_screen_anti_alias`; `halo_ui_pointer_update`,
`halo_scoreboard_pointer_update`; `halo_vertex_shader_lighting`;
`d3d8_renderer_description`; `d3d_find_flipcount`; `xgpu_gl_state_invalidate`.
The audit runs `llvm-nm -g --defined-only` on both objects and puts the
difference in "Progress": it must be exactly `xgpu_compile_shader`,
`xgpu_link_program`, `xgpu_render_target_find` (and `xgpu_capabilities` if
the desktop object has it, which it should not).

The statistics line (`debug.gpu_stats`), every 60 frames from `Present` as
now: `frame <n>: Direct3D 11 draws nothing yet; <d> draws, <i> immediate,
<c> clears asked for; <k> GL stub calls` with the draw counts the device
counted and the stub count from `gl_stubs.c`. The same line names the
backend's debug-layer message count when the layer is on. Phase 2 extends
it.

### The backend: `win32_d3d11.c` and `d3d11_backend.h`

The first Windows-facing unit of the renderer, compiled as `win32_*.c` is
(`WIN32_FLAGS`, no prefix header, no Xbox SDK; `COBJMACROS`;
`d3d11_4.h`, `d3d11sdklayers.h`, `dxgi1_6.h`; phase 0's build finding:
nothing else is needed, and `INTERFACE` must not be defined by the unit).
It copies from the probe, and says so in its header, the adapter choice
(`choose_adapter`), the device creation with the debug layer's fallback
(`create_device`), `hr_text` and `drain_debug`: the pieces phase 0 proved.
What it does not copy: the probe's report, guard, timing and steps.

`d3d11_backend.h` includes `<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only, so
`d3d8_d3d11.c` (Xbox SDK) and `win32_d3d11.c` (Windows SDK) both include it.
Its functions take and return fixed-width integers, `int`, `const char *`
and `void *`; nothing of either SDK's types crosses, as `update.h` and
`posix.h` already do between the two worlds. In this phase:

- `int d3d11_backend_create(const char *adapter, int debug_layer, char
  *line, size_t line_size)`: 1 with a device, 0 without; `line` filled
  either way (the one log line, below). `adapter` is `display.d3d11_adapter`:
  empty for the first hardware adapter `IDXGIFactory6::EnumAdapterByGpuPreference`
  lists for high performance that is not software (the first adapter at all
  without that interface); `"warp"` for `D3D_DRIVER_TYPE_WARP`; anything
  else, the first adapter whose description contains it, case-insensitively,
  and **if none does, the first hardware adapter, and the line says the
  name matched nothing** (the probe failed instead; the game has a window
  to keep). `D3D11CreateDevice` asks for 11_1 then 11_0, and no lower:
  **11_0 is required** (Decisions), so a machine below it gets no device
  and the line says what level it offered (ask once more with 10_1 and
  10_0 only to name it, then release it). Flags:
  `D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_BGRA_SUPPORT` always (the swap chain's format in
  phase 2), `D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_DEBUG` when `debug_layer` is set, falling
  back without it on `DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING` and saying so in
  the line. Kept in the unit's static state: the factory, the adapter, the
  device (`ID3D11Device`), the immediate context, the feature level, the
  `ID3D11InfoQueue` when the layer is on (its storage limit raised as the
  probe's), the adapter's description and driver version
  (`CheckInterfaceSupport` with `IID_IDXGIDevice`, as the probe reads it).
  Nothing is made that phase 2 would make again.
- `uint32_t d3d11_backend_drain_debug(void)`: every stored message of the
  info queue to the log as `d3d11 debug: <severity> <id> <text>`, cleared,
  the count returned; 0 at once without the layer. The device calls it from
  `Present` every frame (cheap with nothing stored), so a message is seen
  in the frame it was made.
- `void d3d11_backend_destroy(void)`: with the layer,
  `ReportLiveDeviceObjects(D3D11_RLDO_SUMMARY)` and the count of its
  messages to the log (phase 0 saw 9 for the device's own objects); then
  every object released in reverse order. Called from `D3DDevice_Release`
  if the game reaches it, and from an `atexit` handler registered at
  creation otherwise, so the layer's report is seen on a normal exit.
- The log: the backend declares `void platform_log(const char *, ...)` by
  hand, as `win32_memory_watch.c` does, for `drain_debug` and `destroy`;
  the creation line is returned, not logged, so the device logs one line
  in one place (the Vulkan plan's deviation 2).

**The one log line**, either way:

```
renderer: Direct3D 11 on Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU, feature level 11_1, driver 31.0.137.0, debug layer off
renderer: Direct3D 11 on Microsoft Basic Render Driver (WARP), feature level 11_1, driver <version>, debug layer on
renderer: Direct3D 11 on <adapter> ... debug layer missing (DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING; install the Graphics Tools feature)
renderer: Direct3D 11 on <adapter> ... ("<name>" matched no adapter)
renderer: Direct3D 11 unavailable: no adapter (<hresult>)
renderer: Direct3D 11 unavailable: D3D11CreateDevice failed (<hresult> <name>)
renderer: Direct3D 11 unavailable: feature level 10_1 is below 11_0
```

The platform layer's own line, `OpenGL <version> on <renderer>`, is not
printed in the no-context mode (there is no string to print); it prints
`window: <w>x<h> pixels, no OpenGL context (the renderer presents)` in its
place, so a log always says which path the window took.

### The platform layer: the no-context mode

The one change to shared code that is not an accessor or a settings row,
in `sdl_platform.c` behind `_WIN32`, taken only when the device asks:

- `void platform_video_without_gl(void)` (declared in `sdl_platform.h`
  under `_WIN32`): sets a static flag; must be called before
  `platform_video_initialize`. The Direct3D device calls it in
  `Direct3D_CreateDevice`; nothing else does, so `halo.exe` never sets it
  (it does not link `d3d8_d3d11.o`, and the OpenGL device does not know the
  call).
- `platform_video_initialize` (753–841) with the flag: no `SDL_GL_SetAttribute`
  (762–775); the window from `SDL_CreateWindow` with the same flags **less
  `SDL_WINDOW_OPENGL`** (801–804; `RESIZABLE`, `HIGH_PIXEL_DENSITY`,
  `HIDDEN` and `FULLSCREEN` as now); the fullscreen kind applied as now
  (811–814); no `SDL_GL_CreateContext`, `SDL_GL_MakeCurrent`,
  `gl_functions_load` or `SDL_GL_SetSwapInterval` (815–833); the event
  thread, the log line above in place of the `glGetString` one (836), and
  the mouse capture as now. Returns TRUE with a window.
- `platform_display_apply` (848–871) with the flag: no
  `SDL_GL_SetSwapInterval` (870).
- `platform_video_swap` (910–931) with the flag: no `SDL_GL_SwapWindow`
  (917); the frame limiter as now, except that **with `display.vsync` on
  the interval is the display's refresh** (`SDL_GetCurrentDisplayMode` of
  the window's display, read when `config_changes()` moves as
  `frame_interval_ns` reads the settings; 60 Hz if unknown), because
  there is no swap to wait on. This stands in for vsync until phase 2's
  present takes the sync interval; phase 2 keeps it for the frames nothing presents (a minimised window,
  no swap chain). With vsync off,
  `display.max_fps` as now. (Phase 0's baseline ran with vsync off and no
  limit; the same settings give this phase's frame count.)
- `void *platform_video_native_window(void)` (under `_WIN32`): the `HWND`
  from `SDL_GetPointerProperty(SDL_GetWindowProperties(window),
  SDL_PROP_WINDOW_WIN32_HWND_POINTER, NULL)`, or NULL without a window.
  Added now, used by phase 2's swap chain, so the shared file is touched
  once. (`sdl_platform.c` is Xbox-facing; SDL's property is a string
  constant and needs no Windows SDK.)
- Everything else as it is: `platform_screen_mode` before the window,
  F11, the display modes, the events, the pointer, `platform_video_drawable_size`
  (phase 2's present polls it each frame as `Present` does now; the
  platform layer handles no resize event today and gains none here).

The Linux and Android builds compile the same file with the flag's code
behind `_WIN32`, and `halo.exe` compiles it with the flag never set.

### The GL stubs

The shared files the Direct3D executable links still call GL:
`hud_hires.c` (7 calls in `hud_hires_png_texture`), `text_hires.c` (8 in
`text_hires_atlas_texture`), `menu_files.c` (2 `glDeleteTextures`),
`xbox_textures.c` (15, in the texture cache the device does not call in
this phase) and `sdl_platform.c` (1, not reached in the no-context mode).
They go through `gl.h`'s `halo_gl*` pointers, which
`gl_functions.c` defines and loads; without a context they are null, so a
call is a crash. The Direct3D executable leaves `gl_functions.o` out and
links `gl_stubs.o` in its place, generated because every stub needs the
function's real signature: GL's entry points are `APIENTRY` (`__stdcall`
on x86), where the callee pops the arguments, so a stub of the wrong
signature corrupts the stack.

- **`tools/windows_gl_stubs.py gl.h SDL_opengl.h SDL_opengl_glext.h
  output.c`**, modelled on `tools/android_gl_stubs.py`: the function list is
  the desktop `GL_FUNCTIONS(X)` of `gl.h` (the one after the
  `ANDROID_GL_FUNCTIONS_END` marker; 114 names), the prototypes from SDL's
  headers (`GLAPI <return> APIENTRY <name> (<parameters>);` in
  `SDL_opengl_glext.h`, and `GLAPIENTRY` in `SDL_opengl.h` for the 1.1
  functions such as `glGetString`); a name with no prototype fails the
  generation, loudly. For each: `static <return>
  APIENTRY stub_<name>(<parameters>) { gl_stub_called(<index>,
  __builtin_return_address(0)); return 0; }` (nothing for `void`, NULL for
  pointers), and `__typeof__(&<name>) halo_<name> = stub_<name>;`, the
  definition `gl.h` declares `extern` (compiled with `GL_FUNCTIONS_DEFINE`
  as `gl_functions.c` is). **The stubs write no output parameter**: a
  `glGenTextures` leaves the name 0, a `glGetIntegerv` leaves the value
  as it was. Phase 6 replaces each caller through the accessors the files
  already have (`hud_hires_png_pixels`, `text_hires_atlas_rows`,
  `menu_art_*`), as the Vulkan plan did.
- **The count**: `gl_stub_called` keeps a count per function and the first
  caller's return address; `unsigned long gl_stubs_calls(void)` returns the
  total for the statistics line, and the first call of each function logs
  once `gl stub: <name> called from <address>`, symbolised by hand from
  `halo_d3d11.pdb` (`llvm-symbolizer --obj=build/windows/halo_d3d11.exe
  <address>`). The Vulkan plan counted 0 of 102 in the menus, because the
  shared files' GL calls are reached only through `d3d8_gl.c`; here the
  expectation is the same, and `menu_files.c`'s `glDeleteTextures` on a
  replaced menu art is the likely exception. "Progress" lists what was
  counted, so phase 6 knows.
- A self-test at creation, as the Vulkan plan's: the device calls one stub
  (`glFlush`, through the pointer) and checks the count moved, logging
  `gl stubs: self-test ok` or failing creation loudly.
- The generated file compiles as `gl_functions.c` does (`platform_cflags`:
  it includes `gl.h`, and through it SDL's GL headers), with the generator
  and `gl.h` as the rule's implicit inputs.

### The build

`tools/windows_build.py`, after `emit()` (331–462) has made `halo.exe`'s
objects and link:

- `emit()` remembers the objects of `d3d8_gl.c`, `xgpu_post.c` and
  `gl_functions.c` as it adds the platform layer (409–416), and returns its
  `objects` list, as `tools/android_build.py` does at 517–531 and 613–627.
- The Direct3D units: every `port/windows/d3d11/*.c` except
  `win32_d3d11_probe.c` (the probe keeps its own target), classified as
  `port/windows/src` is (418–425): `win32_*` with `win32_cflags`, the rest
  with `platform_cflags`. `gl_stubs.c` from a `windows_gl_stubs` rule into
  `build/windows/`, compiled with `platform_cflags`.
- The second link: `halo.exe`'s objects less the three remembered, plus the
  Direct3D units and the stubs, through the same `windows_link` rule with
  the same `base_ldflags` (subsystem, entry, large-address-aware, stack)
  and `libs` plus `-ld3d11 -ldxgi -ldxguid`, into `build/windows/halo_d3d11.exe`
  (lld writes `halo_d3d11.pdb` beside it from `-g`). Under `--lto=full`
  this is a second full LTO link, which about doubles the link time; the
  build's time goes into "Progress". With `--pgo=train` the Direct3D
  executable is not built (the training runs `halo.exe`); with `--pgo=use`
  it links the profile-used objects as they are, and its own units have no
  profile, which is harmless.
- `windows` depends on both executables. `tools/ci_build.py`'s Windows
  outputs and symbols lists gain `halo_d3d11.exe` and `halo_d3d11.pdb`
  (the CI's Windows job is off in this fork, so this changes what a local
  `ci_build.py` packages and nothing that runs). `windows_configure_inputs`
  (`tools/windows_build.py` 144) gains `port/windows/d3d11`, so a new unit
  there re-runs configure.
- The crash reporter (`win32_crash.c` 826) writes `crash: halo.exe at %p`
  and `tools/symbolize_crash.py` looks for `halo.exe at`: the line takes
  the running executable's own base name (`GetModuleFileNameW`, which the
  reporter already uses to relaunch itself), and the script matches
  `.exe at`. The updater's clean-up list (`updater.c` 528) and the rest of
  the two-executable surface are phase 8's.
- Configure with `--portable` on this PC, as phase 0 did; `--release` for
  the runs measured.

### The settings

Two rows in `port_config.c`'s table, `_platform_windows` (the Windows
build's config file lists them; `halo.exe` ignores them; Linux and Android
never see them):

| Setting | Type, default | Environment | Comment |
|---|---|---|---|
| `display.d3d11_adapter` | string, `""` | `HALO_D3D11_ADAPTER` | Direct3D 11 executable: empty for the fastest GPU, "warp" for Microsoft's software renderer, or part of an adapter's name. |
| `debug.d3d11_debug` | boolean, `false` | `HALO_D3D11_DEBUG` | Direct3D 11 executable: the Direct3D debug layer's messages in the log (needs the Graphics Tools feature of Windows). |

No menu row (`tools/port_settings.py` has no Windows platform value; phase
8). `debug.gpu_stats`, `debug.null_renderer`, `debug.telnet_console`,
`debug.test_input`, `debug.screenshot_*` and `display.vsync`/`max_fps` keep
their meaning in the Direct3D executable, as said above.

### Testing on this PC

1. `python configure.py --portable --release`, `ninja windows`: both
   executables. Then the object check: with `main`'s `halo.exe` objects
   built the same way beside them (a second build directory from a
   worktree of `main`), every object of `halo.exe` is byte-identical but
   `port_config.o` and `sdl_platform.o`; an object that differs for another
   reason is explained in "Progress" or fixed. The link's symbol check
   (`llvm-nm`, above) and the GL grep on `d3d8_d3d11.c`.
2. `halo.exe` as before: the main menu and the a10 opening, the same log
   lines as phase 0's baseline; the two new rows in a fresh `config.toml`.
3. `halo_d3d11.exe` on the Adreno, with phase 0's settings (`debug.gpu_stats`,
   `debug.telnet_console`, `display.vsync = false`, `display.max_fps = -1`):
   the log line; the window black and its title bar there; the menus
   driven from the script console and `debug.test_input` as phase 0 drove
   them (the PC menus and, with `display.menus = "xbox"`, the Xbox-style
   ones: the menus' art goes through `menu_files.c`, the likely stub
   caller); `map_name a10` through the cinematic into the cryo room, with
   sound (the log's audio device line and no audio error; the agent cannot
   hear it) and keyboard and mouse input (`look:1` turns, seen in nothing
   but the absence of errors; the console's `camera_control` or the HUD's
   state in the log if there is one); the statistics line every 60 frames
   with its stub count; the frame rate from the lines' timestamps, as the
   baseline measured it, to see what the game costs with nothing drawn
   (deko3d's phase 1 found 0.5 ms a frame); exit through the menu and
   through the window's close button, with no crash and the backend's
   destroy line in the log.
4. The same on WARP (`display.d3d11_adapter = "warp"`).
5. Both again with `debug.d3d11_debug = true`: the line says the layer is
   on; no `d3d11 debug:` line in the menus or the map; at exit the
   live-objects summary names the device's own objects only.
6. The failure paths: `display.d3d11_adapter = "nonesuch"` (the first
   hardware adapter, and the line says the name matched nothing);
   `debug.null_renderer = true` (no window, the same line as `halo.exe`'s,
   no device made); `debug.d3d11_debug = true` on a machine without the
   layer cannot be tried here any more (the feature is installed): the
   fallback's code is read in the audit, as is `unavailable:`, which no
   setting can force on this PC.
7. `display.vsync = true` with `display.max_fps = 0`: the frame count over
   10 s at the display's refresh (120 Hz here), from the statistics line's
   cadence, the stand-in for vsync working.
8. Window transitions are not this phase's (nothing presents): F11 and a
   resize must not crash, and that is all.

### Acceptance

- `ninja windows` makes `halo.exe` and `halo_d3d11.exe`; the object check
  passes with the two exceptions; the symbol difference between
  `d3d8_d3d11.o` and `d3d8_gl.o` is the three named; `d3d8_d3d11.c` has no
  GL call.
- `halo_d3d11.exe` runs the menus and a10's opening through the cinematic
  into the cryo room, with sound and input and a black window, on the
  Adreno and on WARP, with and without the debug layer, and exits clean,
  with the log lines in "Progress".
- The GL stub calls seen, by function and caller, in "Progress" (the
  Vulkan plan's was 0; a number here is a finding, not a failure).
- The frame rate with nothing drawn, on this PC, in "Progress", beside
  phase 0's OpenGL numbers for the same scenes.
- The settings and the executable in the Windows README, marked in
  development; `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` says what the folder holds
  now.
- The code audited against the source (`d3d8_gl.c` function by function:
  the Vulkan audit verified sixty functions byte-for-byte and the rest
  differing only by GL) and against the Direct3D 11 and DXGI
  documentation for what the backend calls, then committed on
  `d3d11-backend`.

### What needs the user

Nothing is known to. The agent can see the window by screen capture as it
did in phase 0, and drives the game through the console. If the two
executables' build time under full LTO is unreasonable, that is a question
for the user (a `--lto=thin` default for the Direct3D one, or none in
debug builds), not a decision to take alone.

## Phase 2 — the backend's skeleton

The first picture: the game's clears, through Direct3D 11, on the screen.
The Vulkan plan's phase 2 (`port/android/VULKAN.md`, "Phase 2 — the
backend's skeleton", and its "Progress" entries for phase 2: the audit, the
summary, the deviations) is the model, less its command stream: here the
device calls the backend directly, and the backend records nothing for
later. Phase 0's `present` step (`win32_d3d11_probe.c`, `step_present`) is
the swap chain's proof on this PC and the code the backend copies for it.
Three of the Vulkan phase's lessons are built into this one: a clear of some
channels only cannot be a clear call and is drawn (its deviation 10 added a
self-test for it, which is in this phase from the start); the marker is
drawn, not cleared, and sits on the picture after the blit (its deviation
2); and "target changes" counts a pair that differs from the last, not each
rebinding (its deviation 11).

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far. The
source facts it relies on (line numbers in `d3d8_gl.c`, `d3d8_d3d11.c`,
`win32_d3d11.c`, `sdl_platform.c`, `win32_d3d11_probe.c`) are from
`d3d11-backend` at `d943c612`; `d3d8_gl.c` is still at `2765d816`, the
commit the device was copied from. Read the files, not the numbers, if they
have moved.

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** the swap chain on the game's window; the game's render targets
  as Direct3D 11 textures, found by the surface they stand for; the game's
  clears, through clear calls where they can be and drawn where they cannot;
  the back buffer presented by a letterboxed draw into the swap chain's
  buffer, with `display.vsync` as the sync interval; the window's resizes
  and modes followed; the diagnostics every later phase relies on (the
  statistics, the debug layer's messages in the frame they are made, a lost
  device as a log line, screenshots, the marker, a clears' self-test).
- **It is not** drawing: no vertex data, no shaders of the game, no
  textures, no multisampling, no anti-aliasing passes (phases 3 to 6). The
  menus show as their clears, plain colours, black for most of them; a map
  shows its sky's clear and its fog's. Draws and immediate-mode draws are
  still counted and dropped, as phase 1 left them.
- **`halo.exe` does not change in what it does**, and this time neither do
  its objects: the two settings rows are the only change under
  `port/linux/src` (the no-context mode gains nothing), so the object check
  of phase 1 holds with `port_config.o` as the one difference (and the
  harmless checksum and time differences phase 1's "Progress" lists).
- **Nothing is copied that this phase does not need.** `xbox_textures.c`,
  `nv2a_vsh.c`, `nv2a_psh.c` and `xgpu_post.c` stay out, as phase 1 left
  them.

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d8_d3d11.c` | the render target table (from `d3d8_gl.c`, its GL replaced by backend handles), `SetRenderTarget` and `Clear` with `d3d8_gl.c`'s arithmetic, `Present` presenting, the screenshot writer, the statistics (below) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11.c` | the targets, the clears, the built-in shaders, the compiler's loading, the self-test, the read-back (below) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_present.c` | the swap chain, the resize, the present, the marker: the first split of the backend, as "Files" allows; it shares the device's state through a header of the backend's own, `win32_d3d11.h` (Windows SDK's types; the Windows-facing units only) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d11_backend.h` | the functions below added; still `<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only |
| `port/linux/src/port_config.c` | `debug.d3d11_present_marker`, `debug.d3d11_self_test`, both `_platform_windows`, behind `_WIN32` as phase 1's rows are |
| `tools/windows_build.py` | nothing: every `port/windows/d3d11/*.c` but the probe is already compiled and linked (`win32_*` against the Windows SDK) |
| `port/windows/README.md`, `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` | the two settings; what the folder holds now; the executable still marked in development |

### Step 0: phase 1's audit, applied

Before the phase's own work, in a commit of its own, the small things the
audit of phase 1 found ("Progress", "Phase 1 — audit"):

- `win32_d3d11.c` `create_device` (295): the second attempt at 10_1 and
  10_0 runs on `DXGI_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED` only, not on `E_INVALIDARG` as well,
  since `E_INVALIDARG` is also what a runtime without Direct3D 11.1 answers
  to a list naming 11_1 (Windows 7 without the platform update), and then an
  11_0 adapter would be reported as below 11_0. On `E_INVALIDARG` the device
  is asked for once more at 11_0 alone before the lower levels are tried.
