- `win32_d3d11.c` `choose_adapter` (262): the enumeration by name ends on
  any failure of `EnumAdapters1`, not only on `DXGI_ERROR_NOT_FOUND`.
- `port/windows/README.md`: the `renderer:` line is not the log's first
  line (the window's comes before it); say "the log has a `renderer:` line".

The build time in phase 1's summary (50 s from clean) is left as it is, with
the audit's note beside it: it is not a number this phase compares against.

### The device: targets, clears and present (`d3d8_d3d11.c`)

What phase 1 cut out of `d3d8_gl.c`'s render targets comes back, with the
GL replaced by the backend's calls, in this order so the audit can follow:

1. **The render target table** (`d3d8_gl.c` 423–460 and 1123–1244:
   `struct render_target_entry`, the buckets, `surface_dimensions`,
   `surface_is_shadow_map`, the recent-targets cache, `render_target_remember`,
   `render_target_get`) copied as it is, with `struct xgpu_render_target`'s
   GL members unused and one member added, the backend's handle (`uint32_t
   backend_target`, 0 while none). The key is `d3d8_gl.c`'s: the surface's
   `Data`, its width and height in the game's units, whether it is a depth
   surface, and its scale (the screen's for the screen's targets, the shadow
   maps' for those, 1 otherwise); its pixel size is `gl_width` and
   `gl_height`, computed as now (`width * scale + 0.5`). Where
   `render_target_get` made the GL texture (1231–1239) it calls
   `d3d11_backend_target_create(gl_width, gl_height, depth)` and keeps the
   handle; a handle of 0 (the backend could not make it) leaves the entry
   with none, logged once per entry, and every use of that entry is skipped.
   The entries are never freed, as now; the statistics say how many exist.
   `recent_render_targets` is forgotten where `halo_screen_commit` forgets it
   (phase 1 left that line out: it comes back). `xgpu_render_target_find`
   stays undefined (its callers are `xgpu_post.c` and the hosts).
2. **`bind_targets`** (1432–1479) copied without multisampling (the
   `samples`, `render_target_multisample` and `renderbuffers` lines; phase 6
   brings them): the colour and depth entries looked up, `last_rendered`
   and `written` kept, `target_scale` set from the colour target's scale
   (or the depth's), and `d3d11_backend_targets_set(color_handle,
   depth_handle)` where it bound the framebuffer. A depth entry that is not
   a depth surface is dropped as now. Called from `Clear` and, from phase 6,
   from every draw; `SetRenderTarget` itself stays as phase 1 has it (it
   keeps the surfaces and resets the viewport; the binding happens at the
   clear or the draw, as in `d3d8_gl.c`).
3. **`Clear`** (4614–4689) copied with its arithmetic whole and its GL
   lines replaced: `bind_targets` first (nothing to clear into: return, as
   now); the trace line and the count as phase 1 has them; then the
   rectangles **in the targets' pixels, as `d3d8_gl.c` works them out**: a
   clear without rectangles is the viewport's rectangle (`target_pixel` of
   its corners, `scaled_pixel` copied with it: `floor(coordinate * scale +
   0.5)`); rectangles are clipped to the viewport, moved right by
   `UI_OFFSET`, and empty ones skipped. One call,
   `d3d11_backend_clear(channels, color[4], z, stencil, rectangles, count)`,
   with `channels` the flags' `D3DCLEAR_TARGET_R/G/B/A` bits and the depth
   and stencil bits (`D3DCLEAR_ZBUFFER`, `D3DCLEAR_STENCIL`, dropped when
   there is no depth target, as `has_depth` drops them now), the colour from
   `color_to_vec4`, and the rectangles as `{x, y, width, height}` in pixels
   with row 0 at the top. `d3d8_gl.c`'s GL rectangles were the other way up
   (`glScissor` counts from the bottom); Direct3D's are not, so **no flip**:
   `y` is `target_pixel(top, 1)`, as the Vulkan plan did. The device does not
   know or care whether the backend clears or draws; the backend's answer
   (how it cleared) goes into the statistics (below).
4. **`Present`** (phase 1's, `d3d8_d3d11.c` 1498): where `d3d8_gl.c`'s
   resolved and blitted the back buffer (the block from `render_target_resolve` to `glBlitFramebuffer`, after 4771), the device looks the
   back buffer up (`render_target_get(&device.back_buffer)`, its entry made
   now if the game cleared nothing into it yet, so a frame with no clear
   still presents black) and calls `d3d11_backend_present(handle,
   window_pixel_width, window_pixel_height, sync_interval)` with the
   window's size from `platform_video_drawable_size` (the resize is found
   there, below) and `display.vsync` as 1 or 0, read as `frame_interval_ns`
   reads it (when `config_changes()` moves). The result says whether a
   picture was presented (1), nothing was (0: the window is minimised or
   occluded, or there is no swap chain), or the device is lost (-1, below).
   Then the pacing: **`platform_video_swap()` is called when the present
   did not pace the frame**, that is when the result is not 1, or when
   `display.vsync` is off (the frame limiter of `display.max_fps` as now);
   with vsync on and a presented frame, the sync interval paced it and
   `platform_video_swap` is not called (the stand-in refresh-rate interval
   phase 1 put in `sdl_platform.c` stays, and is what paces a frame nothing
   presents, so a minimised game does not spin). The frame count, the
   statistics, `platform_pump_events` and the vertical blank block stay as
   they are. `debug.screenshot_every`'s line of phase 1 goes; the screenshot
   is below.
5. **The device lost.** A result of -1 from `present`, or 0 from
   `target_create` after the backend reported the device lost, sets
   `backend_ready` false and logs once `Direct3D 11: the device was lost
   (<reason>); the game goes on without a picture`, with the reason the
   backend logged from `GetDeviceRemovedReason`. From then on `Clear` and
   `Present` keep their counts and call the backend no more (the
   `backend_ready` checks phase 1 put at the visibility tests are the
   pattern); `platform_video_swap` paces every frame. Not a crash, and not
   a new device: the player's way back is `halo.exe`.
6. **Screenshots** (`debug.screenshot_every`, `debug.screenshot_directory`):
   `write_screenshot` (4722–4769) copied, its `glReadPixels` replaced by
   `d3d11_backend_target_read(handle, pixels, size)`, which fills BGRA rows
   from the top; alpha forced to 0xff and the BMP written as now, at the
   target's pixel size, `frame%05lu.bmp`. Called from `Present` before the
   present, as now.
7. **`ui_point_from_window_on`** goes back to `d3d8_gl.c`'s form (the entry
   from `render_target_get`, its `gl_width` and `gl_height`); phase 1's
   `back_buffer_pixels` goes.
8. **The clears' self-test** (`debug.d3d11_self_test`): once, at creation
   after the backend's line, `d3d11_backend_self_test(line, size)`, its
   line logged whatever it says (below). Phase 3 adds its paths to the same
   setting.

**The link is the check**, as phase 1 had it: `llvm-nm -g --defined-only`
on `d3d8_d3d11.o` against `d3d8_gl.o` still differs by exactly
`xgpu_compile_shader`, `xgpu_link_program`, `xgpu_render_target_find`
(everything this phase adds to the device is static), and
`grep -E '\bgl[A-Z]\w+\('` on `d3d8_d3d11.c` is still 0.

**The statistics line** (`debug.gpu_stats`, every 60 frames) becomes:
`frame <n>: Direct3D 11 clears only; <d> draws, <i> immediate dropped; <c>
clears (<f> by clear calls, <p> drawn), <t> target changes, <a> targets;
<s> presented, <o> not; <r> swap chain resizes; <k> GL stub calls[; <m>
debug layer messages]`, the draws and clears a frame as now, the targets
the backend's count of textures alive, the presents and resizes since the
last line, the last two as phase 1 has them. "Target changes" counts a
`bind_targets` whose pair differs from the last bound pair (the game names
its targets again after each present). Phase 3 extends it.

### The backend: the targets and the clears (`win32_d3d11.c`)

Added to `d3d11_backend.h`, fixed-width integers and `void *` only:

- `uint32_t d3d11_backend_target_create(uint32_t pixel_width, uint32_t
  pixel_height, int depth)`: a `D3D11_TEXTURE2D_DESC` of that size, one mip,
  one sample, `DEFAULT` usage: colour targets `DXGI_FORMAT_B8G8R8A8_UNORM`
  (the Xbox's A8R8G8B8 byte order; `BGRA_SUPPORT` was asked for in phase
  1), bound as `RENDER_TARGET | SHADER_RESOURCE`, with a render target view
  and a shader resource view (the present's sampler reads it now; phase 6's
  draws later); depth targets `DXGI_FORMAT_D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`, bound as
  `DEPTH_STENCIL`, with a depth-stencil view (phase 6 changes the format to
  the typeless one when a draw samples a depth target, if any does). **Cleared
  once when made** (`ClearRenderTargetView` to black with alpha 1, or
  `ClearDepthStencilView` to depth 1 and stencil 0), so a target read before
  anything was drawn into it reads the same on both drivers. Kept in a table
  the handle indexes (1 upward; 0 is none), never freed; the count is for the
  statistics (`uint32_t d3d11_backend_targets_alive(void)`). A creation that
  fails is logged with the `HRESULT`, the size and the kind, and returns 0.
- `void d3d11_backend_targets_set(uint32_t color, uint32_t depth)`:
  `OMSetRenderTargets` with the views (either NULL), and the viewport
  `RSSetViewports` to the whole colour target (or the depth one): the clears
  of this phase set their own scissor, and phase 6's draws set the game's
  viewport at each draw. Remembered, so the clears and the present know what
  is bound.
- `uint32_t d3d11_backend_clear(uint32_t channels, const float color[4],
  float z, uint32_t stencil, const int32_t *rectangles, uint32_t count)`:
  rectangles as `x, y, width, height` in the bound targets' pixels, row 0 at
  the top, already clipped (the device's work above). Returns 1 if the whole
  clear went through clear calls, 2 if any of it was drawn, 0 if nothing was
  bound (the device's statistics count by it). The rule:
  - A colour clear **of all four channels whose one rectangle is the whole
    colour target** is `ClearRenderTargetView`; a depth or stencil clear
    whose rectangle is the whole depth target is `ClearDepthStencilView`
    with the flags asked for. `ID3D11DeviceContext1::ClearView` with
    rectangles exists from the 11.1 runtime, and is not used (Decisions:
    nothing of 11.1; and it is a colour-only call).
  - **Everything else is drawn**: each rectangle a `RSSetScissorRects` and a
    `Draw(3, 0)` of a full-target triangle (the vertex shader from
    `SV_VertexID`, no input layout, no vertex buffer), with a blend state
    whose write mask is the colour channels asked for (none for a depth or
    stencil clear alone; the sixteen masks made the first time each is
    used), a depth-stencil state by (depth asked, stencil asked): depth
    test `ALWAYS` with write on or off, stencil `ALWAYS` with `REPLACE` on
    pass (both faces) and the reference set at the draw, the pixel shader
    writing the colour from a constant buffer and the depth as `SV_Depth`
    (the `z` asked for; when depth is not asked the write is masked by the
    state, so one shader serves), a rasterizer state with the scissor on and
    no culling. The state objects and the constant buffer (`DYNAMIC`, `Map`
    with `DISCARD`, 32 bytes) are the backend's own, made at the first use
    and kept; they are not the game's (phase 5). Nothing is restored after:
    from this phase every state the context holds is the backend's, and
    phase 6's draws set what they need.
- **The built-in shaders**: the clear's vertex and pixel shader and the
  present's (below), HLSL string constants in the backend (`vs_4_0`,
  `ps_4_0`, `D3DCOMPILE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL3`), compiled at the first use
  with `D3DCompile` from `d3dcompiler_47.dll`, loaded as the probe loads it
  (`load_compiler`, 656: `LoadLibraryA`, `GetProcAddress`; the `pD3DCompile`
  type from `d3dcompiler.h`). This is the backend's first use of the
  compiler; phase 5 makes the compiler a service and moves the loading
  there. A compile that fails is logged with the compiler's text once, and
  the clears that need it are not drawn (counted as 0: the statistics show
  it); the present without its shaders clears the swap chain's buffer black
  and presents that, so the game still runs and the log says why the window
  is black.
- `int d3d11_backend_target_read(uint32_t handle, void *pixels, size_t
  size)`: a `STAGING` texture of the target's size and format with CPU read
  access, `CopyResource` from the target, `Map` for reading, the rows copied
  out by `RowPitch` (the target's rows are `pixel_width * 4` bytes), `Unmap`;
  the staging texture kept and remade when the size differs (one at a time
  is enough: screenshots and the self-test). 1 on success; 0, logged, on
  anything else (a depth target: not readable this way, and nothing asks).
- `int d3d11_backend_self_test(char *line, size_t line_size)`: the Vulkan
  plan's clears' self-test (its "Phase 2 — summary"): an 8x8 colour target
  and an 8x8 depth target of the backend's own, bound; cleared in full to
  (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0), depth 0.5, stencil 7; then alpha only (0.5) on the
  left half (a drawn clear); then red only (1.0) with a depth clear to 0.25
  on the top-right quarter (drawn); the colour target read back and every
  texel compared within 1 of 255 with what the NV2A would give (the three
  regions); the depth target checked through the next clear's path only if
  phase 6 adds a depth read-back (it is not read here; the line says so).
  The line: `d3d11: clears self-test: ok (a full clear, alpha only on the
  left half, red only on the top right quarter; 2 partial clears drawn, 1 by
  clear calls)`, or the first texel that differs, with what it holds and what
  it should. The targets are released after; the state objects it made stay
  (they are the clears').
- `int d3d11_backend_lost(void)`: 1 once any call answered
  `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED` or `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_RESET`. The backend logs
  `d3d11: the device was removed (<call>: <hresult>; reason <hresult name>)`
  from `GetDeviceRemovedReason` the first time, and from then on every entry
  point returns at once (0, or -1 from `present`). The debug layer's
  messages are drained once more before the backend stops. `destroy` still
  runs at exit and releases what it has.

### The backend: the swap chain and the present (`win32_d3d11_present.c`)

Copied from the probe's `step_present` (2436–2560: the factory, the
tearing check, the description, `CreateSwapChainForHwnd`,
`MakeWindowAssociation`, `make_view`, 2221) and `resize` (2243–2265), and said so
in the file's header. Phase 0 proved these on the Adreno and on WARP,
through every transition, with the debug layer silent.

- `int d3d11_backend_window_set(void *hwnd, char *line, size_t line_size)`:
  called by the device once after `d3d11_backend_create` succeeds, with
  `platform_video_native_window()` (phase 1 added it; `sdl_platform.c` 769).
  The swap chain's factory is the device's own (`IDXGIDevice` →
  `GetAdapter` → `GetParent` of `IDXGIFactory2`), not phase 1's
  `CreateDXGIFactory1` one (DXGI wants the swap chain made by the factory
  the device came from; the probe does this). `CheckFeatureSupport(DXGI_FEATURE_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING)`
  on `IDXGIFactory5` when that interface exists, else no tearing. The
  description as Decisions has it: `FLIP_DISCARD`, two buffers,
  `B8G8R8A8_UNORM`, `DXGI_SCALING_NONE`, no MSAA, `RENDER_TARGET_OUTPUT`,
  width and height 0 (the window's), `ALLOW_TEARING` in the flags when
  supported. Then `MakeWindowAssociation(hwnd, DXGI_MWA_NO_ALT_ENTER |
  DXGI_MWA_NO_WINDOW_CHANGES)`, and the back buffer's render target view.
  The line, logged by the device as the `renderer:` line is: `d3d11: swap
  chain 1280x960, FLIP_DISCARD, 2 buffers, B8G8R8A8_UNORM, tearing allowed`
  (or `tearing not supported`), or `d3d11: no swap chain (<call>: <hresult>)`,
  on which the game runs with the window black and nothing presented (the
  pacing is the stand-in's), as a lost device leaves it.
- `int d3d11_backend_present(uint32_t back_buffer, uint32_t window_width,
  uint32_t window_height, int sync_interval)`:
  1. **The resize.** The window's pixel size, which the device polls each
     frame from `platform_video_drawable_size` (the platform layer handles
     no resize event, and gains none), against the swap chain's: when they
     differ and neither is 0, `OMSetRenderTargets(0, NULL, NULL)`, the view
     released, `Flush`, `ResizeBuffers(0, 0, 0, DXGI_FORMAT_UNKNOWN, flags)`
     with the swap chain's own flags, the view made again; logged as `d3d11:
     swap chain resized 1280x960 to 1600x1000 (<hresult>)` and counted. A
     size of 0 in either axis (minimised) presents nothing and returns 0.
  2. **The letterbox**, as `d3d8_gl.c`'s `Present` works it out (4800–4809)
     from the back buffer's pixel size and the window's: the largest
     rectangle of the back buffer's shape that fits, centred.
  3. **The draw**: the swap chain's view bound, cleared black (alpha 1), the
     viewport set to the letterbox rectangle, the present's pipeline: the
     full-target triangle vertex shader (the same as the clear's, with the
     texture coordinates from `SV_VertexID`), a pixel shader sampling the
     back buffer's shader resource view with a linear-filter, clamp sampler
     (the one sampler state of this phase), the blend state with every
     channel written and no blending, depth off, `Draw(3, 0)`. Row 0 of the
     target is the top of the picture; no flip, as "Clip space" says. The
     back buffer's view must not be bound as a target while it is sampled:
     `targets_set(0, 0)` first (the device binds again at the next clear or
     draw).
  4. **The marker** (`debug.d3d11_present_marker`): after the draw, a red
     square (16 by 16 pixels) at the letterbox rectangle's top-left corner
     and a green one at its top-right, through the clear's drawn path
     (scissor, the clear's shaders, all channels), so a screenshot of the
     window shows the letterbox's edges and the picture's orientation. The
     setting is read when `config_changes()` moves, as the device reads its
     own.
  5. **`Present(sync_interval, flags)`** with `DXGI_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING`
     when the interval is 0 and tearing is supported (the probe's rule). The
     debug layer's messages are drained by the device as before (phase 1's
     `drain_debug` at `Present`). `S_OK`: return 1. `DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED`
     (minimised, or covered): return 0 (the device paces by the stand-in,
     so the game does not spin, as the probe slept 16 ms). `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED`
     or `DEVICE_RESET`: the lost path above, return -1. Anything else: logged
     once per distinct result with its name (`hr_text`), return 0.
- **Fullscreen is SDL's alone** (Decisions): the swap chain never calls
  `SetFullscreenState`; F11 (borderless) and `display.mode`'s exclusive
  fullscreen change the window's pixel size, which the resize above follows.
  Alt+Enter does nothing (`DXGI_MWA_NO_ALT_ENTER`; phase 0 saw it).
- A display change (`SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_DISPLAY_CHANGED`) needs nothing of the
  swap chain in the flip model; the pixel size, if it changes, is followed as
  above. The stand-in pacing's refresh rate is read when a setting changes
  (phase 1); a window moved to a display of another refresh rate while vsync
  is on is paced by the present's interval, which follows the display, so
  the stand-in's stale rate matters only to frames nothing presents.
- `d3d11_backend_destroy` releases the present's objects first (the view,
  the swap chain, the factory, the sampler and the shaders), then the targets
  and the clears' objects, then what phase 1 released; the live-object report
  with the layer on should again name the device's own objects only.

### The platform layer

Nothing changes in `sdl_platform.c` or `sdl_platform.h`: the no-context mode,
`platform_video_native_window`, `platform_video_drawable_size` and the
stand-in refresh-rate interval are what this phase needs, and the device
decides when `platform_video_swap` is called (above). The sentence in phase
1's section that said phase 2 removes the stand-in is corrected: it stays,
for the frames nothing presents.

### The settings

Two rows in `port_config.c`'s table, `_platform_windows`, behind `#ifdef
_WIN32` beside phase 1's:

| Setting | Type, default | Environment | Comment |
|---|---|---|---|
| `debug.d3d11_present_marker` | boolean, `false` | `HALO_D3D11_PRESENT_MARKER` | Direct3D 11 executable: a red square at the top left and a green one at the top right of the picture, so that a screenshot shows which way up it is and where its edges are. |
| `debug.d3d11_self_test` | boolean, `false` | `HALO_D3D11_SELF_TEST` | Direct3D 11 executable: at start, the renderer tries its own clears (and, from phase 3, its memory paths) and says in the log whether each is right. |

`debug.screenshot_every` and `debug.screenshot_directory` now work in the
Direct3D executable as in `halo.exe`. `debug.gpu_trace_frame`'s lines for
`SetRenderTarget` and `Clear` are phase 1's.

### Testing on this PC

`python configure.py --portable --release`, `ninja windows`; phase 1's
`config.toml` beside the executables, with `debug.gpu_stats` and
`debug.telnet_console`, `display.mode = "windowed"` at 1280x960 as phase 1
ran (the captures need the window). Each case on the Adreno and on WARP
(`display.d3d11_adapter = "warp"`), and both again with `debug.d3d11_debug =
true`:

1. **The object check, the symbol check and the GL grep** as phase 1's,
   with `port_config.o` now the only expected difference (and the checksum
   and time ones).
2. **The self-test**: `debug.d3d11_self_test = true`: its `ok` line on both
   drivers, before the first frame.
3. **The menus with the marker** (`debug.d3d11_present_marker = true`): a
   capture of the window (topmost first, as phase 1 did) shows a black client
   area with the red square at its top left and the green at its top right
   (1280x960 holds a 4:3 picture whole: no bars); the PC menus and the
   Xbox-style ones driven by keys as phase 1 drove them show their clears
   (the Xbox-style menus clear to a colour: say which). `display.resolution_scaling
   = "original"` (640x480 drawn at 640x480) for one run, so the letterbox
   scales up; and the window resized from outside to a 16:9 size, so there
   are bars: the squares at the picture's corners, not the window's. The
   statistics line: 1 clear a frame in the PC main menu by clear calls, 0
   drawn, 1 target (the back buffer; the depth buffer when a menu clears
   it), `presented` 60.
4. **A map**: `map_name a10` from the console, through the cinematic into the
   cryo room: the sky's clear colour in the window during the cinematic (a
   capture; say what colour), the clears a frame and the targets alive (the
   screen's colour and depth, and the small targets the Vulkan phase saw,
   64x64 and 128x128), and whether the fog's alpha-only clear comes (the
   drawn count; the Vulkan run did not meet it in the opening). The game
   runs on; the console's `game_time` keeps real time.
5. **Pacing**: `display.vsync = true`, `display.max_fps = 0`: 120 frames a
   second from the statistics lines' cadence, the present's interval pacing
   (the log shows `platform_video_swap` is not what paced: add a one-off
   line in the test if needed, not committed). `display.vsync = false`,
   `display.max_fps = -1`: the frame rate with clears only, in the three
   scenes of phase 0's baseline, beside phase 1's numbers with nothing
   presented. `display.max_fps = 60` with vsync off: 60.
6. **The window**: resized by its edge and from outside (1600x1000, 800x600,
   1900x1200): each a `swap chain resized` line with `S_OK`, the picture
   following (a capture after each, the marker on); F11 to borderless and
   back; `display.mode = "fullscreen"` (SDL's exclusive mode) for one run,
   and back to windowed; minimised for 10 s and restored (`presented` 0 and
   `not` 60 in the lines while minimised, no spin: the process's CPU time
   over those 10 s from `Get-Process`, under a second); Alt+Enter (nothing
   happens). No crash, no layer message.
7. **Screenshots**: `debug.screenshot_every = 300` into a folder in the
   scratch space: BMP files at the back buffer's pixel size, the clears'
   colours, the right way up (the marker is not in them: it is drawn into
   the swap chain's buffer, not the back buffer).
8. **The debug layer** (`debug.d3d11_debug = true`): no `d3d11 debug:` line
   in any of the above; at exit the live-object summary names the device's
   own objects only (9 lines, as phase 1 saw).
9. **The failure paths**: a lost device cannot be forced here (read in the
   audit: the `-1` path, the `lost` flag, the device's line); `d3dcompiler_47.dll`
   missing cannot be forced either (read: the log line and the black
   present); a swap chain that cannot be made (read). `display.d3d11_adapter
   = "nonesuch"` still runs (phase 1's note), now with a picture.
10. **`halo.exe`** as before: one run of the menus and a10's opening, the
    same log lines as phase 1's.

### Acceptance

- The object check with its one expected difference; the symbol difference
  the same three; no GL call in `d3d8_d3d11.c`.
- The self-test `ok` on both drivers, with and without the layer.
- The menus' and a map's clears on the screen on the Adreno and on WARP,
  the marker where it should be in the captures, resizing, both fullscreen
  kinds, minimising and Alt+Enter survived, vsync pacing at the display's
  rate, screenshots written; the log lines and the frame rates in "Progress"
  (the summary and the deviations, as phase 1's), the Status line updated.
- No debug layer message in any run; no lost device.
- `halo.exe` unchanged.
- The code audited against the source (`d3d8_gl.c`'s render targets, `Clear`
  and `Present` function by function: which lines are the copy's, which are
  the backend's calls in their place) and against the Direct3D 11 and DXGI
  documentation for every call the backend makes (`CreateSwapChainForHwnd`,
  `ResizeBuffers`'s rules on outstanding references, `Present`'s results,
  `ClearRenderTargetView`, `ClearDepthStencilView`, `Map` on a staging
  texture, `CopyResource`'s format rule, `OMSetRenderTargets` against a bound
  shader resource), then committed on `d3d11-backend`.

### What needs the user

Nothing is known to. If the fog's alpha-only clear is not met in a10's
opening, the self-test is the proof the drawn path works, as it was for the
Vulkan phase; the user may know a scene that clears alpha only (the fog
screen) and can say so for phase 6.

## Phase 3 — the game's memory in Direct3D buffers

How a draw's data reaches the GPU, before any draw exists: the stream ring,
the index ring, the mirror of the contiguous window and immediate mode's
vertices, with a self-test that proves the copy is made at the draw. The
design is `d3d8_gl.c`'s ("the contiguous window in GL buffers", "vertex
data", immediate mode), which this phase translates rather than replaces:
the device keeps every line of its bookkeeping (the mirror's pages,
generations and volatile rule, the rings' offsets, the index extent cache)
and the backend does only what GL did in the lines replaced: make a buffer,
write bytes into it. The Vulkan plan's phase 3 (`port/android/VULKAN.md`,
"Phase 3 — reading the game's memory", and its "Progress" entries: the
audit, the summary, the deviations) is the model for the self-test and for
what "at the draw" means; its command stream, parts and ids have no
counterpart here (the device calls the backend directly and the bytes are
copied in the call). Phase 0's `memory` step (`win32_d3d11_probe.c`,
`memory_ring`, `memory_mirror`, `memory_rewrite`) proved the three copy paths
on this PC, on both drivers, and is the code the backend copies.

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far. The
source facts it relies on (line numbers in `d3d8_gl.c`, `d3d8_d3d11.c`,
`win32_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11_present.c`, `win32_d3d11_probe.c`,
`platform.h`) are from `d3d11-backend` at `4992b2c1`; `d3d8_gl.c` is still
at `2765d816`, the commit the device was copied from. Read the files, not
the numbers, if they have moved.

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** the two rings (vertices streamed, indices streamed) as `DYNAMIC`
  buffers written with `Map`; the mirror's segments as `DEFAULT` buffers,
  their pages written through an upload ring and `CopySubresourceRegion`;
  the device's mirror, rings, index extent cache and immediate-mode vertex
  gathering back from `d3d8_gl.c` with the GL lines replaced; **the draws'
  data work running on the game's real traffic** (every draw the game makes
  looks its streams and indices up in the mirror or streams them, exactly as
  `d3d8_gl.c` does, and then stops where GL drew), so the statistics show the
  bytes mirrored and streamed a frame beside the OpenGL executable's own
  line; and a self-test of the copy paths.
- **It is not** drawing: no input layouts, no vertex shaders of the game, no
  draw calls, no textures (phases 4 to 6). The screen shows what phase 2
  shows. Busy tracking does not exist: with every draw's data copied at the
  draw, the GPU never reads the game's memory, and `IsBusy`, the locks and
  `BlockUntilNotBusy` keep answering as they do now (not busy, return at
  once; `d3d8_resources.c` does not change).
- **Nothing under `port/linux/src` changes**: no new setting (the self-test
  is `debug.d3d11_self_test`'s), no accessor. So `halo.exe` does not change
  at all, and the object check is a diff (below).
- **Nothing is copied that this phase does not need.** `xbox_textures.c`,
  `nv2a_vsh.c`, `nv2a_psh.c` and `xgpu_post.c` stay out.

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d8_d3d11.c` | the mirror (from `d3d8_gl.c` 3815–4057, its GL replaced by backend handles and writes), the rings' bookkeeping and uploads (4058–4150), the draws' data path (`setup_streams`' upload half, the indexed draw's mirror lookup and extent, quad lists' indices), immediate mode's `End` uploading, the statistics (below) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_memory.c` | the rings, the segments, the upload ring, the memory self-test: the backend's second split, sharing `win32_d3d11.h` |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11.h` | the compiler's `compile` exported to the backend's units (the self-test's pipeline needs it); `destroy` releases the memory unit's objects; Step 0 |
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d11_backend.h` | the functions below added; still `<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only |
| `tools/windows_build.py` | nothing: every `port/windows/d3d11/win32_*.c` is compiled against the Windows SDK and linked already; `python configure.py` runs again after the file is added |
| `port/windows/README.md`, `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` | the memory unit; the self-test's second line; the executable still marked in development |

### Step 0: phase 2's audit, applied

Before the phase's own work, in a commit of its own, the code findings of
the audit of phase 2 ("Progress", "Phase 2 — audit"):

- `win32_d3d11_present.c` `d3d11_backend_present` (203): after a
  `ResizeBuffers` that failed there is no view (232), and the function
  returns 0 at once; with `display.max_fps = -1` nothing paces that frame,
  so the game spins until the window's size changes. Return through
  `nothing_shown` (197), the minimised window's rest, as the other frames
  that show nothing do.
- `win32_d3d11.c` `d3d11_backend_target_create` (747): the bound pointers
  are compared with the old table after `realloc` freed it (759–762). Take
  their indices before the call and point them into the new table after.

The acceptance figure the audit found wrong (the minimised CPU time) is
text, and is left as the audit recorded it.

### The device: the mirror, the rings and the draws' data (`d3d8_d3d11.c`)

What phase 1 cut out of `d3d8_gl.c`'s memory paths comes back, with the GL
replaced by the backend's calls, in this order so the audit can follow:

1. **The sizes and the members** (`d3d8_gl.c` 461–476, 505–535):
   `STREAM_BUFFER_SIZE` (32 MB) and `INDEX_BUFFER_SIZE` (8 MB) as the
   desktop branch has them (no `STREAM_BUFFER_RING`: that is Android's);
   `stream_offset` and `index_offset` in `struct d3d11_device` where the GL
   handles were (`stream_buffer`, `index_buffer`, `vertex_array` are not
   needed: the backend owns the two rings). `streams[16]`,
   `base_vertex_index`, `attributes`, and the immediate-mode members are
   phase 1's already.
2. **The rings' bookkeeping** (`stream_reserve` 4064, `stream_upload` 4075,
   `index_upload` 4134) copied, with the GL replaced: where
   `stream_reserve` orphaned the buffer (`glBufferData(NULL)`) it calls
   `d3d11_backend_ring_orphan(D3D11_BACKEND_RING_STREAM)` and sets
   `stream_offset` to 0; where `stream_upload` wrote (`buffer_upload`, the
   `#else` of the Android branch) it calls
   `d3d11_backend_ring_write(D3D11_BACKEND_RING_STREAM, offset, data, size)`;
   `index_upload` the same with `D3D11_BACKEND_RING_INDEX`. The 16-byte
   rounding, the reservation of a whole draw's streams at once (so a wrap
   never splits them) and the offsets returned stay as they are. The
   `BUFFER_UPLOAD_PIECE` chunking of `buffer_upload` (3790–3813) is Mesa's
   GL thread's workaround and does not come: a write is one `Map`.
   **The per-frame orphan goes**: `d3d8_gl.c`'s `Present` sets both offsets
   to the buffers' sizes at each frame (4860: "orphan next frame") because
   GL's orphaning is what keeps a new frame's writes off the last frame's
   reads; `Map` with `NO_OVERWRITE` promises the same by contract (the
   bytes already written are never touched again until the wrap, and the
   wrap is a `DISCARD`), so the rings run on across frames and discard only
   when full, as phase 0's ring did (one discard every eight frames at
   4 MB a frame). Recorded as the one change to `d3d8_gl.c`'s design.
3. **The mirror** (3815–4013: the defines, the page states, `mirror`,
   `mirror_refresh`, `mirror_range`) copied whole. `mirror.buffers[]` of
   `GLuint` becomes `mirror.segments[MIRROR_SEGMENT_COUNT]` of `uint32_t`
   backend handles (0 while none; 128 segments for the 512 MB window, made
   when first needed, never freed); where `mirror_refresh` made the
   segment's buffer (3930–3935) it calls
   `d3d11_backend_segment_create(MIRROR_SEGMENT_SIZE)`, and a 0 answer
   (logged by the backend) makes `mirror_refresh` return FALSE for that
   range, as a volatile page does, so the draw streams its data instead;
   where it uploaded a run of pages (3951, `buffer_upload`) it calls
   `d3d11_backend_segment_write(segment, offset_in_segment, address, size)`.
   The `#ifdef HALO_ANDROID` branch (the `unused` flag, `host_gl_buffer_write`)
   goes. `mirror_range` returns the segment's handle where it returned the
   buffer name. `memory_watch_protect` and `memory_watch_generation` are
   called as they are: `win32_memory_watch.c` is the same object in both
   executables, running since phase 1 (`backend_initialize`, 798).
   **The volatile rule, the rewrite counting and the generations are not
   touched.** `index_extent` and its cache (4015–4057) copied as they are
   (no GL in them).
4. **The draws' data path.** `setup_streams` (4205–4333) comes back as
   `stream_data(first, count)`: its first loop (the mirror first, then one
   reservation for everything streamed) and, of its second loop, the
   streaming of the streams the mirror does not hold (`stream_upload`,
   `stats.streamed_bytes`); the `layout_attribute`, `vertex_array_get`,
   `state_vertex_array`, `state_vertex_buffer` and `state_attribute_value`
   lines are phase 6's and are left out, with a comment saying so where
   they were. The Android branches (`stream_has_colors`,
   `stream_upload_swizzled`, `NORMPACKED3`'s attribute) go. `DrawVertices`
   (4375) and `DrawIndexedVertices` (4397) take their shape from
   `d3d8_gl.c`: the guards `prepare_draw` has (3423: a draw with no current
   program, no vertex shader or no instructions is counted as
   `skipped_no_program` and does nothing; `debug.gpu_skip_vertex_shaders`
   as there), then the data work as there (the indexed draw's `mirror_range`
   on its indices, `index_extent`, `stream_data` from the base vertex; quad
   lists' `quad_indices` and `index_upload`; the streamed indices'
   `index_upload`), and then, where GL drew (`glDrawArrays`,
   `glDrawElementsBaseVertex`), nothing: the draw is counted as phase 1
   counts it. `prepare_draw` itself does not come back (phase 4 brings its
   key, phase 5 its programs): the device has `current_program()` (1579)
   and `device.vertex_shader` for the guards. `SetStreamSource` and
   `SetIndices` are phase 1's and do not change.
5. **Immediate mode**: `End` (4476–4560, the desktop branch after `#else`):
   `stream_upload(device.immediate_vertices, count * stride)` and, for a
   quad list, `quad_indices` and `index_upload`, then nothing where
   `glDrawElements`/`glDrawArrays` were; the `immediate_array` block
   (`layout_attribute`, `vertex_array_get`) is phase 6's and is left out
   with a comment. `prepare_draw(TRUE)`'s guards as above. `Begin`,
   `immediate_emit` and the `SetVertexData*` family are phase 1's.
6. **Creation**: in `backend_initialize`, after the swap chain's line,
   `d3d11_backend_memory_initialize(STREAM_BUFFER_SIZE, INDEX_BUFFER_SIZE,
   line, size)` and its line logged (below). With 0 the device logs the line
   and runs on: every ring write answers 0 and the draws' data work is
   skipped (counted as `skipped: no buffer`), the clears still show.
7. **The self-test** (`debug.d3d11_self_test`): after the clears' line,
   `d3d11_backend_memory_self_test(line, size)`, its line logged whatever it
   says (below).
8. **The device lost**: a ring or segment write that answers 0 after the
   backend reported the device lost is `backend_lost_check`'s (phase 2);
   the mirror's bookkeeping does not care (its pages are "present" as far
   as it knows; nothing reads them).

**The link is the check**, as before: `llvm-nm -g --defined-only` on
`d3d8_d3d11.o` against `d3d8_gl.o` still differs by exactly the three
symbols (everything this phase adds is static), and `grep -E '\bgl[A-Z]\w+\('`
on `d3d8_d3d11.c` is still 0.

**The statistics line** (`debug.gpu_stats`, every 60 frames) becomes:
`frame <n>: Direct3D 11 clears only; <d> draws, <i> immediate dropped,
skipped <q> no program, <b> no buffer; <c> clears (...), <t> target
changes, <a> targets; <m> KB mirrored, <s> KB streamed, <u> KB uploaded to
<g> segments, <w> ring discards; <s> presented, <o> not; <r> swap chain
resizes; <k> GL stub calls[; <m> debug layer messages]`: the mirrored and
streamed KB a frame as `d3d8_gl.c`'s line has them (`stats.mirrored_bytes`,
`stats.streamed_bytes`, the same places), the KB uploaded into segments a
frame (`mirror_refresh`'s writes), the segments alive (the backend's
count), the discards since the last line (the backend's count of `DISCARD`
maps on both rings). Phase 6 extends it.

### The backend: the rings, the segments and the upload ring (`win32_d3d11_memory.c`)

Added to `d3d11_backend.h`, fixed-width integers and `const void *` only:

- `int d3d11_backend_memory_initialize(uint32_t stream_size, uint32_t
  index_size, char *line, size_t line_size)`: the stream ring
  (`D3D11_USAGE_DYNAMIC`, `D3D11_BIND_VERTEX_BUFFER`, `CPU_ACCESS_WRITE`,
  `stream_size`), the index ring (the same with `BIND_INDEX_BUFFER`,
  `index_size`) and the upload ring (below), made as the probe's
  `buffer_make` (860) makes them. The line: `d3d11: buffers: stream ring 32
  MB, index ring 8 MB, upload ring 8 MB; address space 3,7xx MB free` (the
  free virtual space from `GlobalMemoryStatusEx`, as the probe reported it:
  the process is 32-bit and these buffers are mapped into it), or `d3d11: no
  buffers (<which>: <hresult>)`, on which 0 is returned and nothing below
  works (every call answers 0). Called once by the device.
- `void d3d11_backend_ring_orphan(uint32_t ring)`: the next write to that
  ring (`D3D11_BACKEND_RING_STREAM` 0, `D3D11_BACKEND_RING_INDEX` 1) maps
  with `D3D11_MAP_WRITE_DISCARD`; counted.
- `int d3d11_backend_ring_write(uint32_t ring, uint32_t offset, const void
  *data, uint32_t size)`: `Map` with `D3D11_MAP_WRITE_NO_OVERWRITE` (or
  `DISCARD` once after an orphan, which also starts the buffer again: the
  device writes at 0 then), `memcpy` to `offset`, `Unmap`. The probe's
  `memory_ring` (1564) is the code. A range past the buffer's end is
  refused (0, logged once) rather than mapped. 1 on success; 0 on a `Map`
  that failed (logged with its `HRESULT`; a lost device through
  `backend_failed`).
- `uint32_t d3d11_backend_segment_create(uint32_t size)`: a `DEFAULT`
  buffer of `size` bytes bound as `VERTEX_BUFFER | INDEX_BUFFER` (the
  mirror holds the game's vertices and its indices in one window, and a
  Direct3D 11 buffer may carry both bind flags), no CPU access, no initial
  data. Its handle, from 1; 0 on failure, logged with the `HRESULT` and the
  size. Kept in a table, never freed; the count for the statistics
  (`uint32_t d3d11_backend_segments_alive(void)`).
- `int d3d11_backend_segment_write(uint32_t segment, uint32_t offset, const
  void *data, uint32_t size)`: the bytes into the **upload ring** and then
  `CopySubresourceRegion(segment, 0, offset, 0, 0, upload_ring, 0, &box)`
  with the box the bytes' range in the ring. The upload ring is a
  `DYNAMIC` buffer of 8 MB bound as `VERTEX_BUFFER` (a `DYNAMIC` buffer
  needs a bind flag), written with `Map` `NO_OVERWRITE` at a running offset
  (16-aligned) and `DISCARD` when the write does not fit from the offset
  on; a write larger than the ring is refused (0, logged once: it cannot
  come, `mirror_refresh` writes at most 256 pages, 1 MB, at a time). **This
  refines the Decisions row** ("The game's memory"), which said a ring of
  `STAGING` buffers one a frame in flight: a `STAGING` buffer's `Map` waits
  for every copy still queued from it, so mapping it again for the next run
  of pages in the same frame (the mirror writes runs as draws meet them,
  dozens a frame while a map loads) would wait on the GPU at each one, where
  the probe mapped it once a frame; a `DYNAMIC` buffer's `NO_OVERWRITE` map
  never waits, the copy call is the same, and phase 0 measured that map at
  5 µs a piece. The staging ring with its frame slots is not needed, so
  there is no per-frame call. The row is changed to say so, with the
  user's decision (the copy through a buffer, not `UpdateSubresource`)
  kept. The copy is queued in the context in order with the draws that
  follow it (phase 6), which is what "at the draw" needs. 1 on success; 0
  (logged, a lost device through `backend_failed`) otherwise.
- `int d3d11_backend_memory_self_test(char *line, size_t line_size)`: the
  Vulkan plan's data self-test as the probe's `memory_rewrite` (1745) ran
  it, on the backend's own objects: a 16x16 colour target
  (`target_make`), a pass-through pipeline (the probe's `colour_hlsl` 690:
  `POSITION` float2 and `COLOR` `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`, the input layout of
  `pipeline_make` 720, compiled through `win32_d3d11.c`'s compiler), and
  three vertices covering the target (`struct vertex`, 703) in a buffer of
  the test's own in ordinary memory, "the game's memory". Four cases, each a
  write, a draw into the target, the same bytes rewritten in place with
  another colour, written again through the same path, drawn into a second
  target, both targets read back (`read_target`) and the texel at (8, 8)
  compared:
  1. the stream ring, appended (red at offset 0 with `DISCARD`, green after
     it with `NO_OVERWRITE`);
  2. the stream ring across an orphan (red, then `ring_orphan`, then green
     at 0 again: the first draw must still be red, which is what `DISCARD`'s
     renaming promises);
  3. a segment through the upload ring (red and then green at the **same**
     offset of one segment, the two copies queued in one frame: the first
     draw must be red, the second green, which is the copy's ordering in the
     context);
  4. the index ring: an indexed draw whose three indices come from the index
     ring, written red then green as in 1, the vertices in a segment.
  The line: `d3d11: memory self-test: ok (the stream ring appended and
  across a discard, a segment through the upload ring rewritten in place,
  the index ring: 8 draws, each the colour its bytes had at the draw)`, or
  the first case that failed with what was seen and what was wanted (`case
  3: second draw B G R A 255 0 0 255, wanted 0 255 0 255`). The test's
  objects are released after, the rings and the upload ring left as they
  were (its writes advance their offsets, which the device does not use
  before the first frame: the device starts at 0 with an orphan; say so in a
  comment). Immediate mode and streamed draws share case 1's path and are
  not a case of their own.
- `d3d11_backend_destroy` releases the memory unit's objects
  (`d3d11_backend_memory_release`: the three rings, the segments, the
  test's objects if any remain) after the present's and before the targets',
  as phase 2's order has it; the live-object report with the layer on
  should again name the device's own objects only.

### The platform layer and the settings

Nothing changes in `sdl_platform.c`, `sdl_platform.h` or `port_config.c`.
`debug.d3d11_self_test` now runs two tests (the clears', then the
memory's); `port_config.c`'s comment for it already says "and, from phase 3,
its memory paths". `debug.gpu_stats`' line grows as above.

### Testing on this PC

`python configure.py --portable --release`, `ninja windows`; the settings
from the environment as phase 2 ran (`HALO_DISPLAY_MODE=windowed`,
`HALO_WINDOW_SIZE=1280x960`, `HALO_D3D11_*`; `config.toml` holds no data
path and no mode, the data is found beside the executable). Each case on
the Adreno and on WARP (`HALO_D3D11_ADAPTER=warp`), and both again with
`HALO_D3D11_DEBUG=1`. Long a10 runs with vsync on or `display.max_fps`
set: the Adreno's resets of phase 2 came only with no frame limit, and
this phase has nothing to measure at thousands of frames a second but one
run (6 below).

1. **The object check** is a diff this time: `git diff --name-only
   d943c612..HEAD` lists nothing under `port/linux/src`, `source/`,
   `port/windows/src` or `tools/` but what phase 2 changed
   (`port_config.c`), so `halo.exe`'s objects are phase 2's (its audit
   accepted them without a rebuild on the same reasoning); if anything
   else under those folders changes, the full method of phase 1's summary
   runs instead. **The symbol check and the GL grep** as before.
2. **The self-test**: `HALO_D3D11_SELF_TEST=1`: the clears' `ok` line and
   the memory's `ok` line on both drivers, with and without the layer, and
   the `d3d11: buffers:` line with its free address space beside phase 0's
   figures (3,754 MB after the device on the Adreno).
3. **The menus**: the statistics line's `KB streamed` a frame (the HUD and
   text draw in immediate mode: hundreds of draws a frame, each streamed),
   `KB mirrored` (the menus' own vertex buffers, if any), `segments` (0 or
   a few), `skipped` 0 no buffer; beside `halo.exe`'s line for the same
   screen (`debug.gpu_stats` there prints `KB mirrored, KB streamed` too):
   the same order of magnitude, the differences explained (the PC menus'
   draws are the same calls in both executables).
4. **A map**: `map_name a10` from the console, through the cinematic into
   the cryo room, with `halo.exe` run the same way beside it: **the KB
   mirrored and streamed a frame in the two executables' lines for the same
   scene** (phase 0's baseline under OpenGL: 4.2 to 6.8 MB mirrored, 0.2 to
   0.7 MB streamed a frame in a10), the segments alive once the map is
   loaded (the map's window: say how many), the KB uploaded a frame after
   the load settles (phase 0's "quiet frame" was 64 pages), the ring
   discards (the stream ring at 32 MB wraps every few seconds at a10's
   rates), `skipped no program` (draws the device has no program for: say
   how many, they are phase 5's). The game runs on; `game_time` keeps real
   time; no crash.
5. **The volatile rule**: a scene with dynamic vertices (a10's cinematic
   has contrails and effects; the cryo room's particles) makes pages
   volatile and the draws stream them: `KB streamed` rises there, as it
   does under `halo.exe`. Record the two executables' numbers for the same
   moment, not a judgement.
6. **The cost**: the process's CPU time over 60 s in the cryo room from
   `Get-Process`, beside phase 2's (the clears only) and `halo.exe`'s in
   the same spot; one short run with vsync off and no limit for the frame
   rate beside phase 2's (and stopped at the first reset, if one comes).
7. **The window**: resized, F11, minimised 10 s, as phase 2's cases, one
   run each: nothing of this phase is in the window, but the rings run on
   through them.
8. **The debug layer** (`HALO_D3D11_DEBUG=1`): no `d3d11 debug:` line in
   any of the above (a `Map` misuse, a copy past a buffer's end or a
   `DISCARD` out of order would show here, and nowhere else until phase 6);
   at exit the live-object summary names the device's own objects only.
9. **The failure paths**, read in the audit, as none can be forced here: a
   ring that cannot be made (the line, `skipped no buffer` counted, the
   clears still shown); a write refused (past the end); a lost device
   during a write (the `-1`/0 path and the device's line).
10. **`halo.exe`** as before: one run of the menus and a10's opening for
    its lines (and its `KB mirrored, KB streamed` beside the Direct3D
    executable's, above).

### Acceptance

- The self-tests `ok` on both drivers, with and without the layer; the
  `buffers:` line and the address space in "Progress".
- The KB mirrored and streamed a frame in a10 and in the menus beside
  `halo.exe`'s for the same scenes, in "Progress", with any difference
  explained; the segments, uploads, discards and skips recorded; the CPU
  time and the frame rate beside phase 2's.
- No debug layer message in any run; no lost device; no crash.
- `IsBusy`, the locks and `BlockUntilNotBusy` unchanged (read in the
  audit: nothing in the device or the backend waits on the GPU for a
  resource; the only `Map` that can wait is the self-test's read-back).
- Nothing under `port/linux/src` changed; the symbol difference the same
  three; no GL call in `d3d8_d3d11.c`.
- The code audited against the source (`d3d8_gl.c`'s mirror, rings,
  `setup_streams`, the draws and `End`, function by function: which lines
  are the copy's, which are the backend's calls in their place, which are
  left out for phase 6 and marked) and against the Direct3D 11
  documentation for every call the backend makes (`Map`'s rules for
  `NO_OVERWRITE` and `DISCARD` on `DYNAMIC` buffers and the bind flags they
  need, `CopySubresourceRegion`'s rules on usage, overlap and the box,
  `CreateBuffer`'s on `DYNAMIC` with no bind flag), then committed on
  `d3d11-backend`.

### What needs the user

Nothing is known to. The Decisions row "The game's memory" is refined
above (the upload ring is `DYNAMIC`, not a ring of `STAGING` buffers; the
copy call and the reason for it are the user's decision as taken); if the
user wants the `STAGING` ring instead, phase 3 maps it once a frame and
batches the copies at the frame's end, which means a draw in the same frame
reads the segment before its pages arrive, and the plan would have to say
which draws that is right for. Phase 6 is where the bytes are seen on the
screen; until then the self-test and the statistics are the proof.

## Phase 4 — HLSL

The two shader generators as copies emitting HLSL, the header that fixes the
constant buffers' layout and the interface between the stages, the pixel
shader key made at every draw, a dump of each shader with its GLSL twin,
and the proof on this PC that every shader of the game's corpus compiles
and says the same as the OpenGL renderer's. Nothing on the screen changes.
The Vulkan plan's phase 4 (`port/android/VULKAN.md`, "Phase 4 — GLSL for
Vulkan", and its "Progress" entries: the audit, the summary, the
deviations) is the model, down to the dump's shape and the check script's
comparison; what differs is the dialect (HLSL, not GLSL 450 for Vulkan),
the clip space (the desktop branch's, with nothing to remove) and the
binding model (constant buffers in registers, not one descriptor set).
Phase 0's `compile` step (`win32_d3d11_probe.c` 1248–1527, the six
hand-converted shaders in `port/windows/probe/`, the results in "Progress",
"Phase 0 — summary") proved the compiler, the targets and `precise` on this
PC, and its conversions are the dialect's first draft.

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far, and
**to be run without the user**: every decision the phase needs is taken
below ("Standing decisions"). The source facts it relies on (line numbers
in `nv2a_vsh.c`, `nv2a_psh.c`, `xgpu.h`, `d3d8_gl.c`, `d3d8_d3d11.c`,
`win32_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11_probe.c`, `tools/vk_shader_check.py`) are
from `d3d11-backend` at `af8a31ce`; `d3d8_gl.c`, `nv2a_vsh.c` and
`nv2a_psh.c` are still at `2765d816`. Read the files, not the numbers, if
they have moved.

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** `nv2a_vsh_hlsl.c` and `nv2a_psh_hlsl.c`, copies of the
  generators with the GLSL replaced by HLSL and nothing else; `hlsl_shaders.h`
  with the constant buffers' members and offsets as `#define`s, the C
  structures checked against them at compile time, the interface's fixed
  text and the prototypes; the key made at every draw in the device
  (`pixel_key_make`, `prepare_draw`'s key without GL) and the vertex
  shader's variant chosen as `prepare_draw` chooses it; the dump
  (`debug.gpu_dump_shaders`, the existing setting) writing each shader's
  HLSL, its GLSL twin from the unchanged generators, the key's bytes and a
  manifest; a generator self-test that compiles synthetic shaders for the
  key fields the corpus lacks; and `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`, which
  compiles every dumped shader with `d3dcompiler_47.dll` and proves each
  HLSL text is its GLSL twin with the dialect's changes applied.
- **It is not** compiling in the backend at the draw, caching, input
  layouts or state objects (phase 5), nor drawing (phase 6). The HLSL is
  compiled only by the self-test and the script. The screen shows what
  phase 3 shows.
- **Nothing under `port/linux/src` changes.** `nv2a_vsh.c` and `nv2a_psh.c`
  are already compiled into `halo_d3d11.exe` (`tools/windows_build.py`
  compiles every `port/linux/src/*.c` but the replaced ones; only
  `d3d8_gl.c`, `xgpu_post.c` and `gl_functions.c` are left out of it), so
  the GLSL twins come from the real generators in the same process. No new
  setting. `halo.exe` does not change at all; the object check is a diff.
- **Nothing is translated.** The instruction decoding, the combiners, fog,
  the alpha test, alpha kill, colour sign, `coverage_alpha`, per-pixel
  lighting, the `debug.gpu_debug_*` settings and the epilogue's arithmetic
  are kept line for line; only the lines that are GLSL's grammar change.
  The copies' headers say so and name the commit they were copied from.

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/nv2a_vsh_hlsl.c` | `nv2a_vsh.c` (588 lines) with the GLSL replaced: `nv2a_hlsl_vertex_shader(const uint32_t *instructions, uint32_t count, uint32_t packed_mask, uint32_t integer_mask, uint32_t three_component_mask, const struct nv2a_vertex_lighting *lighting)`. The lighting detection (`nv2a_vertex_shader_lighting`, 218–327) is not copied: the device calls the original, as it does now (`d3d8_d3d11.c` 1563–1575) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/nv2a_psh_hlsl.c` | `nv2a_psh.c` (750 lines) with the GLSL replaced: `nv2a_hlsl_pixel_shader(const struct nv2a_pixel_shader_key *key, char target[8])`, which also says the target (`ps_4_0`, or `ps_4_1` with `alpha_test_samples`) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/hlsl_shaders.h` | `HLSL_SHADER_GENERATOR_VERSION`; the constant buffers' registers, sizes and every member's byte offset as `#define`s; the C structures with `_Static_assert` on `offsetof` and `sizeof` against them; the interface text; the semantics; the prototypes. `<stdint.h>` and the key's header only, so both halves can include it |
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d8_d3d11.c` | `pixel_key_make`; the vertex variant; the key table and the variant table; the dump; the generator self-test's call; the statistics |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11.c`, `d3d11_backend.h` | one function, `d3d11_backend_shader_compiles` (below), for the self-test; nothing else |
| `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py` | the check, from `tools/vk_shader_check.py`'s shape |
| `tools/windows_build.py` | nothing: `port/windows/d3d11/*.c` not named `win32_*` is compiled against the Xbox SDK already (486–492); `python configure.py` runs again after the files are added |
| `port/windows/README.md`, `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` | the generators, the dump, the script, the self-test's third line |

### Step 0: phase 3's audit, applied

Before the phase's own work, in a commit of its own, the code findings of
the audit of phase 3 ("Progress", "Phase 3 — audit"):

- `win32_d3d11_memory.c` `d3d11_backend_memory_self_test`, case 3: make the
  green write wrap the upload ring (set the ring's offset to its end before
  it) so the upload ring's `DISCARD`, with the red copy still queued, is
  proven as the stream ring's is. The `ok` line says "across its wrap".
- `d3d8_d3d11.c`, the statistics line: `skipped <q> no program, <b> no
  buffer` are totals over the 60 frames among per-frame averages; say so
  in the line (`skipped in 60 frames: ...`), or divide as the others are.
- `win32_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11.h`: define `d3d11_backend_target_make`,
  `_target_free` and `_read_target` under their exported names and keep the
  short names as the `#define`s only, so a search finds the definition.

The main menu's eight extra immediate draws are text, not code, and stay as
recorded.

### The dialect: what changes in the copies, and nothing else

Each item names the GLSL in the original and the HLSL in the copy. The
check script applies exactly these to the GLSL twin and expects the HLSL
text; anything else that differs is a failure.

1. **The prologue** (`nv2a_vsh.c` 331–384 `shader_prologue`, and 392–394;
   `nv2a_psh.c` 317–329, 585–622): no `#version`, no `precision`, no
   `layout(location = ...)`, no `invariant`. The `HALO_ANDROID` branches
   are dropped (the copies are desktop-only). The helpers
   (`unpack_normpacked3`, `nv2a_rcc`, `nv2a_exp`, `nv2a_log`, `nv2a_lit`,
   `signed_byte`, `signed_bytes`) keep their names and bodies, with the
   type names changed (below).
2. **Types and constructors**: `vec2/3/4` → `float2/3/4`, `ivec` →
   `int2/3/4`, `uvec` → `uint2/3/4`, `uint` and `int` as they are, `bool`
   as it is. A one-argument `vecN(x)` → `(floatN)(x)` (HLSL's constructors
   take every component; a cast broadcasts). `float(x)` → `(float)(x)`,
   `uint(x)` → `(uint)(x)`.
3. **Functions**: `mix` → `lerp`, `fract` → `frac`, `inversesqrt` →
   `rsqrt`, `texture(tex, c)` → `tex.Sample(tex_sampler, c)`,
   `texelFetch(tex, p, 0)` → `tex.Load(int3(p, 0))`, `textureSize` →
   `GetDimensions` where the point-threshold path (`nv2a_psh.c` 379–388)
   needs it. **`mod(x, y)` → `(x - y * floor(x / y))`**, not `fmod`:
   `fmod` truncates toward zero and differs for a negative `x`. `clamp`,
   `floor`, `abs`, `dot`, `normalize`, `max`, `min`, `pow`, `exp2`,
   `log2`, `fwidth`, `isnan`, `all`, `any` are the same names.
4. **Constant buffers** in place of the loose uniforms, every member a
   `float4` or an array of them, every member with `packoffset` from the
   header's `#define`s (bytes / 16, which the header also gives as
   `_REGISTER` defines so the generator prints them without arithmetic):
   - vertex stage: `cbuffer vertex_constants : register(b0) { float4 c[192]
     : packoffset(c0); }` (3,072 bytes); `cbuffer vertex_parameters :
     register(b1) { float4 viewport_scale; float4 viewport_offset; float4
     screen; }` (48 bytes; `screen_offset` → `screen.x`; `point_size` is
     gone, with `gl_PointSize = oPts.x` in the epilogue: D3D11 has no point
     size, "What D3D11 does not have").
   - pixel stage: `cbuffer pixel_parameters : register(b0)` with `ps_c0[8]`,
     `ps_c1[8]`, `ps_final_c0`, `ps_final_c1`, `fog_color`,
     `fog_parameters`, `alpha_reference` (a `float4`; the uses become
     `alpha_reference.x`), `bump_matrix[4]`, `bump_luminance[4]`,
     `texture_scale[4]` (528 bytes, the Vulkan plan's layout; `xgpu.h`
     161–172 is the list); `cbuffer model_lights : register(b1) { float4
     model_lights[12]; }` (192 bytes) declared only by lit pixel shaders
     (`nv2a_psh.c` 545–583). `texture_lod_bias` (`xgpu.h` 152–157, ES only)
     does not exist: the desktop branch already has the bias in the sampler.
   The device's `struct draw_uniforms` (`d3d8_gl.c` 366–383) is phase 6's;
   phase 4 adds the three C structures to the header and the asserts.
5. **Textures and samplers**: `uniform sampler2D tex0` → `Texture2D tex0 :
   register(t0); SamplerState tex0_sampler : register(s0);`, and `Texture3D`,
   `TextureCube` by the key's `sampler_type` (`nv2a_psh.c` 277–285), the
   stage's number as the register. `sample` (331–357) becomes
   `texN.Sample(texN_sampler, coords.xy * texture_scale[N].xy)` and `.xyz`
   for 3D and cube, with `SAMPLE_BIAS` empty as the desktop branch has it.
6. **The interface between the stages**, fixed text from the header
   (`HLSL_INTERFACE_MEMBERS`), the nine values in this order with these
   semantics: `float4 xD0 : TEXCOORD0; float4 xD1 : TEXCOORD1; float4 xB0 :
   TEXCOORD2; float4 xB1 : TEXCOORD3; float4 xT0 : TEXCOORD4; ... float4 xT3
   : TEXCOORD7; float xFog : TEXCOORD8;`, after `precise float4 position :
   SV_Position;` (phase 0: `precise` on the output member compiles at
   `vs_4_0`; it replaces `invariant gl_Position`). Lit shaders add `float4
   xWorldNormal : TEXCOORD9;` and, with two lights, `float3 xWorldPosition :
   TEXCOORD10;` **after the nine** (`nv2a_vsh.c` 397–400, `nv2a_psh.c`
   545–583). Every vertex shader writes all nine (and its lit extras) and
   every pixel shader declares all nine (and its lit extras): a vertex
   shader's outputs may exceed a pixel shader's inputs, never the reverse,
   and the order is the header's, so a lit vertex shader pairs with any
   pixel shader and a lit pixel shader only with a lit vertex shader, which
   is how `prepare_draw` pairs them (3482–3500). The vertex shader's entry
   is `vs_out main(vs_in input)`; the pixel shader's `float4 main(vs_out
   input) : SV_Target`, and `gl_FragCoord` → `input.position`.
7. **Vertex inputs** (`nv2a_vsh.c` 401–416): `struct vs_in` with sixteen
   members, `float4 v<i>_in : TEXCOORD<i>` for an attribute the masks do
   not name, `uint v<i>_packed : TEXCOORD<i>` for one in `packed_mask`
   (`D3DVSDT_NORMPACKED3`, unpacked as now), `int4 v<i>_int : TEXCOORD<i>`
   for one in `integer_mask` (`D3DVSDT_SHORT1` to `SHORT4`: DXGI has no
   "signed 16-bit as float" format, so the layout gives `SINT` and the
   shader converts, `v<i> = (float4)v<i>_int`); and after the copies,
   `v<i>.w = 1.0` for one in `three_component_mask` (`SHORT3`,
   `NORMSHORT3`, `PBYTE3`: no three-component 16-bit or 8-bit DXGI format
   exists, so phase 5's layout reads four and the shader restores the `w`
   GL's three-component format gave). The three masks are made by the
   device from the declaration where `parse_declaration` makes the packed
   one (`d3d8_d3d11.c` 1479–1526) and are part of the shader's identity.
   All sixteen are declared always, as now, and the Vulkan plan's stride-0
   slot for an attribute the declaration does not feed is phase 6's.
8. **Clip space and the epilogue** (`nv2a_vsh.c` 531–587): the desktop
   branch as it is: the screen transform undone, the half pixel, the
   `w == 0` guard, the clamps; the Android flip and remap (567–573) do not
   come, and nothing replaces them ("Clip space, the one place this is
   simpler"). `gl_PointSize` goes (item 4).
9. **The pixel shader's ends**: `discard;` is `discard;`. The alpha test
   (`nv2a_psh.c` 711–737) as it is, with `alpha_reference.x`; its
   multisampled form (719–732, `gl_SampleMask[0]`) writes a second output,
   `uint coverage : SV_Coverage`, through a `struct ps_out`, and the
   generator reports `ps_4_1` for that shader (`SV_Coverage` as an output
   is shader model 4.1's; feature level 11_0 has it; everything else stays
   `ps_4_0` as the Decisions row says). `fragment_color` is the return
   value (748). The atomic counter (597–607, 744–747) does not exist:
   `count_samples` is 0 in every key (occlusion queries are phase 6's).
   `layout(early_fragment_tests)` does not exist.
10. **`debug.gpu_debug_expression`, `_texture0`, `_flat`** (738–743): read
    as now, inserted as now; the expression is the user's and is HLSL here.

### The device: the key at every draw, the variant, the dump (`d3d8_d3d11.c`)

1. **`pixel_key_make(struct nv2a_pixel_shader_key *key)`**: `prepare_draw`'s
   key (`d3d8_gl.c` 3440–3476) without GL: the combiner state copied with
   the constants zeroed; `texture_modes`; **`bind_textures`' decisions
   only** (3022–3124): for each stage, no texture, no `Data`, or mode 0,
   0x04, 0x05 → `_xgpu_sampler_none`, mode 0x11 → `_xgpu_sampler_2d`; else
   `xgpu_texture_describe(texture->Format, texture->Size)` (the device's
   `textures[stage]`, 343) gives `cube_map` → cube, `volume` → 3D, else 2D
   (a render target the stage samples, `xgpu_render_target_find`, is 2D);
   for stage 0, `coverage_alpha` and `point_threshold` from the hi-res
   override as the Vulkan plan's deviation 3 found them:
   `hud_hires_override_find(address, width, height, ...)` then
   `hud_hires_override_coverage` and `_point_threshold` (`hud_hires.h` 49,
   58, 61; `xbox_textures.c` 921–922, 1040 show the inputs), without the decode;
   then `alpha_kill`, `color_sign`, the `coverage_alpha` blend rule
   (3464–3467), `alpha_test_function`, `fog_enable`, `fog_table_mode`;
   `alpha_test_samples` 0 (the target's samples are phase 6's;
   recorded), `count_samples` 0. The key is zeroed first so its padding
   hashes the same.
2. **The variant**: `per_pixel_lighting()` (`d3d8_gl.c` 3394–3405) and the
   object's `lighting.lights` and `lighting_failed` decide lit or not, as
   3482–3500 do (a lit vertex shader that fails is phase 5's concern: here
   `lighting_failed` stays FALSE); the immediate variant has every mask 0
   (`End`). So a vertex shader is identified by (`HLSL_SHADER_GENERATOR_VERSION`,
   the program's words, the three masks, lit).
3. **The tables**: the key table as `fragment_shader_get`'s (2603–2652: the
   4-entry MRU, the 1,024 buckets, `hash_words` of the key), each entry
   holding the key and, from phase 5, a handle; the variant table on the
   object (`vertex_shader_object`, 308–325: a `[2 lit][3 variant]` of
   entries, stream/immediate/lit as GL's `shader[2]` and `lit_shader[2]`
   were). A draw looks both up after `draw_ready` (2031–2058), in a
   `draw_note(immediate)` that phase 5 grows; an entry first met is counted
   and dumped. The statistics line gains `<k> pixel keys, <v> vertex
   variants met` (totals since the start).
4. **The dump** (`debug.gpu_dump_shaders`, read at 851–854 and unused
   since phase 1): into the folder, the first time each is met:
   `vs<hash16>.hlsl` and `vs<hash16>.glsl` (the twin from
   `nv2a_vertex_shader_to_glsl` with the same lighting argument, which is
   the OpenGL executable's text for the same shader, byte for byte);
   `ps<hash16>.hlsl`, `ps<hash16>.glsl` (the twin from
   `nv2a_pixel_shader_to_glsl`) and `ps<hash16>.key` (the key's bytes);
   `manifest.txt`, one line a file: the name, the stage, the entry, the
   target, the masks in hex, the program's id, and for vertex shaders the
   OpenGL executable's dump name for it (`vs%03lu_%d[_lit].glsl`, 2583–2595)
   so the two dumps can be matched by eye. The hash is the FNV-1a 64 of
   the identity above (phase 5's cache name); the GLSL twin of a lit
   variant is the lit text. The log says `shader dump: <v> vertex shaders
   and <p> pixel shaders written` every 600 frames when more came.
5. **The generator self-test** (`debug.d3d11_self_test`, third line):
   synthetic keys for what the corpus is known to lack (the Vulkan
   summary: fog, colour sign, alpha kill on stages 1–3, and here
   `alpha_test_samples`, each sampler type, a lit pair with one and with
   two lights, every mask bit of a vertex shader made from a short
   built-in program): each generated and handed to
   `d3d11_backend_shader_compiles(source, size, name, entry, target)`,
   which compiles it and answers 1, or 0 with the compiler's text logged.
   The line: `d3d11: shader self-test: ok (<n> synthetic shaders compile:
   fog, colour sign, ..., SV_Coverage at ps_4_1)` or `FAILED (<which>)`.
   Without this the `ps_4_1` path and the lit interface would be unproven
   until phase 6.

### The backend (`win32_d3d11.c`, `d3d11_backend.h`)

`int d3d11_backend_shader_compiles(const char *source, size_t size, const
char *name, const char *entry, const char *target)`: `d3d11_backend_compile`
(exported in phase 3) with the blob released; 0 with the compiler's
messages logged. Nothing else: the backend keeps no shader of the game's
until phase 5.

### The check: `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py <dump folder> [<OpenGL dump folder>]`

Runs on this PC from a clean checkout with Python alone:

1. Loads `d3dcompiler_47.dll` with `ctypes.WinDLL` and calls `D3DCompile`
   (`pSrcData, SrcDataSize, pSourceName, NULL, NULL, pEntrypoint, pTarget,
   D3DCOMPILE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL3 (1 << 15), 0, &code, &errors`); reads a
   blob through its vtable (`GetBufferPointer` at index 3, `GetBufferSize`
   at 4) and releases it (`Release` at 2). Compiles every `.hlsl` with the
   manifest's entry and target; a failure is reported with the compiler's
   text; the bytecode sizes and the times are summed (phase 0's figures
   beside them).
2. Builds the expected HLSL from each `.glsl` twin by the dialect's rules
   above, the prologue and the interface from `hlsl_shaders.h`'s text (the
   script parses the header's `#define`s and strings, as
   `vk_shader_check.py` parses `vk_shaders.h`: `expected_from_gl`, 195),
   normalises whitespace and diffs. **A shader that compiles and draws
   black is the failure this exists to catch**; prove the comparison once by
   a one-line change in a pixel shader, which must fail with the diff, and
   put that in "Progress".
3. Reads every `.key`, reports distinct keys and how many values each
   field takes (`key_statistics`, 295); a field that never varies is
   flagged (an unfilled key was the Vulkan attempt's black-shader bug).
4. With the OpenGL executable's dump folder: matches its `.glsl` files to
   the twins by content, reports how many of each dump have a match and
   lists the rest (a shader one executable met and the other did not is
   normal across two runs; a twin with no OpenGL file of the same content
   from the same scenes is not).
5. Prints the counts, and exits non-zero on any failure.

### The platform layer and the settings

Nothing changes. `debug.gpu_dump_shaders` and `debug.d3d11_self_test` are
the settings used. `debug.gpu_debug_*` reach the copies as they reach the
originals.

### Testing on this PC

`python configure.py --portable --release`, `ninja windows`; the settings
from the environment (`HALO_DISPLAY_MODE=windowed`, `HALO_WINDOW_SIZE=1280x960`,
`HALO_VOLUME=0.05`, `HALO_MAX_FPS=120`, `HALO_D3D11_*`,
`HALO_GPU_DUMP_SHADERS=<folder>`), `config.toml` backed up by MD5 before
the first run and put back after the last. The game driven as phase 3
was (the brief's harness: keys with scan codes, the script console on
port 2323, `map_name a10`, `game_speed 8` to the cryo room and `1` again).

1. **The object check** is a diff: `git diff --name-only d943c612..HEAD`
   over `port/linux/src`, `source/`, `port/windows/src` and `tools/` lists
   `port_config.c` (phase 2's) and `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py` (no
   input of `halo.exe`); **the symbol check and the GL grep** as before,
   and the same grep on the two copies (0).
2. **The copies against the originals**, by a script that diffs
   `nv2a_vsh_hlsl.c` with `nv2a_vsh.c` and `nv2a_psh_hlsl.c` with
   `nv2a_psh.c` and classes every differing line under one of the dialect's
   ten items; a line under none is a finding. The counts in "Progress".
3. **The self-tests**: `HALO_D3D11_SELF_TEST=1`: the clears', the memory's
   and the shader self-test's `ok` lines on the Adreno and on WARP (the
   compiler is the same CPU code on both), with the layer on once.
4. **The corpus**: `halo_d3d11.exe` with the dump set, the PC menus (each
   item entered and left by keys: Campaign, Multiplayer, Settings and its
   screens, Profiles), `HALO_MENUS=xbox` once for the Xbox-style menus, and
   a10 from the console through the cinematic into the cryo room and 60 s
   of the settled room (phase 3's a10 figures: `game_time` 6,600 and on);
   `halo.exe` the same way with its dump (`debug.gpu_dump_shaders` writes
   `vs%03lu_%d.glsl` and `ps_%08lx.glsl` there) for the match. Phase 0's
   corpus from the same scenes was 19 vertex and 80 pixel shaders.
5. **The script** on the dump folder with the OpenGL folder beside it: every
   shader compiles, 0 differences, the key fields' statistics (the Vulkan
   corpus: 35 of 57 combiner words varied, `texture_modes` 13 values,
   `sampler_type` 3/4/4/3, `alpha_kill[0]` 2, `alpha_test_function` 2,
   `coverage_alpha` 2; `color_sign`, `fog_enable`, `fog_table_mode` never),
   the match counts; the one-line change proven to fail.
6. **The game as before**: the statistics line's `pixel keys` and `vertex
   variants` settle (the menus: a handful; a10: tens); the KB mirrored and
   streamed as phase 3's; `skipped 0`; no crash; `halo.exe` unchanged.
7. **The debug layer**: no `d3d11 debug:` line (nothing of this phase
   reaches the device but the self-test's compiles, which the layer does
   not see; the run is the regression check).

### Acceptance

- The copies differ from the originals only under the dialect's ten items
  (the classed diff in "Progress"); the header's asserts compile; the
  interface text is one string used by both generators.
- Every dumped shader compiles on this PC; the comparison finds nothing;
  the one-line change fails; the key fields vary as the Vulkan corpus's
  did, with any field that never varies explained from the scenes.
- The shader self-test `ok` on both drivers: the `ps_4_1` coverage path,
  the lit interface with one and two lights, every mask bit compile.
- The OpenGL dump of the same scenes matches the twins by content, with the
  unmatched listed and explained.
- Nothing under `port/linux/src` changed; the symbol difference the same
  three; no GL call in the device or the copies.
- The code audited: the copies against the originals line by line; the key
  against `prepare_draw` and `bind_textures` field by field; the dump's
  twin call against `vertex_shader_get` and `fragment_shader_get`; then
  committed on `d3d11-backend`.

### Standing decisions (no user step)

This phase and the next are run without the user. What earlier phases
asked is decided here:

- **WARP**: the self-tests and the menus run on WARP; a10 does not (the
  user's decision in phases 2 and 3, kept).
- **Frame limit**: every run at `HALO_MAX_FPS=120` or with vsync; no
  unlimited run (the Adreno's resets are known and not this phase's).
- **A reset, a crash or a lost device** in a run is recorded with its log
  and the run repeated once; twice is a finding that stops the phase, not
  a reason to change system settings.
- **What is left for the user** (a second GPU family, the first fight by
  hand) is listed in "Progress" and not asked for.

## Phase 5 — shaders and state objects

What a draw binds, made once and kept: the shader service (a shader by its
hash, the bytecode cache on disk, `D3DCompile` at the draw for one not in
it, the shader object), the input layouts from the declarations, the four
kinds of state objects from the render state and the texture stage state,
all requested at every draw and counted, none bound. The Vulkan plan's
phase 5 ("Phase 5 — shader and pipeline cache", and its "Progress" entries)
is the model for the service, the files and the tests; what has no
counterpart here is its thread (the Decisions row "Shader compiler": the
compile is synchronous at the draw), its pipeline objects (D3D11's state
objects are made in microseconds and need no cache) and its placeholder
state (the real state is made here, since D3D11 lets each piece be made
alone). The Vulkan plan's deviations 1–7 and the two audit fixes are read
before the cache is written. Nothing on the screen changes.

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far, and
to be run without the user. The line numbers are from `d3d11-backend` at
`af8a31ce`, before phase 4's changes: read the files.

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** `win32_d3d11_shaders.c`: the compiler's loading moved there
  from `win32_d3d11.c` (phase 2's `load_compiler`, phase 3's
  `d3d11_backend_compile`); the cache on disk; the shader table by handle;
  the input layouts by hash; the blend, depth-stencil, rasterizer and
  sampler state objects by hash; the self-test's cases. In the device, at
  every draw after phase 4's note: the shaders' handles (compiled through
  the service when first met), the layout's handle, the four state
  descriptions translated from the Xbox's enumerants as `apply_raster_state`
  and `configure_sampler` translate them to GL, each handed to the backend
  for a handle. The statistics say what was made, compiled, cached and
  failed.
- **It is not** binding or drawing (phase 6, which takes the handles from
  the entry the draw filled), the uniforms (phase 6), textures (phase 6).
  No compile thread: the Decisions row stands, and the cost of the first
  run is measured here so the row can be revisited with numbers.
- **Nothing under `port/linux/src` changes.** No new setting: the cache's
  folder is fixed (below) and a cold start is the folder removed. The
  object check is a diff.

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_shaders.c` | the service: the compiler, the cache, the shaders, the layouts, the state objects, the self-test; the backend's third split, sharing `win32_d3d11.h` |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11.h` | `load_compiler`, `d3d11_backend_compile` and the built-in shaders' compiles moved to the service's unit (`make_shaders` calls it); `destroy` releases the service's objects after the memory unit's |
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d11_backend.h` | the functions and the four description structures below; `<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only |
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d8_d3d11.c` | the hashes; `draw_note` grown with the shaders, the layout and the states; the translation tables; `lighting_failed` set as `prepare_draw` sets it; the statistics |
| `port/windows/d3d11/hlsl_shaders.h` | nothing new unless the self-test needs a string |
| `port/windows/README.md`, `port/windows/d3d11/README.md` | the cache's folder and what may be deleted; the service; the statistics; the self-test's fourth line |

### Step 0: phase 4's audit, applied

Before the phase's own work, in a commit of its own, the code findings of
the audit of phase 4 ("Progress", "Phase 4 — audit"), when there are any.

### The device: the handles at every draw (`d3d8_d3d11.c`)
