1. **Identity.** A vertex shader's hash: FNV-1a 64 over
   `HLSL_SHADER_GENERATOR_VERSION`, the instruction words (hashed once in
   `CreateVertexShader`, 1528–1548, and kept on the object), the three
   masks and the lit flag; a pixel shader's: FNV-1a 64 over the version and
   the key's bytes (zeroed before filling, so the padding is 0). The same
   values name the dump's files (phase 4) and the cache's. `hash_words`
   (`d3d8_gl.c` 2560–2568, FNV-1a 32) stays for the key table's buckets.
2. **`draw_note`** (phase 4's) grows, in `prepare_draw`'s order
   (3482–3500): the pixel key's entry has a handle or is asked for one
   (`d3d11_backend_shader_find`; 0 → the HLSL generated and
   `d3d11_backend_shader_make`, which compiles now and answers the handle,
   or 0 failed); the vertex variant's entry the same; a lit vertex shader
   that fails sets `lighting_failed`, logs the line `prepare_draw` logs
   (3489–3491) and the draw takes the unlit variant with the key's
   `per_pixel_lighting` 0, as there. A shader that failed stays failed for
   the run and its draws are counted `skipped no shader`.
3. **The layout**: one per (vertex shader object, variant) made when first
   needed, from the declaration's elements (`vertex_element`, 318–325:
   `reg`, `stream`, `type`, `offset`) through the format table below and
   the variant's vertex shader handle (the layout needs its bytecode): `d3d11_backend_layout_make(elements, count, vertex_shader)`; kept on
   the object's variant entry. The immediate variant's layout is sixteen
   `R32G32B32A32_FLOAT` from slot 0 at `i * 16` with the stride of
   `XGPU_VERTEX_ATTRIBUTE_COUNT * 16` (`End`). An attribute the
   declaration does not feed is left out of the layout here; phase 6 gives
   it the stride-0 slot (the compiler keeps every declared input in the
   signature; `CreateInputLayout` accepts a layout missing an element the
   shader declares only if that element is unused, so phase 6's slot is
   where this is settled, and phase 5 records which shaders have unfed
   inputs).
4. **The format table** (`attribute_format`, `d3d8_gl.c` 4155–4184, and
   `vertex_type_bytes`, 2371–2396), one entry an Xbox type:
   `FLOAT1`–`FLOAT4` → `R32_FLOAT`, `R32G32_FLOAT`, `R32G32B32_FLOAT`,
   `R32G32B32A32_FLOAT`; `FLOAT2H` → `R32G32B32_FLOAT` (GL reads three
   floats); `D3DCOLOR` → `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` (GL: `GL_BGRA` normalised bytes);
   `SHORT1`, `SHORT2`, `SHORT4` → `R16_SINT`, `R16G16_SINT`,
   `R16G16B16A16_SINT` and `SHORT3` → `R16G16B16A16_SINT` (the shader's
   `integer_mask` and `three_component_mask`, phase 4); `NORMSHORT1`,
   `2`, `4` → `R16_SNORM`, `R16G16_SNORM`, `R16G16B16A16_SNORM` and
   `NORMSHORT3` → `R16G16B16A16_SNORM`; `PBYTE1`, `2`, `4` → `R8_UNORM`,
   `R8G8_UNORM`, `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` and `PBYTE3` → `R8G8B8A8_UNORM`;
   `NORMPACKED3` → `R32_UINT`. A four-component format over a
   three-component element reads the next element's bytes for `w`, which
   the shader overwrites, and at a buffer's very end reads past it, which
   D3D11 defines as zero (the input assembler is bounds-checked). The IA
   fills the components a format lacks with `(0, 0, 0, 1)`, as GL does.
5. **The state descriptions**, filled from `D3D__RenderState` and
   `D3D__TextureState` as `apply_raster_state` (3145–3322) and
   `configure_sampler` (2858–2883) read them, zeroed first, each handed to
   the backend which hashes it and answers a handle. The Xbox's values are
   GL's enumerants (`xdk_pdb.h` 114–157, 172–189, 470–513), so this is
   where the tables that GL never needed are written, entry by entry:
   - **blend** (`struct d3d11_backend_blend`): enabled
     (`ALPHABLENDENABLE`); source and destination factors from `SRCBLEND`,
     `DESTBLEND`: `ZERO` 0, `ONE` 1, `SRCCOLOR` 768, `INVSRCCOLOR` 769,
     `SRCALPHA` 770, `INVSRCALPHA` 771, `DESTALPHA` 772, `INVDESTALPHA`
     773, `DESTCOLOR` 774, `INVDESTCOLOR` 775, `SRCALPHASAT` 776,
     `CONSTANTCOLOR` 32769, `INVCONSTANTCOLOR` 32770, `CONSTANTALPHA`
     32771, `INVCONSTANTALPHA` 32772 → `D3D11_BLEND_ZERO`, `ONE`,
     `SRC_COLOR`, `INV_SRC_COLOR`, `SRC_ALPHA`, `INV_SRC_ALPHA`,
     `DEST_ALPHA`, `INV_DEST_ALPHA`, `DEST_COLOR`, `INV_DEST_COLOR`,
     `SRC_ALPHA_SAT`, `BLEND_FACTOR`, `INV_BLEND_FACTOR`, `BLEND_FACTOR`,
     `INV_BLEND_FACTOR` (D3D11 has no separate constant alpha: the factor's
     alpha is the constant's alpha, which is what GL's `CONSTANT_ALPHA` reads
     for the alpha channel); **the alpha channel's factors are the same with
     `_COLOR` replaced by `_ALPHA`** (`SRC_COLOR` → `SRC_ALPHA`, `DEST_COLOR`
     → `DEST_ALPHA`, inverses likewise: D3D11 refuses a colour factor in
     `SrcBlendAlpha`/`DestBlendAlpha`, and `glBlendFunc`'s alpha channel
     reads exactly those); the op from `BLENDOP` as `blend_equation`
     (3132–3143): `SUBTRACT` 32778 → `SUBTRACT`, `REVSUBTRACT` 32779 and
     `REVSUBTRACTSIGNED` 61445 → `REV_SUBTRACT`, `MIN` 32775 → `MIN`,
     `MAX` 32776 → `MAX`, else (`ADD` 32774, `ADDSIGNED` 61446) → `ADD`;
     the write mask from `COLORWRITEENABLE` (3258–3263 in the function);
     `BLENDCOLOR` is not in the object (the factor is set at the bind,
     phase 6); no alpha-to-coverage; one target. The sixteen write-mask
     objects of phase 2's clears stay the clears' own.
   - **depth-stencil** (`struct d3d11_backend_depth_stencil`): depth
     enabled when a depth target is bound and `ZENABLE` (the `has_depth`
     of `bind_targets`, which the note calls as `prepare_draw` does, 3451,
     without binding: the targets' table is phase 2's); write when also
     `ZWRITEENABLE`; the function from `ZFUNC`: 512–519 → `NEVER` …
     `ALWAYS` (`D3D11_COMPARISON_NEVER` is 1, so `value - 511`), 0 →
     `NEVER`; stencil enabled when `has_depth` and `STENCILENABLE`; the
     read mask `STENCILMASK` and write mask `STENCILWRITEMASK` (8 bits);
     both faces the same: the function from `STENCILFUNC` as `ZFUNC`'s, the
     ops from `STENCILFAIL`, `STENCILZFAIL`, `STENCILPASS` as
     `stencil_operation` (3126–3130) reads them: `KEEP` 7680, `ZERO` 0,
     `REPLACE` 7681, `INCRSAT` 7682, `DECRSAT` 7683, `INVERT` 5386, `INCR`
     34055 (wrapping), `DECR` 34056 → `D3D11_STENCIL_OP_KEEP`, `ZERO`,
     `REPLACE`, `INCR_SAT`, `DECR_SAT`, `INVERT`, `INCR`, `DECR`;
     `STENCILREF` is not in the object (set at the bind).
   - **rasterizer** (`struct d3d11_backend_rasterizer`): fill from
     `FILLMODE`: `WIREFRAME` 6913 → `WIREFRAME`, `SOLID` 6914 and `POINT`
     6912 → `SOLID` (D3D11 has no point fill; recorded); cull from
     `CULLMODE` and `FRONTFACE` as 3268–3290 decide: `NONE` 0 → `NONE`,
     else `FRONT` when `CULLMODE == FRONTFACE`, `BACK` otherwise, and
     `FrontCounterClockwise` true when `FRONTFACE` is `D3DFRONT_CCW` (the
     desktop branch, 3276); the z bias from `SOLIDOFFSETENABLE` with
     `POLYGONOFFSETZSLOPESCALE` as `SlopeScaledDepthBias` and
     `POLYGONOFFSETZOFFSET` as `DepthBias` (an integer; GL's "units" and
     D3D11's `DepthBias` both scale the format's smallest step, so the
     float is rounded), `DepthBiasClamp` 0, 0 and 0 when disabled;
     `ScissorEnable` true always (GL's scissor is the viewport, 3167–3172);
     `DepthClipEnable` true; `MultisampleEnable` and `AntialiasedLineEnable`
     false here (phase 6 decides with the target's samples).
   - **sampler** (`struct d3d11_backend_sampler`), from
     `configure_sampler`'s eleven inputs (2858–2883) as `sampler_parameters`
     (2784–2823) reads them: the filter from `MINFILTER`, `MIPFILTER`,
     `MAGFILTER` (`POINT` 1, `LINEAR` 2, `ANISOTROPIC` 3, `NONE` 0 for the
     mip): `D3D11_FILTER_MIN_MAG_MIP_POINT` … `MIN_MAG_MIP_LINEAR` by the
     eight combinations (`D3D11_ENCODE_BASIC_FILTER` with the min, mag, mip
     each point or linear; a mip filter of `NONE` is point with `MaxLOD`
     0, which is how GL's `_NEAREST`/`_LINEAR` without mipmaps read, and
     `levels > 1` false from `configure_sampler` says the texture has none),
     `ANISOTROPIC` when the min filter is `ANISOTROPIC` and `MAXANISOTROPY`
     is above 1, with `MaxAnisotropy` from it; the address modes from
     `ADDRESSU/V/W` (`address_mode`, 2750–2764): `WRAP` 1 → `WRAP`, `MIRROR`
     2 → `MIRROR`, `CLAMP` 3 and `CLAMPTOEDGE` 5 → `CLAMP`, `BORDER` 4 →
     `BORDER`, else `WRAP`; `MipLODBias` from `MIPMAPLODBIAS`'s float bits
     (the desktop branch's `GL_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS`); `MinLOD` from
     `MAXMIPLEVEL`, `MaxLOD` `FLT_MAX`; `BorderColor` from `BORDERCOLOR`
     (`color_to_vec4`); `ComparisonFunc` `NEVER`. One sampler object a
     stage with a texture; `sampler_get`'s 512-entry cache (2827–2853) is
     replaced by the backend's hash.
6. **The statistics line** gains: `shaders <c> cached, <k> compiled in
   <ms> ms, <f> failed; <l> layouts, <b>/<d>/<r>/<s> states; skipped <n>
   no shader` (totals since the start; the compile time is the sum of the
   `D3DCompile` calls, which is the first run's hitch budget).

### The backend: the service (`win32_d3d11_shaders.c`, `d3d11_backend.h`)

1. **The compiler**: `load_compiler` and `d3d11_backend_compile` move here
   unchanged; the DLL's file version (phase 0 reported it:
   `GetFileVersionInfo`, as the probe's `load_compiler` 656–688 does)
   names the cache's folder.
2. **The cache's folder**: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\halo\d3d11_shaders\<HLSL_SHADER_GENERATOR_VERSION>-<compiler version>\`
   (`GetEnvironmentVariableA("LOCALAPPDATA")`; the saves are under
   `%APPDATA%\halo`, `xbox_files.c` 151, and are not touched; this folder
   is the renderer's own, and removing it is the cold start). If the
   variable is unset or the folder cannot be made: no cache, logged once,
   every shader compiled each run. At start the folder is listed once
   (`FindFirstFileA`) into a set of the names' hashes, no file opened;
   every other folder under `d3d11_shaders\` is removed when it holds only
   `.dxbc` and `.tmp` files (anything else stops the removal, logged);
   `failed\` beside the version folders holds the HLSL of failed compiles,
   `<name>.hlsl`, for the check script.
3. **The file** `<v|p><hash 16 hex>.dxbc`: a header of magic `HDXB`, a
   format version, the generator version, the hash, the target (8 bytes),
   the bytecode's size and its CRC-32 (a table in the unit; no library),
   then the bytecode; written to `<name>.tmp` and `MoveFileExA` with
   `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`. A file asked for whose header or CRC is
   wrong is deleted and its shader compiled (the Vulkan plan's deviation
   7: at the ask, not at start).
4. **The functions**:
   - `uint32_t d3d11_backend_shader_find(uint32_t stage, uint64_t hash)`:
     the handle (from 1) of a shader already made this run; else, if the
     set has the file, the file read, checked, the object made from the
     bytecode (`CreateVertexShader`/`CreatePixelShader`, phase 0: 0.01 to
     0.1 ms) and the handle; else 0. A vertex shader's bytecode is kept
     (the layouts need it).
   - `uint32_t d3d11_backend_shader_make(uint32_t stage, uint64_t hash,
     const char *source, size_t size, const char *entry, const char
     *target)`: compiles now (`D3DCompile`, `OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL3`), writes
     the file, makes the object, answers the handle; 0 when the compile
     failed, with the compiler's text logged once per hash and the source
     written to `failed\`. The source is copied during the call. When any
     `debug.gpu_debug_*` setting is set (the device says so once through
     `d3d11_backend_shader_cache_enable(0)`), nothing is written or read:
     the text is not the generator's.
   - `uint32_t d3d11_backend_layout_make(const struct d3d11_backend_layout_element *elements, uint32_t count, uint32_t vertex_shader)`:
     the element as fixed-width fields (semantic index, DXGI format as its
     number, slot, byte offset); hashed with the vertex shader's hash; the
     handle of one made before, else `CreateInputLayout` with the shader's
     bytecode. A failure (a declared input not in the layout) is logged
     once with the shader's hash and answered 0, and the draw is counted
     `skipped no layout`.
   - `uint32_t d3d11_backend_blend_make(const struct d3d11_backend_blend *)`,
     `_depth_stencil_make`, `_rasterizer_make`, `_sampler_make`: the
     description hashed (FNV-1a 64 over the zeroed structure); the handle
     of one made before, else the object. D3D11 itself returns the same
     object for an equal description; the hash saves the call.
   - `void d3d11_backend_shaders_statistics(uint32_t counts[8])`: cached,
     compiled, compile milliseconds, failed, layouts, blend, depth-stencil,
     rasterizer, samplers (the device prints them).
   - `int d3d11_backend_shaders_self_test(char *line, size_t line_size)`:
     the Vulkan plan's three cases on the service: a good built-in pair
     made (compiled, the files written), found again from memory, released
     from memory and found again from the files; a bad shader (`'this' :
     Reserved word` as there) failed, logged once and written to `failed\`;
     a file damaged by the test (its CRC changed) deleted and remade. Then a
     layout from a declaration with every type in the format table over the
     test's vertex shader, and one object of each state kind from a
     description, found again by the same description. The line: `d3d11:
     shader service self-test: ok (...)` or `FAILED (<case>)`.
   - `d3d11_backend_shaders_release` in `destroy`'s order, after the memory
     unit's.
5. **The built-in shaders** (phase 2's clear and present, the memory
   self-test's) compile through the moved `d3d11_backend_compile` as before,
   outside the cache.

### The platform layer and the settings

Nothing changes. `debug.d3d11_self_test` runs four tests. `debug.gpu_stats`'
line grows as above.

### Testing on this PC

As phase 4's, with the cache's folder removed before the first run of each
driver (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\halo\d3d11_shaders\`). a10 on the Adreno only;
the menus on both.

1. **The object check** (a diff: `tools/` gains nothing this time), the
   symbol check, the GL grep, as before.
2. **The self-tests** (`HALO_D3D11_SELF_TEST=1`): the four `ok` lines on
   both drivers, with and without the layer.
3. **Cold start** (the folder removed): the menus, then a10 to the settled
   cryo room: the statistics line's `compiled` grows and settles, `cached`
   0, `failed` 0, `skipped no shader` and `no layout` 0 after the first
   frames of each scene; the compile milliseconds total beside phase 0's
   per-shader figures (the largest vertex shader 146 ms, a typical pixel
   shader 8.5 ms); the files in the folder counted and sized; the frame
   rate in the settled room beside phase 3's (121.9 at the limit).
4. **Second start**: `compiled` 0, `cached` the same count, the shaders
   found in under a millisecond each (the self-test's figure, and the first
   frame's time in the log).
5. **A broken file**: one `.dxbc` truncated by the agent, one with a byte
   changed: each reported, deleted and remade on the next start; the
   run unaffected.
6. **Another version**: a folder `0-test` with a `.dxbc` in it is removed
   at start; one with a stray file in it is left, logged.
7. **The debug settings**: `HALO_GPU_DEBUG_FLAT=1` once: the cache neither
   read nor written (the folder's files unchanged by date), the shaders
   compiled.
8. **The states**: the counts of layouts and state objects in a10 settle
   (tens of each; say how many) and the layer says nothing of the
   descriptions (`CreateBlendState` with a colour factor in an alpha
   slot, a `DepthBias` out of range, an unknown filter would show here);
   every type of the format table met in the corpus is listed with the
   count of layouts using it, and the unfed-input shaders counted (item 3).
9. **The debug layer** on through 3 and 4: no message; the live-object
   report at exit names the device's own objects only.
10. **The failure paths**, read in the audit: no `LOCALAPPDATA`; a folder
    that cannot be made; a write that fails (the `.tmp` left, logged once);
    a compile that fails (the draw skipped, the HLSL in `failed\`); a
    layout that fails.
11. **`halo.exe`** unchanged: one run of the menus for its line.

### Acceptance

- The four self-tests `ok` on both drivers, with and without the layer.
- A cold start compiles everything once, with the totals in "Progress"; a
  second start compiles nothing; a broken file is replaced, not fatal; the
  debug settings bypass the cache.
- No draw stays `skipped no shader` or `no layout` after a scene's first
  frames; no failed compile in the corpus; the layouts and the state
  objects counted and stable; no debug layer message; no crash; the frame
  rate beside phase 3's.
- Nothing under `port/linux/src` changed; the symbol difference the same
  three; no GL call in the device.
- The code audited: the translation tables against `apply_raster_state`,
  `configure_sampler`, `sampler_parameters`, `address_mode`,
  `blend_equation` and `stencil_operation` entry by entry and against
  `xdk_pdb.h`'s values; the format table against `attribute_format` and
  `vertex_type_bytes`; the service against the Direct3D 11 documentation
  (`D3DCompile`'s flags, `CreateInputLayout`'s rules on the signature,
  `D3D11_BLEND_DESC`'s rules on alpha factors, `D3D11_SAMPLER_DESC`'s on
  the filter and anisotropy, `D3D11_RASTERIZER_DESC`'s on the bias) and the
  Win32 documentation for the files (`MoveFileExA`, `FindFirstFileA`);
  then committed on `d3d11-backend`.

### Standing decisions (no user step)

Phase 4's, and: **the cache's folder may be removed and its files damaged
by the agent** (it is the renderer's own, under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, not game
data); **the compile stays synchronous** whatever the totals are, and the
numbers go to "Progress" and the Decisions row's last sentence for the user
to read later; **a draw a shader fails for is skipped and recorded**, never
drawn with another shader.

## Phase 6 — draws, textures and render targets

The game drawn through Direct3D 11: at the end, the menus and the maps look
as they do under OpenGL, on the Adreno and on WARP. The largest phase, the
equivalent of most of `d3d8_gl.c`, worked in six steps each tried here
before the next, compared with `halo.exe` by screenshot (the agent reads
both pictures), with the debug layer on. The Vulkan plan's phase 6
(`port/android/VULKAN.md`, "Phase 6 — draws, textures and render targets",
and under "Progress" its audit, its summary and its step entries) is the
model and its "Pitfalls already known" the list of traps; the deko3d
plan's phase 6 "Progress" (`port/switch/DEKO3D.md`) is the list of what
went wrong first. What differs here is Direct3D 11: no render passes, no
layouts, no barriers, no descriptor sets, no dummies (an unbound texture
reads zero), a queue that keeps its state, textures updated in place and
mips made by the device, and one place Direct3D 11 is stricter, a resource
bound as a target and as a texture at once.

Written to be picked up by an agent that has not seen the work so far, and
**to be run without the user** ("Standing decisions"). The line numbers
are from `d3d11-backend` at `d211e09a`; `d3d8_gl.c` is still at
`2765d816`, `xbox_textures.c` at `1bc58494`, `xgpu_post.c`, `hud_hires.c`,
`text_hires.c` and `menu_files.c` at the tree's head. Read the files, not
the numbers, if they have moved.

### What this phase is, and is not

- **It is** the draw: at every draw the device fills one record (the
  shaders, the layout and the state objects phase 5 made, the dynamic
  values, the streams in the rings and the segments phase 3 wrote, the
  textures, the constant buffers) and the backend binds what changed and
  draws (`win32_d3d11_draw.c`); the texture cache as a copy
  (`xbox_textures_d3d11.c`, the decoders and the cache kept, the GL calls
  replaced by the backend's: `win32_d3d11_textures.c`); the high-res HUD,
  the text atlas and the menus' art through the accessors the shared
  files have, so the GL stub count is 0; render targets sampled as
  textures, the mip composite, multisampling and supersampling, the
  shadow maps' scale, the anti-aliasing passes (`win32_d3d11_post.c`);
  the visibility tests as occlusion queries. And "Step 0": the audits'
  findings.
- **It is not** performance (phase 7 measures; nothing here is tuned past
  what keeps the layer silent and the counts right), the compile thread
  (the Decisions row stands), one executable (phase 8), or any change to
  how `halo.exe` draws.
- **Under `port/linux/src`, one change**: the read-only accessors the
  deko3d and Vulkan renderers added behind `#if defined(HALO_SWITCH) ||
  defined(HALO_ANDROID)` (`hud_hires_png_pixels`, `text_hires_atlas_rows`,
  `menu_art_name`, `menu_art_serial`, `menu_art_png`, and `art_serial`'s
  two increments) get `|| defined(_WIN32)`: the kind of change "Decisions"
  allows, and the one the Vulkan plan's step 3 made. **`halo.exe` changes
  by exactly those functions** (the shared objects are the same in both
  executables): the proof is below ("Testing", 1). Nothing else under
  `port/linux/src`, `source/` or `port/windows/src` changes; `d3d8_gl.c`,
  `xbox_textures.c` and `xgpu_post.c` never.
- **No new setting.** `display.anti_aliasing`, `display.resolution_scaling`,
  `display.shadow_resolution`, `display.high_res_hud`, `display.high_res_text`,
  `display.menus`, `display.per_pixel_lighting`, `debug.texture_log`,
  `debug.texture_no_cache`, `debug.texture_dump_directory`,
  `debug.screenshot_*`, `debug.gpu_trace_frame` and `debug.gpu_stats` mean
  here what they mean in `halo.exe`.

### Files

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `port/windows/d3d11/d3d8_d3d11.c` | `draw_note` grown into the draw: the record, the uniforms, the streams' binds, the attribute slot, the primitives' indices, the dynamic values, the textures through the copy; `bind_targets` with multisampling; the mip composite's bookkeeping; `halo_screen_anti_alias`; the visibility tests; `Present` calling the cache's frame; the statistics |
| `port/windows/d3d11/xbox_textures_d3d11.c`, `xbox_textures_d3d11.h` | `xbox_textures.c` (1,111 lines, at `1bc58494`) with the GL replaced: the formats, the decoders, the Custom Edition channel orders and the cache kept; textures made and written through the backend; the channel order in the decode; the three high-res replacements made here from the accessors. It replaces `xbox_textures.c` in the Direct3D executable's objects (`tools/windows_build.py`); the header declares what the device calls (`xgpu_d3d11_texture_get`, `xgpu_d3d11_texture_cache_begin_frame`) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_draw.c` | the backend's draw: the record bound and drawn, the state shadowed, the attribute slot's buffer, the constant buffers, the self-sampling copy, the queries |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_textures.c` | the backend's textures: made, written, their mips generated, freed; the shader resource views; the mip composites' textures |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11_post.c` | the anti-aliasing passes: FXAA and SMAA as HLSL, their textures and draws (a backend unit, not the Files table's `xgpu_post_d3d11.c`: every piece of a pass is a Direct3D object, and nothing in it is the Xbox SDK's; the device inflates SMAA's lookup textures, which need the port's zlib, and hands the texels over) |
| `port/windows/d3d11/win32_d3d11.c`, `win32_d3d11.h` | the targets with samples and the resolve; `destroy`'s order grown |

### Step 0: phase 5's audit, applied

Before the phase's own work, in a commit of its own, the code findings of
the audits of phases 4 and 5 ("Progress", "Phase 5 — audit"; the phase 4
audit's finding, the key's high-res flags, needs the texture cache and is
step 2's, item 7 below):

- **The attribute slot's elements** (`win32_d3d11_shaders.c`
  `d3d11_backend_layout_make`, 955–1030): the format from the input's
  component type in the signature (`input_signature`, 725–763: the `ISGN`
  entry's word at byte 12, `D3D_REGISTER_COMPONENT_UINT32` 1 →
  `R32G32B32A32_UINT`, `SINT32` 2 → `R32G32B32A32_SINT`, `FLOAT32` 3 →
  `R32G32B32A32_FLOAT`), so a packed or integer input reads its bits
  as the shader declares them (the layer's `CREATEINPUTLAYOUT_TYPE_MISMATCH`
  otherwise); **and the class `D3D11_INPUT_PER_INSTANCE_DATA` with
  `InstanceDataStepRate` 1**, not per-vertex data at a stride of 0: a
  non-instanced draw is one instance, so every vertex reads element 0 of
  the slot's buffer, which is what Direct3D 11 documents (a stride of 0
  on per-vertex data is not promised anywhere). The self-test's layout
  with an input left out proves the class compiles and draws (its case
  draws nothing; the memory self-test's pipeline can draw one triangle
  with an input from the slot, if the worker wants the proof before step
  1).
- **The four small ones in the service**: `load_compiler` tried once and
  its absence logged once; `cache_read`'s `size` initialised before
  `GetFileSizeEx` can fail; a `.tmp` left by a failed write in this
  version's folder removed at the next start's listing; a layout that is
  not made logged once a shader's hash, not once in all.

### The draw: what the device hands the backend

One self-contained record a draw, as the Vulkan plan's `VK_COMMAND_DRAW`
(its "The draw record"), in fixed-width fields the backend copies during
the call; the backend keeps what it last bound and sets only what differs
(`gl_state`'s rule, `d3d8_gl.c` 739–760, moved to the backend: the device
does not know what is bound). `struct d3d11_backend_draw`
(`d3d11_backend.h`):

- **The objects**: `vertex_shader`, `pixel_shader`, `layout`, `blend`,
  `depth_stencil`, `rasterizer` (phase 5's handles, from `device.draw`);
  `samplers[4]` (0 for a stage without a texture).
- **The dynamic values**: `blend_factor[4]` (`BLENDCOLOR` by
  `color_to_vec4`), `stencil_reference` (`STENCILREF`); the viewport
  `x, y, width, height` in the targets' pixels (`apply_raster_state`
  3155–3159: `target_pixel` of the corners; row 0 at the top, as the
  clears' rectangles, so no flip) and `min_depth`, `max_depth`
  (`MinZ`, `MaxZ`, 3178–3184); the scissor is the viewport
  (3161–3177: the NV2A's default, what keeps split screen apart), so the
  backend sets `RSSetScissorRects` from the same four numbers, and
  nothing is drawn when the width or height is 0.
- **Vertex input**: for each of the sixteen slots, `kind` (none, the
  stream ring, a segment), the segment's handle, the byte offset in it
  and the stride (`stream_data`'s `stream_buffers`, `stream_offsets` and
  `device.streams[].stride`, 2048–2102, now kept instead of dropped);
  the attribute slot's sixteen values (`device.attributes`, 256 bytes:
  the backend writes its buffer when they differ from what it last
  wrote, the deko3d plan's trap: never once a frame).
- **Indices**: `kind` (none, the index ring, a segment), the byte offset,
  `index_count`, `base_vertex` (an `int32_t`), and for a plain draw
  `vertex_count`; the topology (below).
- **Textures**: for each stage, `kind` (none, a texture of the cache, a
  render target, a mip composite) and the handle; a stage of kind none is
  bound as no view (Direct3D 11 reads zero from an unbound resource, and
  the layer says nothing: no dummies).
- **The visibility test** the draw is inside, if any: `query_slot` (below).

The constant buffers do not travel in the record: the device writes them
through `d3d11_backend_constants_write(which, data, size)` when its
serials say they changed (below), and the backend binds the same three
buffers at every draw (`VSSetConstantBuffers(0, 2)`, `PSSetConstantBuffers(0, 2)`).

### The device: the draw (`d3d8_d3d11.c`)

`prepare_draw` (`d3d8_gl.c` 3414–3690) comes back whole in `draw_note`'s
place, in its order, with the backend's calls where the GL was:

1. **The order.** The guards (`draw_ready`, 3720–3747), the key and the
   textures (`pixel_key_make`, now through the cache: item 7), **the
   targets after the textures** (`bind_targets`, with multisampling, item
   9), the state objects (`draw_states`), the shaders, the constant
   buffers, the record, then the data (`stream_data`) and the backend's
   draw. `draw_flush` (3380–3390, Mesa's Intel workaround) does not come.
2. **The vertex constants**: one `DYNAMIC` constant buffer of
   `HLSL_VERTEX_CONSTANTS_SIZE` (3,072 bytes) for every program, written
   whole with `Map` `DISCARD` when `constants_serial` differs from the
   serial at its last write (`constants_store`, 991–1009, keeps the
   serials as now). `d3d8_gl.c`'s ranges, log and checkpoint (3520–3565)
   were for one program's uniform array among many; with one buffer
   shared by every program they have nothing to save, and a `DISCARD`
   must write the whole buffer anyway. Recorded as the difference.
   Phase 3's figure stands beside: a skinned model changes up to 132
   registers a draw, so most of a10's draws write the 3 KB (about 2 MB a
   frame).
3. **The model lights** (3566–3588): the lit pixel shaders' `b1`
   (`HLSL_MODEL_LIGHTS_SIZE`, 192 bytes) from `model_light_register`
   (3409–3412), written when any of the twelve registers' serials passed
   the serial at its last write, and only for a draw whose key is lit.
4. **The other uniforms** (3592–3690): `draw_uniform_inputs` compared as
   there (the viewport's scale and offset, the texture scales, the
   combiner constants, fog, the alpha reference, `UI_OFFSET`, the bump
   matrices and luminance; `POINTSIZE` and `MIPMAPLODBIAS` out: the HLSL
   has no `point_size`, and the bias is the sampler's), **and
   `BLENDCOLOR` in** (item 6). When they differ: `struct
   hlsl_vertex_parameters` (the scale, the offset, `screen.x =
   UI_OFFSET`) and `struct hlsl_pixel_parameters` (the rest, converted
   as 3631–3666 convert them, `alpha_reference.x`, and `blend_constant`)
   written with `DISCARD`, each when its own inputs changed.
5. **The streams and the layout**: `stream_data` keeps what it works out
   (the mirror's segment and offset, or the ring's offset, per stream
   with data) into the record, with the stride; the layout is phase 5's
   (`draw_layout`, 2813–2858). **The attribute slot**: `device.attributes`
   into the record, with zeros for a register in the declaration's
   `packed_mask` (`setup_streams` 4310–4314: GL gives a packed attribute
   the integer zero, `state_attribute_value(index, NULL)`). Immediate
   mode (`End`, 3844–3869): the ring's offset from `stream_upload` into
   slot 0 with the stride `XGPU_VERTEX_ATTRIBUTE_COUNT * 16`, the
   variant's immediate layout.
6. **Primitives** (`primitive_mode` 4317–4332, `quad_indices`
   4334–4356): `POINTLIST`, `LINELIST`, `LINESTRIP`, `TRIANGLELIST`,
   `TRIANGLESTRIP` and `QUADSTRIP` (a strip) are Direct3D 11's topologies.
   **Direct3D 11 has no fan, no loop and no quads**: `QUADLIST` is indexed
   triangles from `quad_indices` as now; `TRIANGLEFAN` and `POLYGON` are
   indexed triangles `(0, i, i + 1)` (or, for an indexed draw,
   `(i0, ii, ii+1)`) from a `fan_indices` beside it; `LINELOOP` is an
   indexed line strip with the first index again at its end. The made
   indices go through `index_upload` as the quads' do, and the indexed
   draw's `base_vertex` is `-minimum` as `glDrawElementsBaseVertex`'s was
   (4421, 4448). The lens flares (`rasterizer_xbox_lights.c`), the widgets,
   the motion sensor, text and the screen effects draw fans.
   `base_vertex_index` (`SetIndices`) is added where `d3d8_gl.c` adds it
   (4416).
7. **The key's high-res flags from the cache** (phase 4's audit): with
   the texture cache back, `pixel_key_make` takes stage 0's
   `coverage_alpha` and `point_threshold` and each stage's `hires` and
   `mipmapped` from the description `xgpu_d3d11_texture_get` answers, as
   `bind_textures` does (3080–3086), and `key_textures`' own
   `hud_hires_override_find` lookup (2223–2273) goes: the cache knows
   when an override has no texture, when the entry's size is 0, and the
   text atlas's and the menu art's precedence (deviation 13 of phase 4).
   The key of a corpus file does not change for it (the audit found none
   differing); the dump's manifest is the same.
8. **The constant's alpha** (phase 5's finding). The transparent meter
   shader (`source/rasterizer/xbox/rasterizer_xbox_transparent_geometry.c`
   3379–3387) blends with `SRCBLEND` `CONSTANTALPHA` and `DESTBLEND`
   `CONSTANTCOLOR` (the colour channels only: `COLORWRITEENABLE` without
   alpha, 3375), the one such pair in the game; GL reads the constant's
   alpha in every channel for the source and the constant's colour for
   the destination, and Direct3D 11 has one blend factor for both. **The
   source's factor goes into the pixel shader**: when `SRCBLEND` is
   `CONSTANTALPHA` or `INVCONSTANTALPHA` and `DESTBLEND` is
   `CONSTANTCOLOR` or `INVCONSTANTCOLOR`, the pixel entry's key carries a
   flag beside the key (`struct pixel_entry`, 2305–2315, gains
   `by_constant_alpha`, 0, 1 or 2 for the inverse, hashed into the
   identity and compared in the table with the key); the generator
   (`nv2a_hlsl_pixel_shader` takes it as a parameter) multiplies the
   final colour, all four channels, by `blend_constant.w` or `1.0 -
   blend_constant.w` **after the alpha test and before the return** (the
   alpha test reads the shader's alpha, blending reads the product); the
   blend description's source factors become `ONE`, the destination's
   stay `BLEND_FACTOR`/`INV_BLEND_FACTOR` with the factor the constant
   colour: `src.rgb * Fa + dst.rgb * Fc` and `src.a * Fa + dst.a * Fa`,
   GL's result in every channel. `blend_constant` is a new `float4` of
   `pixel_parameters` (`packoffset(c33)`, the structure 544 bytes, the
   asserts with it) filled from `BLENDCOLOR` by `color_to_vec4`;
   `HLSL_SHADER_GENERATOR_VERSION` becomes 2 (the cache's folder with
   it). The dump's manifest marks the flag (`constant_alpha=1|2`), the
   GLSL twin is the unflagged text, and `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`
   gains the dialect's item 11: with the mark, the HLSL has the one
   multiply line the GLSL lacks, and nothing else. The other way round
   (a source constant colour with a destination constant alpha) is not
   in the game: `blend_description`'s once-logged line stays for it and
   the factor is the constant's colour. The flash's `INVCONSTANTCOLOR`
   with `INVDESTCOLOR`, `MIN` and `MAX` (`rasterizer_xbox_screen_effect.c`
   1270–1330) needs nothing: both sides read the constant's colour.
9. **`alpha_test_samples`** (3469–3473): the target's samples when they are
   more than 1, the alpha test is on and blending off (the key's `ps_4_1`
   shader with `SV_Coverage`, phase 4's item 9), from `bind_targets`
   (item 9 below). `count_samples` stays 0.
10. **The record and the draw**: `d3d11_backend_draw(&record)` where
    `glDrawArrays` and `glDrawElementsBaseVertex` were (4385–4393,
    4421–4449; `End`'s 4559); 1 drawn, 0 skipped with the reason the
    statistics count (no target, lost).
11. **`trace_draw`** (3701–3786, `debug.gpu_trace_frame`) comes back as it
    is, with `render_target_find` for `rt`.

### The textures: `xbox_textures_d3d11.c`

A copy of `xbox_textures.c` with the GL replaced and the Android branches
dropped, in the Vulkan plan's shape (its "Textures" and step 2's entry):

1. **The exports.** `xgpu_texture_describe`, `xgpu_texture_face_size`,
   `xgpu_texture_level_offset`, `xgpu_texture_level_pitch`,
   `halo_custom_edition_texels_channels` and `_forget` keep their names:
   `d3d8_resources.c` (57–485) and `port/linux/game/custom_edition_bitmaps.c`
   (670, 706) call them, and the copy's object is the one they link.
   `xgpu_texture_get` and `xgpu_texture_cache_begin_frame`, called by
   `d3d8_gl.c` alone, become `xgpu_d3d11_texture_get(const DWORD
   *resource, const D3DCOLOR *palette, struct xgpu_d3d11_texture *result)`
   (the backend's handle, its kind, the description) and
   `xgpu_d3d11_texture_cache_begin_frame`, declared in
   `xbox_textures_d3d11.h` (Xbox SDK side). The copy's own `llvm-nm`
   difference against `xbox_textures.o` is exactly those two names
   against the two new ones ("Testing", 1).
2. **The formats.** BC1, BC2 and BC3 (`_texel_dxt1`, `_dxt3`, `_dxt5`) go
   as they are (`DXGI_FORMAT_BC1_UNORM`, `BC2_UNORM`, `BC3_UNORM`; feature
   level 11_0 requires them for 2D, cube and 3D textures: the Vulkan
   audit's item 3 does not apply), the row pitch `level_bytes / block
   rows`; every other format is decoded to `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` by
   `decode_level` as now (the converted words are BGRA bytes, as
   `GL_BGRA` read them). Palettes, linear textures (with
   `texture_scale`), cube maps (six faces, `MISC_TEXTURECUBE`,
   subresource `face * levels + level`) and volumes (`CreateTexture3D`),
   every mip level, as `upload` (700–800) walks them.
3. **The channel order in the decode** (the Decisions row): where `upload`
   set `GL_TEXTURE_SWIZZLE_*` from `custom_edition_channel_sources`
   (716–746), the copy reorders the converted texels' channels as it
   converts; a BC texture whose order is not the Xbox's is decoded
   (`dxt_decode_level`, 474–548, kept outside the `HALO_ANDROID` guard at 426)
   and reordered. The Custom Edition caches' maps are where this shows
   (`docs/custom_edition_caches.md`).
4. **Made and written through the backend**: `d3d11_backend_texture_create`
   with the kind, the format, the size, the levels (the `DEFAULT` usage,
   `SHADER_RESOURCE`; a shader resource view over every level); each
   level and face written with `d3d11_backend_texture_write` (an
   `UpdateSubresource` with the row and slice pitch: the texels are
   copied during the call, so they are the game's as they were then);
   an entry whose pages the memory watch says changed is written again
   into the same texture (the description does not change for an entry:
   its key is the format and size words); a dropped entry
   (`xgpu_texture_cache_begin_frame`, 1081–1111) frees its texture
   (`d3d11_backend_texture_free`: the runtime keeps a resource alive
   while queued draws read it, so the release is at once and the handle
   is reused). `debug.texture_log`, `debug.texture_no_cache` and the
   recent-lookup cache as now; `debug.texture_dump_directory` through
   `d3d11_backend_texture_read` (a staging copy of level 0, the TGA as
   now).
5. **The memory watch** runs since phase 1 (`backend_initialize`, 935);
   `memory_watch_protect` and `memory_watch_generation` are called as
   now.
6. **The three replacements**, made here from the accessors as the
   Vulkan plan's step 3 made them, where `texture_entry_result`
   (871–926) returned GL names it got from the shared files: the text
   atlas (`text_hires_atlas_rows`: a 2048-square `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` texture
   of one level, white with the coverage as alpha, the rows written since
   the last call updated with a box), a menu's art (`menu_art_name`,
   `menu_art_png`, `menu_art_serial`: decoded with `hud_hires_png_pixels`,
   `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` with every level, the levels made by
   `d3d11_backend_texture_generate_mips` (`GenerateMips`: the texture
   made with `RENDER_TARGET` and `MISC_GENERATE_MIPS` for it), kept by
   name until the serial moves), a high-res HUD bitmap (the override of
   `hud_hires_override_find`, its PNG from `hud_hires_embedded[asset]`
   through `hud_hires_png_pixels`, kept by asset). The PNGs are RGBA with
   red first, which `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` reads: no swap. `xbox_textures.c`'s
   calls of `hud_hires_override_texture` (916, 1043), `menu_art_texture`
   (899, 904) and `text_hires_atlas_texture` (878) go: each would reach
   the GL stubs and answer 0.
7. **Samplers** are phase 5's (`sampler_inputs`, `sampler_description`,
   3101–3162), with `hires` and `mipmapped` from the cache's description
   now (item 7 above).
8. **A texture whose data is a render target's** (`bind_textures`
   3047–3068): the target's handle in the record (kind: target), its
   linear scale from the target's size, levels 1, **or the mip composite**
   when `bind_textures`' test holds (not linear, not a cube, more than one
   level, the top level the size of the target drawn there): item 10
   below.

### Render targets, multisampling and the passes

1. **`bind_targets`** (`d3d8_gl.c` 1432–1481) comes back whole: the
   `samples` of `display.anti_aliasing`'s `msaa` values
   (`anti_aliasing_samples`, at most what `CheckMultisampleQualityLevels`
   gives for `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` and `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`, asked once and
   logged as `anti_aliasing_prepare` logs the GL's maximum, 1627–1649),
   multisampled where either target is a screen buffer or is multisampled
   already (1456–1461); `render_target_multisample` (1357–1410) as
   `d3d11_backend_target_multisample(handle, samples)`: the backend keeps,
   beside the target's texture, a multisampled texture of the same size
   and format with its own view (`SampleDesc.Count`, made at the first
   ask and remade for other samples), resolves it into the texture first
   (`ResolveSubresource`; a depth target cannot be resolved: it is
   cleared, the Android branch's choice, 1385–1397, since nothing samples
   a depth buffer), and copies the texture into the new storage for
   colour (a full-target draw with the present's shader: Direct3D 11 has
   no blit into a multisampled target); `render_target_resolve`
   (1333–1355) as `d3d11_backend_target_resolve(handle)` before a target
   is sampled, read back or presented (`unresolved` kept by the device as
   now). `target_samples` for the key. **The multisampled rasterizer
   state**: `MultisampleEnable` true in the rasterizer description when
   the bound targets have samples (phase 5 left it false; the layer does
   not demand it, but line rasterization follows it), so the description
   gains the draw's `samples > 1` as an input.
2. **The viewport is the record's** (above); `d3d11_backend_targets_set`
   keeps binding the whole target's viewport for the clears.
3. **A target bound and sampled at once.** GL let the back buffer be
   sampled while drawn into (`prepare_draw`'s comment, 3447–3451);
   Direct3D 11 unbinds the view and the layer warns
   (`PSSETSHADERRESOURCES_HAZARD`, `OMSETRENDERTARGETS_HAZARD`). The
   backend's rule, in `d3d11_backend_draw`: a stage whose target is the
   bound colour target samples **a copy** (`CopyResource` into a scratch
   texture of the target's size and format, one kept a size, counted in
   the statistics as `self-sampling copies`); and at
   `d3d11_backend_targets_set`, any shader resource slot holding a view
   of the target about to be bound is set to none first (the backend
   knows what it bound). The present already unbinds its slot (phase 2).
4. **The mip composite** (`mip_composite_get`, 2933–3020): a
   `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` texture with every level
   (`d3d11_backend_composite_create(width, height, levels)`); the levels
   the game drew copied from their targets (`CopySubresourceRegion`,
   level to level, the sizes equal, after each target's resolve), the
   levels below the last drawn made by `GenerateMips` on a view whose
   `MostDetailedMip` is the last drawn level (`d3d11_backend_composite_update(handle, targets[], count)`),
   copied again only when a level's target was written since
   (`level_sources`, `level_written`, as now: `written` is kept by
   `bind_targets`). The composite's view is the stage's. The water's
   ripples (`rasterizer_xbox_water.c`) are where this shows; b30's beach
   has them in view at its start.
5. **Supersampling** (`ssaa2x`, `screen_mode_choose` 188–240):
   `maximum_target_size` is `D3D11_REQ_TEXTURE2D_U_OR_V_DIMENSION`
   (16,384 at feature level 11_0; the device's copy at 181 has it 0, so
   the factor is never applied until it is set in `backend_initialize`);
   the present's linear blit scales it down as now. **The shadow maps'
   scale** (`display.shadow_resolution`, 262–300) is phase 2's
   `render_target_get` already; the maps are drawn into targets at that
   scale by the draws now, and the blur's quads with them.
6. **The anti-aliasing passes** (`xgpu_post.c`, `halo_screen_anti_alias`
   4696–4720): in `win32_d3d11_post.c`, called through
   `d3d11_backend_anti_alias(smaa, target, x0, y0, x1, y1)` with the
   back buffer's handle (resolved and no longer multisampled, as there)
   and the corners in its pixels, and prepared through
   `d3d11_backend_anti_alias_prepare(smaa, area_texels, search_texels)`
   where `anti_aliasing_prepare` prepared the programs (the device
   inflates SMAA's lookup textures with the port's zlib as
   `lookup_texture` does, 364–391, and hands the bytes: the Windows SDK
   units have no zlib on their path). The passes as `xgpu_post_anti_alias`
   (467–524) runs them: the target's pixels copied into the pass's
   colour texture (`CopySubresourceRegion` of the rectangle), one
   full-rectangle triangle a pass (the present's vertex shader's shape,
   `SV_VertexID`), the viewport the rectangle, the colour write mask
   RGB for the last pass (the game keeps values of its own in
   destination alpha), the intermediates cleared whole; FXAA as HLSL
   (`fxaa_source`, 96–192, by the dialect: `SV_Position` for
   `gl_FragCoord`, `SampleLevel` for `textureLod`, `discard`), SMAA from
   the embedded `SMAA.hlsl` (`xgpu_smaa_shader`, declared by hand as the
   `extern` it is) with `#define SMAA_HLSL_4 1`, `SMAA_PRESET_HIGH`, and
   `SMAA_RT_METRICS` a `cbuffer` member as the GL version makes it (its
   `LinearSampler` and `PointSampler` are its own declarations, 541–542:
   their registers from `D3DReflect`'s `GetResourceBindingDescByName`,
   the DLL's other export, not assumed from their order), the three
   mains as `smaa_mains` (194–237) with the vertex functions' offsets
   found per pixel. A pass that does not build is logged once and the
   view is not antialiased, as now.
7. **Split screen** needs no new code (the scissor follows the viewport,
   the clears are clipped to it: phase 2) and a second controller (the
   user's).
8. **The screenshot** (`write_screenshot`) reads the back buffer after
   its resolve.

### Visibility tests

`D3D11_QUERY_OCCLUSION` (a 64-bit count of samples passed; exact at
feature level 11_0), in the game's 4,096 slots plus one scratch, as
`d3d8_gl.c`'s desktop path (`BeginVisibilityTest` 2073–2094: `Begin` on
the scratch; `EndVisibilityTest` 2096–2149: `End`, the scratch swapped
into the slot, `query_area` from `target_scale` and `target_samples`,
`query_pending`, `visibility_unread`; `GetVisibilityTestResult`
2170–2231: the latest count known, from this test or an earlier one
while the GPU is behind, **never a wait**: `GetData` with
`D3D11_ASYNC_GETDATA_DONOTFLUSH`, `S_FALSE` leaves the slot's earlier
count standing; `visibility_unscaled`, 2151–2159, divides by the area so
the count is in the game's pixels, which the game divides by its own
test's area: lens flares too bright otherwise). A query spans target
changes in Direct3D 11 (no render pass), so the Vulkan plan's split is not
needed. Phase 0's finding stands: WARP answers only after a flush, and
`Present` flushes every frame, so a result is one frame late there. The
backend: `d3d11_backend_query_begin(slot)`, `_end(slot)`,
`_result(slot, uint64_t *samples)` (1 ready, 0 not yet, as `GetData`
says), the queries made at the first use and kept.

### The backend (`win32_d3d11_draw.c`, `win32_d3d11_textures.c`, `win32_d3d11_post.c`, `d3d11_backend.h`)

- `int d3d11_backend_draw(const struct d3d11_backend_draw *draw)`: the
  shadowed binds (`IASetInputLayout`, `IASetVertexBuffers` for the slots
  that changed with the ring's or the segment's buffer, `IASetIndexBuffer`
  (`R16_UINT`), `IASetPrimitiveTopology`, `VSSetShader`, `PSSetShader`,
  the three constant buffers, `PSSetShaderResources(0, 4)` and
  `PSSetSamplers(0, 4)` for the stages that changed, `OMSetBlendState`
  with the factor and sample mask all, `OMSetDepthStencilState` with
  the reference, `RSSetState`, `RSSetViewports`, `RSSetScissorRects`),
  the attribute slot's buffer written when its 256 bytes differ (a
  `DYNAMIC` buffer, `Map` `DISCARD`), the self-sampling copy, then
  `Draw(vertex_count, 0)` or `DrawIndexed(index_count, index_offset / 2,
  base_vertex)`. The shadow is cleared by anything that binds behind it
  (the clears, the present, the passes, the resolves set what they use,
  so the shadow is reset at their end: `d3d11_backend_state_forget`, the
  backend's `xgpu_gl_state_invalidate`). 0 and a reason when nothing is
  bound or the device is lost.
- `void d3d11_backend_constants_write(uint32_t which, const void *data,
  uint32_t size)`: the vertex constants, the vertex parameters, the
  pixel parameters, the model lights (four `DYNAMIC` constant buffers of
  the header's sizes, `Map` `DISCARD`, the bytes copied during the call).
- `uint32_t d3d11_backend_texture_create(const struct
  d3d11_backend_texture *description)` (kind 2D, 3D or cube; format
  BGRA8, RGBA8, BC1, BC2, BC3 as numbers the backend checks; width,
  height, depth, levels; a flag for `GenerateMips`), the handle from 1,
  reused after `d3d11_backend_texture_free(handle)`; `int
  d3d11_backend_texture_write(handle, face, level, const void *texels,
  uint32_t row_pitch, uint32_t slice_pitch, const uint32_t *box)` (the
  whole subresource, or `box` as left, top, right, bottom for the
  atlas's rows); `void d3d11_backend_texture_generate_mips(handle)`;
  `int d3d11_backend_texture_read(handle, pixels, size)` (level 0, for
  the dump); `uint32_t d3d11_backend_textures_alive(void)` and the bytes
  they hold, for the statistics.
- `uint32_t d3d11_backend_composite_create(width, height, levels)`,
  `int d3d11_backend_composite_update(handle, const uint32_t *targets,
  uint32_t count)`.
- `void d3d11_backend_target_multisample(handle, samples)`, `void
  d3d11_backend_target_resolve(handle)`, `int
  d3d11_backend_multisample_levels(samples)` (what
  `CheckMultisampleQualityLevels` allows for both formats).
- `int d3d11_backend_anti_alias_prepare(int smaa, const void *area, const
  void *search)`, `int d3d11_backend_anti_alias(int smaa, uint32_t target,
  const int32_t corners[4])`.
- `void d3d11_backend_query_begin(uint32_t slot)`, `void
  d3d11_backend_query_end(uint32_t slot)`, `int d3d11_backend_query_result(uint32_t
  slot, uint64_t *samples)`.
- `void d3d11_backend_draw_statistics(uint32_t counts[])`: draws, the
  binds skipped by the shadow, self-sampling copies, texture writes and
  their bytes, textures alive and their bytes, resolves, composite copies,
  queries begun.
- The units' objects released in `destroy`'s order after the shader
  service's.

### The platform layer and the settings

Nothing new. `debug.d3d11_self_test` runs the four tests as before; no
fifth (the screen is the test now). The accessors' guards under
`port/linux/src` are the one change ("What this phase is").

### Testing on this PC

As phase 5's: `python configure.py --portable --release`, `ninja windows`,
the settings from the environment, `config.toml` copied before and put
back after at its MD5, `HALO_MAX_FPS=120` in every run, the layer on for
each step's runs and off for one last run of each. **Screenshots are the
evidence**, and they do not need the screen: `HALO_SCREENSHOT_DIR` and
`HALO_SCREENSHOT_EVERY` write the back buffer as BMPs from both
executables (`write_screenshot`), a locked session notwithstanding; a
short Python script (zlib and struct, no PIL) turns a BMP into a PNG the
agent reads, and the same scene under `halo.exe` sits beside it. Scenes by
the console alone, the window behind others or minimized (the console's
`key_press <name> <frames>` presses a key at the window as if typed, so
the menus are walked without `SetForegroundWindow`, and the user keeps
the PC): the PC menus (the walk of phase 4's corpus, and its quit:
`key_press up 2`, `return`, `left`, `return`), the Xbox menus
(`HALO_MENUS=xbox`), `map_name a10` (the cinematic, the flash
as the tube opens, the cryo room: `game_speed 8` then `1`), `map_name a30`
(outdoors: the sun's lens flare, fog, the sky, grass; `cheat_all_weapons`
for the pistol's zoom through the zoom key), `map_name b30` (the beach:
the sea's water and its ripples in view at the start), `debug.test_input =
"look:<seed>"` to turn; `halo.exe` the same way for the pictures beside
(it runs at a few frames a second in a10's cryo room on this PC: match by
`game_time`, as phase 3 did).

1. **The object check**: `git diff --name-only d943c612..HEAD -- port/linux/src
   source port/windows/src tools` lists `port_config.c`,
   `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`, `tools/windows_build.py` and the six
   accessor files, nothing else. **`halo.exe` changes by the accessors
   alone**: for each of `hud_hires.o`, `text_hires.o`, `menu_files.o`,
   `llvm-nm -g --defined-only` before and after the guards differs by
   exactly the accessors' names, and `llvm-objdump -d` of
   `halo_menus_art_register` and `halo_menus_art_forget` differs by the
   increment of `art_serial` alone (the method of "Windows object
   compare" in the audit of phase 1, with `-ffile-prefix-map`, if the
   objects are compared whole); `halo.exe` runs the menus once for its
   line. **The symbol check** on `d3d8_d3d11.o`: still the three (the
   device's additions are static); on `xbox_textures_d3d11.o` against
   `xbox_textures.o`: `xgpu_texture_get` and `xgpu_texture_cache_begin_frame`
   against `xgpu_d3d11_texture_get` and `xgpu_d3d11_texture_cache_begin_frame`,
   nothing else. **The GL grep** on the device, the copies and the
   backend's units: 0; **the GL stub count** in the statistics line: 0
   from step 3 on, in every scene.
2. **Step 1, first draws**: the record, the uniforms, the streams, the
   attribute slot, the primitives, the dynamic values; a draw with a
   texture skipped and counted (`skipped: textures`) until step 2. The
   main menu's fades and solid layers on the screen; the statistics'
   draws and immediate draws made match `halo.exe`'s for the screen;
   nothing skipped but for textures; the layer silent. The picture's
   orientation and the front face are checked here on the fade and in
   step 4 on a map: the expectation is **no flip anywhere** (the vertex
   shaders are the desktop branch's, Direct3D's clip space is the one
   `glClipControl(GL_UPPER_LEFT, GL_ZERO_TO_ONE)` gave it, and
   `FrontCounterClockwise` from `D3DFRONT_CCW` is the desktop branch's
   front face in the same window space); if a map is seen from inside,
   the front face is inverted in `rasterizer_description` alone and
   "Progress" says so.
3. **Step 2, textures**: the Xbox menus look as under `halo.exe` by
   screenshot (the logo, the planet, the ring, the items); the texture
   statistics (alive, bytes, writes a frame: 0 after the first frames);
   `debug.texture_log` once; the recent-lookup cache working (the writes
   stop). Both drivers.
4. **Step 3, the high-res HUD and text, the menus' art**: the PC menus
   look as under `halo.exe` (the title, the items, the orange version
   number, the renderer's line "Direct3D 11" under it); the GL stub count
   0; the atlas's rows written only when the text changes. Both drivers.
5. **Step 4, a map**: a10 from the console: the cinematic shot for shot
   against `halo.exe` (by `game_time`), the cryo room, the marines, the
   Pelican, the hands on the console; culling and the front face (above),
   depth, stencil (the active camouflage, the shadows' stencil), z bias
   (decals on a30: bullet holes, scorch marks), fog (a30's distance),
   every vertex format met (the layouts' line), cube maps (the reflective
   surfaces), skinned characters, dynamic geometry (the mirror's volatile
   pages: particles, the contrails), per-pixel lighting
   (`HALO_PER_PIXEL_LIGHTING=1` once); **the corpus dumped again from
   play** on a10 and a30 with `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py` on it (fog,
   colour sign and alpha kill on stages 1 to 3 were never met before).
   The Adreno; WARP for a10's first 90 s only (the standing decision
   changes: see below). Nothing skipped but a shader's first compile; the
   layer silent; the frame rate in the cryo room beside phase 5's
   (122.9 at the limit, and `halo.exe`'s 3).
6. **Step 5, render targets**: the wake-up flash in a10 (white and
   blurred as the tube opens: a render target sampled), the marines'
   blob shadows (the shadow maps, at `display.shadow_resolution` 128 and
   512), the water at b30's start (the mip composite), the pistol's zoom
   on a30 (`key_press` of the zoom's key), `display.anti_aliasing` each of `fxaa`,
   `smaa`, `ssaa2x`, `msaa4x` in the cryo room by screenshot (the edges
   smoothed, the HUD sharp, the alpha-tested edges with `msaa4x`), and
   `display.resolution_scaling = "original"` once; the layer silent
   through the resolves and the copies; the self-sampling copies counted.
   Split screen is the user's.
7. **Step 6, visibility tests**: a30's sun and a10's lights: the lens
   flares show, fade as something passes in front and go when hidden, as
   under `halo.exe` (screenshots at the same `game_time`); the count
   divided by the area (a flare too bright is the suspect); no wait (the
   frame rate unchanged); WARP's one-frame lateness noted.
8. **The debug layer** on through every step's runs: no message; the
   live-object report at exit (the menus' runs) the device's own objects
   only.
9. **The failure paths, read**: a texture that is not made (the draw goes
   on with no view, counted); a composite or a pass that is not made
   (logged once, the plain target sampled, the view not antialiased); a
   query that is not made (the slot answers 0, as now); the lost device
   (every call answers 0; `draw_ready` stops the draws).

### Acceptance

- Each of the six steps done as it says, on both drivers where the
  standing decisions say, with the screenshots' findings in "Progress"
  (what matched, what differed, which adapter); the debug layer clean;
  nothing the game draws missing for longer than a shader's first
  compile; the shader check clean on the corpus from play; the GL stub
  count 0; what is left for the user listed with what to look for.
- `halo.exe` changed by the accessors alone, proven as "Testing" 1 says;
  nothing else under `port/linux/src`; the symbol differences as there;
  no GL call in the new units.
- The code audited: the draw against `prepare_draw`, `setup_streams`,
  `apply_raster_state`'s dynamic lines and the three draw entry points
  line by line; the copy against `xbox_textures.c` line by line (every
  changed line a GL call's replacement, an Android branch dropped, the
  channel order's move or the three replacements); the composite against
  `mip_composite_get`; the queries against the desktop path; the passes
  against `xgpu_post.c`; the backend against the Direct3D 11 documentation
  (`IASetVertexBuffers` and the per-instance slot, `UpdateSubresource`'s
  pitches, `CreateTexture2D`'s cube and BC rules, `ResolveSubresource`'s
  format rule, `GenerateMips`' bind flags, `CopySubresourceRegion` between
  levels, the hazard rules of `OMSetRenderTargets` and
  `PSSetShaderResources`, `GetData`'s flags, `D3D11_QUERY_OCCLUSION`);
  then committed on `d3d11-backend`.

### Standing decisions (no user step)

Phases 4 and 5's, and: **WARP runs the menus, the self-tests and a10's
first 90 s of each step** (the cinematic at its own pace: WARP is slow,
and a difference between the drivers is what it is for); **a scene the
console does not reach** (split screen, a fight) is listed for the user
with what to look for, not asked
for; **a difference from `halo.exe` seen on both drivers is the
renderer's and is fixed before the step is accepted; one seen on one
driver is recorded and the step goes on**; **the front face is inverted
in one place if a map shows it, and recorded**; **`display.anti_aliasing`'s
values that the GPU refuses** (`msaa8x` on WARP, say) are logged and run
at what it allows, as `anti_aliasing_prepare` does; **the cache's folder
changes with the generator's version** (2), and the old folder is removed
at the first start, as phase 5 made it.

## Phase 7 — the renderers compared

The point of the renderer, in two halves:

- **On this PC, three ways, the same scenes**: OpenGL (through the
  compatibility pack), Direct3D 11 on the Adreno, Direct3D 11 on WARP.
  Every map's scenes the agent can reach by keys, and the user's list:
  split screen, water, lens flares, decals, fog, the HUD's meters, the PC
  menus. A table in "Progress", one row per scene. A difference between the
  two Direct3D drivers, or between Direct3D and OpenGL where OpenGL is
  right, is the renderer's and is fixed. **And the frame rate**, the three
  scenes of phase 0's baseline, OpenGL against Direct3D 11 on the Adreno:
  the number this plan was for.
- **Testers**, with the release of phase 8: GPUs under no emulation (Intel,
  AMD, NVIDIA). The probe's report, and a screenshot of each scene under
  each executable, with `debug.txt`; each report into "Progress" with the
  GPU, the driver and what each renderer showed.

## Phase 8 — for players

- `port/windows/README.md`: the two executables, the settings, the
  shader cache's folder, how to report a problem (the probe).
- The release: `halo_d3d11.exe` in the Windows zip beside `halo.exe`; the
  updater, the crash reports (`--crash-report` starts the executable that
  crashed) and Sentry's symbols for both. The fork's CI has the Windows job
  turned off (`.github/workflows/build.yml`); turning it on, or building
  releases here, is decided then.
- **One executable or two** (Decisions): a launcher choosing by
  `display.renderer` and the Video Setup menu, as Android's chooses
  (commit `2865b4ae`); and the default on Windows on ARM (the host's
  architecture is known to the process: `IsWow64Process2`). Phase 7's
  numbers decide.

---

## How this plan is worked

What made the deko3d and Vulkan plans go well, kept here on purpose:

- **Each phase is written out in detail before it is handed over**, with its
  files, its steps, how to test it on this PC and what counts as done. The
  detailed phase is written so that an agent that has not seen the work so
  far can pick it up: it names what to read first, in what order.
- **Each phase goes to an agent with a hand-off prompt**, and comes back
  for an audit: the code against the source and the Direct3D 11
  documentation, the "Progress" entry against what was asked, before the
  commit.
- **Each step is tried on this PC before the next starts**, on the Adreno
  and on WARP. A step that is "done in the tree" and not seen on the screen
  is not done. Screenshots are the evidence: the same scene under the
  OpenGL executable and the Direct3D one, side by side.
- **Every result goes into "Progress"** with what shows it: what was seen,
  what the log said, which adapter. "Progress" is newest first.
- **Work is audited against the source, not against its own description,**
  before it is committed.
- **Diagnostics come before the feature**: the statistics line, the debug
  layer's messages, device-removed handling and screenshots are in from
  phase 2, because a silently dropped draw costs hours without them.
- **The OpenGL renderer stays untouched**, so the comparison in phase 7 is
  against something that does not move, and a player always has a fallback.
- The work is on the `d3d11-backend` branch, from `main`.

## Risks

| Risk | Settled in |
|---|---|
| The emulated x86 process gets Microsoft's software adapter, not the Adreno | Phase 0, step 2: the first finding; the plan stops there if so |
| The Xbox SDK's and the Windows SDK's headers in one renderer | The header boundary (above); phase 0, step 1 |
| `D3DCompile` at the draw hitches more than GLSL does | Phase 0, step 4 measures; phase 5's cache; the compile thread as the fallback |
| SDL's window and DXGI's swap chain disagree on fullscreen, resizing, Alt+Enter | Phase 0, step 3; phase 2 |
| The driver's own mappings crowd the 32-bit address space | Phase 0, step 5 |
| Faults copied from `d3d8_gl.c`'s logic, not the driver's | Phase 7: a fault on the Adreno and on WARP is ours |
| The GL calls shared files still make | The counted stubs, zero by the end of phase 6 |
| Two executables double the release's surface (updater, crash reports, symbols) | Phase 8 |
| The fork's Windows CI is off | Phase 8 |

---

## Progress

Newest first.

### Phase 6 — summary (steps 0 to 3; steps 4 to 6 to follow)

Worked unattended on this PC (Snapdragon X Plus, Adreno X1-45 driver 31.0.137.0, WARP 10.0.26100.9444, Windows 11 on Arm64,
the game x86 under emulation), release build, `--portable`, full LTO, PGO off (clang 20). Commits: `5406685f` ("Step 0": phase
5's audit), `c0555a04` (step 1: the draw), `5e0a5329` (step 2: the textures), `5c5ca6eb` (step 3: the high-res HUD, the text
atlas and the menus' art, the accessors' guards, the READMEs); this entry and the deviations below follow. **The menus are on
the screen through Direct3D 11 on both drivers, as `halo.exe` draws them**: the PC menus with their title, items, orange version
number and the renderer's line, the Xbox menus with the logo, the ring, the planet, the stars and their items. Settings from the
environment as phase 5's (`HALO_DISPLAY_MODE=windowed`, `HALO_WINDOW_SIZE=1280x960`, `HALO_VOLUME=0.05`, `HALO_MAX_FPS=120` in
every run, `HALO_D3D11_*`, `HALO_GPU_STATS=1`, `HALO_MENUS=xbox`, `HALO_TEXTURE_LOG=1` once, `HALO_SCREENSHOT_DIR` and
`HALO_SCREENSHOT_EVERY=600`, `HALO_GPU_TRACE`, `HALO_GPU_DEBUG_FLAT=1` and `HALO_GPU_DUMP_SHADERS` for the diagnosis in step 1);
`config.toml` copied to the scratchpad before the first run (MD5 `85841a6b...`, checked by the harness before each start) and
put back after every run, at that MD5 at the end. The game was driven through the script console alone (`key_press`, the window
behind the user's or the session locked; a locked session makes `Present` answer occluded, `0 presented, 60 not`, and the
screenshots come from the back buffer regardless). No crash, no reset, no lost device, no `d3d11 debug:` line in any run.

**The copy against the original** (`xbox_textures_d3d11.c`, 1,079 lines, against `xbox_textures.c`, 1,111, at `1bc58494`):
every differing line is a GL call, type or enumerant (54), an Android branch (19), the backend in the GL's place (52: the
texture made once at the first upload, each level and face written, the dump read back, the texture freed), the channel order
in the decode (9: `reorder_channels`, a BC texture of another order decoded for it), the header comment and the includes (18),
the three replacements (16 lines, plus step 3's functions), or C around those changes (braces, control lines, comments, 75) and
blank lines (14). The exports every build shares are untouched.

**The device against `d3d8_gl.c`**, read: `draw_prepare` is `prepare_draw` (3414–3688) in its order: the guards
(`draw_ready`), the key and the textures (`pixel_key_make`, `key_textures` as `bind_textures` decides, through the cache),
the targets after the textures (`bind_targets`, `skipped no target`), the state objects (`draw_states`, `apply_raster_state`'s
tables of phase 5 with `has_depth` from `bind_targets`), the shaders and the lit pair's fallback (3480–3496), the constants
(`constants_update`: the whole buffer when the serial moved, the lights when one of the twelve registers moved, 3516–3588),
the other uniforms (`uniforms_update`: `draw_uniform_inputs` 3592–3627 without `POINTSIZE` and `MIPMAPLODBIAS`, with
`BLENDCOLOR`; the conversions 3631–3666), then the record's dynamic values (`apply_raster_state` 3155–3184), the attribute
slot (`setup_streams` 4310–4314) and the data (`stream_data`: `setup_streams`' loops; `DrawVertices`, `DrawIndexedVertices`,
`End` 4375–4562 with the record where GL drew). `trace_draw` is 3701–3783 with `render_target_find` for `rt`.

**The backend against the documentation**, read: `IASetVertexBuffers` a slot at a time with the ring's or the segment's buffer
(both bound as vertex buffers), the attribute slot's buffer as per-instance data at step rate 1 (a non-instanced draw is one
instance, which reads element 0); `IASetIndexBuffer` at offset 0 with `DrawIndexed`'s `StartIndexLocation` the byte offset
over 2 and `BaseVertexLocation` the negative minimum (an `INT`); `Map` `DISCARD` on the four `DYNAMIC` constant buffers and the
attribute slot's; `OMSetBlendState` with the factor and the sample mask all; `OMSetDepthStencilState` with the reference;
`RSSetViewports` within [0, 1] depth and `RSSetScissorRects` the same rectangle; `PSSetShaderResources` with no view for a
stage without a texture (reads zero, no message); `UpdateSubresource` with the row pitch (`((width + 3) / 4) * block bytes`
for BC, `width * 4` else) and the slice pitch, a box for the atlas's rows; `CreateTexture2D` with `MISC_TEXTURECUBE` and
`ArraySize` 6 for a cube (subresource `level + face * levels`), `CreateTexture3D` for a volume; `GenerateMips` on a view of a
texture made with `RENDER_TARGET | SHADER_RESOURCE` and `MISC_GENERATE_MIPS`; `CopyResource` into a scratch texture of the
same size and format for a stage that samples the bound colour target, and `PSSetShaderResources` to none for a stage holding
the view of a target about to be bound (the hazard rules of `OMSetRenderTargets`); the shadow forgotten after the clears'
draws, the present and the self-tests, which bind behind it.

**Not run**: a10 beyond the diagnosis of step 1 (step 4's); the textures of a map (step 4's; the menus' 18 to 62 textures are
BC1, L8, X8R8G8B8 and the PNGs); the `debug.texture_dump_directory` read-back (read in the audit); split screen (the user's).
Not done by the agent in any case: a second GPU family.

### Phase 6: deviations from the spec (steps 0 to 3)

Everything steps 0 to 3 did differently from "Phase 6 — draws, textures and render targets", or beyond it.

**Step 0**

1. **The attribute slot's elements take the format of the signature's component type** (`D3D_REGISTER_COMPONENT_UINT32` →
   `R32G32B32A32_UINT`, `SINT32` → `_SINT`, `FLOAT32` → `_FLOAT`, the `ISGN` entry's word at 12, kept on the shader as
   `input_types`) as per-instance data at step rate 1; a layout that is not made is logged once a shader (`layout_failed_logged`
   on the shader, the service's once-in-all slot for layouts no longer used); the compiler's load tried once (`compiler.tried`);
   `cache_read`'s `size` zeroed first; the `.tmp` files of this version's folder deleted at the listing and counted in the
   cache's line (`(<n> .tmp files of failed writes removed)`).

**The draw (step 1)**

2. **The blend factor is the constant's alpha in every channel when a factor reads the constant's alpha and none the constant's
   colour** (`blend_factor_values`), which is what GL's `CONSTANT_ALPHA` read; the spec's `color_to_vec4` alone would have given
   the colour. With the meter's pair (the source's alpha into the shader, `by_constant_alpha`) the factor is the colour.
3. **The constants' log and checkpoint do not come** (`constants_store` keeps the serials only), as the section's item 2
   records the difference; the model lights' buffer has one serial for every lit shader and is written the first time whatever
   the serials say (`lights_written`).
4. **The two parameter structures are compared whole with their last written copies** (`vertex_parameters_written`,
   `pixel_parameters_written`), not by subsets of the inputs, after the inputs' comparison that `prepare_draw` makes.
5. **The pixel entry's flag** (`by_constant_alpha`) is in the entry, the recent-four and the buckets' comparison and the identity
   (hashed after the key), and the dump's manifest marks it `constant_alpha=1|2`; `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`'s item 11
   expects the one multiply line before the return (after the debug settings' lines, which bypass the cache anyway).
6. **The targets are bound only when the pair changes** (`targets_bind` keeps `bound_valid`; the present, the resize and the
   self-tests forget), since the device asks at every draw; the viewport's shadow alone is forgotten then
   (`d3d11_backend_draw_viewport_forget`).
7. **A draw whose viewport has no width or height counts as drawn** (GL drew nothing there too); a draw the backend refuses
   (a handle naming nothing, logged once a kind) is counted `not drawn` in the statistics line; `skipped no target` is counted
   by the device as `prepare_draw` counts it.
8. **The statistics line** gives the draws and the immediate draws made (counted when the backend drew, as `prepare_draw`
   counts after `program_get`), the skips by reason, the binds the shadow skipped and the self-sampling copies since the last
   line, the textures alive with their KB and the texture writes with their KB since the last line; no line of its own for the
   draw unit's counts.
9. **`win32_d3d11_textures.c` was made in step 1** with what the draw unit links (the views, the statistics, the release), and
   filled in step 2.
10. **The index buffer is bound at offset 0** and the draw's byte offset becomes `StartIndexLocation` (over 2), so a buffer
    already bound is not bound again for another offset.
11. **The self-sampling copies' scratch textures** are eight, kept by size and replaced round-robin.
12. **A fan or polygon drawn from mirrored indices is not mirrored**: its indices are made for the draw (`fan_indices`) and
    streamed, as a quad list's are; the extent cache keys on the mirror's generation as before.

**The textures (step 2)**

13. **The texture is made at the first upload** (`texture_make` in `upload`), not when the entry is made: an entry whose size
    the device refuses, or whose pages are not contiguous, has no texture, and the draw samples zero (the backend logs once).
14. **The texture's format is decided once**: BC where the texels are BC in the Xbox's channel order, else BGRA8; a Custom
    Edition order's texture is decoded (`dxt_decode_level` kept outside the Android guard, as the section says) and reordered
    by `reorder_channels` on the converted words.
15. **`D3D11CalcSubresource` is computed by hand** (`level + face * levels`): the SDK's inline is C++'s.
16. **`d3d11_backend_textures_alive(bytes)`** is a function beside the statistics, for the device.

**The replacements (step 3)**

17. **The accessors' guards** are `#if defined(HALO_SWITCH) || defined(HALO_ANDROID) || defined(_WIN32)` in the six files (seven
    places: `menu_files.c` has four). The replacements are the Vulkan copy's (`xbox_textures_vk.c`: `png_image`,
    `atlas_image`, `menu_image`, `hud_image`) with the backend's calls: the PNGs' levels made by `GenerateMips` on an RGBA8
    texture (no halving, no red-blue swap), the atlas 2048 square RGBA8 of one level with its changed rows written by a box.
18. **An override whose texture cannot be made is dropped at the upload** (`hud_texture` asked where the original asked
    `hud_hires_override_texture`), so the key's flags follow the cache's description as the phase 4 audit asked.

**Testing**

19. **The harness is Python through the console** (`harness.py` in the session's scratchpad: the game started with
    `stderr` to a file, `key_press` for the menus, `map_name` and `game_speed` for the maps, the BMPs made PNGs by `zlib`
    and `struct`), the window never brought to the front; the PC menus' quit sequence fails when another item than CAMPAIGN is
    selected (`up` then goes to it, not to QUIT), and such runs were killed.
20. **Step 1's picture could not show a draw**: the diagnosis above (the trace, the flat shader, a hack with neutral state and
    the clears' pixel shader, removed before the commit) stands in for "the main menu's fades and solid layers on the screen".
21. **The session was locked for the later runs**: `Present` answered `DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED` (`0 presented, 60 not`, the
    frames paced at 60 a second by the backend's rest), and the pictures are the back buffer's screenshots.

### The console's `key_press`, before phase 6

So that phase 6's runs need not take the PC from the user, the script console presses keys (2026-10-13):
`key_press <name> <frames>` (`source/hs/hs.c`, the port's last function, the console's and not a map's;
`halo_keyboard_press` in `port/linux/src/sdl_platform.c`, declared in `port/linux/include/halo_keyboard.h`)
sets the key in the platform's key array as a typed key is set, with `keys_pressed`, and lets it go after that
many frame reads, under the input lock. It takes every path a typed key does (the menus' controller, the
bindings, the held-over-switch rule) and not the debug keyboard's, which the developer console reads. Tried on
both executables with the window minimized and another process in front: `key_press bogus 1` answers `false`,
and `up 2`, `return 2`, `left 2`, `return 2` a second apart quit the PC main menu with exit code 0 (halo.exe
and halo_d3d11.exe). The brief and "Testing on this PC" walk the menus through the console; `halo.exe` and
the Linux and Android builds change by it (the function table has one more entry), as the README's row for
`debug.telnet_console` says.

### Phase 5 — audit

The audit (a session of its own, after the five commits) found the record true to the code and nothing
blocking. Reproduced: the commits (`7ea007f1` the service, the layouts, the states and the device's handles;
`b6a75592` the unfed layouts named; `f37aace7` the READMEs; `81ad2bf8` the layouts counted by type; `a49e7b16`
the record; no "Step 0", phase 4's audit having left none for it); their files: `port/windows/d3d11/` and the
two READMEs and `D3D11.md` alone; `ninja windows` with no work to do at `a49e7b16`; the object check (`git diff
--name-only d943c612..HEAD` over the four trees: `port/linux/src/port_config.c` and `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`,

against `setup_streams` (the streams with data, `D3DVSDT_NONE` left out, the last element of a register). The
