Found, none blocking:

- **The attribute slot's elements are `R32G32B32A32_FLOAT` whatever the shader's input type** (code, for phase 6's
  "Step 0", where the slot's buffer is made). `nv2a_vsh_hlsl.c` declares a register in `packed_mask` as `uint` and
  one in `integer_mask` as `int4`; when such a register's element is left out of the layout (its stream has no
  data), `d3d11_backend_layout_make` adds it from slot 16 as four floats: `CreateInputLayout` then warns with the
  layer (`CREATEINPUTLAYOUT_TYPE_MISMATCH`), and the shader would read a float's bits as an integer, where GL gives
  a packed attribute the integer zero (`setup_streams`: `state_attribute_value(index, NULL)`). Not met: a10's one
  unfed input (program 2's `v9`) is a float, and the layer was silent. Phase 6: the element's format from the
  signature's component type (the `ISGN` entry's word at 12) or from the masks (`R32G32B32A32_SINT`, `R32_UINT`),
  and the slot's bytes zero for a packed register.
- **Four small ones in the service** (code, for phase 6's "Step 0" at its choice): without `d3dcompiler_47.dll`,
  `load_compiler` is tried and logged again for each new shader (bounded by the shaders met; the entries fail and
  are not asked again); `cache_read` logs `size` uninitialised when `GetFileSizeEx` fails; a `.tmp` a failed write
  left in this version's folder is never removed (other versions' folders take theirs away); a layout that is not
  made is logged once in all, where the section says once for each shader.
- **Not tried by the self-test or the corpus: element offsets that are not multiples of four.** The self-test puts
  every element at a multiple of 16 ("the alignment is not what is tried"), so the offsets Direct3D 11 accepts are
  proven only by a10's declarations (every layout made, the layer silent). A map whose declaration Direct3D 11
  refused would show as the log's `the input layout ... was not made` and `skipped ... no layout`: phase 6's and
  phase 7's other maps should watch that count. Words, not behaviour.
- `port/windows/README.md`: the paragraph the README commit changed was left with a short line; rewrapped in this
  audit's commit (no other change). `d3d11_backend.h`'s header comment has the same short line ("differently, so
  no unit"); left, to not touch a compiled file for a comment. Cosmetic.

### Phase 5 — summary

Worked unattended on this PC (Snapdragon X Plus, Adreno X1-45 driver 31.0.137.0, WARP 10.0.26100.9444, Windows 11 on Arm64, the
game x86 under emulation), release build, `--portable`, full LTO, PGO off (clang 20). Commits: `7ea007f1` (the shader service with

read the attribute slot named in the log), `f37aace7` (the READMEs), `81ad2bf8` (the layouts counted by type, as this section asks,
not their elements); this entry is the last. No "Step 0" commit: phase 4's audit left no code finding for it. **Every draw now has
its two shaders (compiled at the draw the first time, from the cache on the disk after), its input layout and its four kinds of

**Found, for phase 6**: a10 has draws that blend with `SRCBLEND` `CONSTANTALPHA` (32771) and `DESTBLEND` `CONSTANTCOLOR` (32769)
(`Direct3D 11: a draw blends by the constant's alpha (source 32771, destination 32769): ...`, logged once, in every a10 run). Direct3D
11 has one blend factor for both: the source needs it as the constant's alpha in every channel, the destination as the constant's
colour. The translation gives both `BLEND_FACTOR` (the spec's table); phase 6 must draw these differently (the source's factor taken
into the pixel shader's colour, or a factor of `ONE` with the shader multiplying), and look at them on the screen. Also for phase 6:
the 21 render targets the draws now make in a10 (phase 4: 4, made by the clears and the present alone; deviation 9), and program 2's
input 9 from the attribute slot.

**The failure paths, read** (the ones not forced above): a folder that cannot be made (`no shader cache (<folder> could not be made:
error <n>)`, every shader compiled, nothing written); a write that fails (the `.tmp` left, `was not written (error <n>; the .tmp may
be left; logged once)`, the shader made and used, compiled again next run); a compile that fails (logged once a hash with the
compiler's text, the HLSL in `failed\`, the entry failed for the run and its draws counted `skipped no shader`; a lit pair's failure
logs `prepare_draw`'s line and falls back to the unlit pair); a layout that is not made (logged once, 0 answered, the draw counted
`skipped no layout`); a state object that is not made (logged once a kind, its 0 kept in `device.draw`, the draw not skipped); a lost device (every call answers 0, the
entries' failures are moot: `draw_ready` stops the draws).

**The device against `d3d8_gl.c`**, read: `blend_factor`, `blend_operation`, `comparison`, `stencil_operation`, `address_mode` against
`apply_raster_state` (3145–3322), `blend_equation`, `stencil_operation`, `address_mode` and `xdk_pdb.h`'s values entry by entry (GL's
`GL_INCR`/`GL_DECR`, Xbox `INCRSAT`/`DECRSAT` 7682/7683, saturate: Direct3D's `INCR_SAT`/`DECR_SAT`; the `_WRAP` forms, Xbox `INCR`/
`DECR` 34055/34056, are Direct3D's `INCR`/`DECR`; a comparison 0 is `NEVER` as `rs ? rs : GL_NEVER` reads it); the colour mask's
four bits; cull `FRONT` when `CULLMODE == FRONTFACE`, `FrontCounterClockwise` from `D3DFRONT_CCW` (the desktop branch's front face);
the polygon offset's units rounded; `sampler_inputs` is `configure_sampler`'s eleven inputs line for line and `sampler_description`
`sampler_parameters`' choices; `attribute_format` (the device's) is `d3d8_gl.c`'s `attribute_format` and the packed attribute's
`GL_UNSIGNED_INT`, with the DXGI formats the spec's table names. The backend's numbers for Direct3D's enumerants and formats are
checked against `d3d11.h` and `dxgiformat.h` by `_Static_assert` in `win32_d3d11_shaders.c`.

**The object check**: `git diff --name-only d943c612..HEAD -- port/linux/src source port/windows/src tools` lists
`port/linux/src/port_config.c` and `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py` (phase 4's), nothing else; this phase changed
`port/windows/d3d11/` and the two READMEs alone. **The symbol difference**, `llvm-nm -g --defined-only` without the `??_C@`
literals: exactly `_xgpu_compile_shader`, `_xgpu_link_program`, `_xgpu_render_target_find` (106 against 103). `grep -E
'\bgl[A-Z]\w+\('` on `d3d8_d3d11.c`, `nv2a_vsh_hlsl.c` and `nv2a_psh_hlsl.c`: 0, 0, 0.

**Not run**: a10 on WARP (the standing decision); the Adreno's runs with the layer on a10 were ended by killing the process (the
console's `quit` does not exit), so the live-object report at exit is the menus' runs'; the Xbox menus' run was ended by killing it
too (the PC menu's quit keys do not apply there; the log runs to the kill, no crash). Not forced: a cache folder that cannot be
made, a write that fails, a layout or a state object that fails (read above). Not done by the agent in any case: a second GPU
family; seeing a picture (nothing is drawn).

### Phase 5: deviations from the spec

Everything phase 5 did differently from the "Phase 5 — shaders and state objects" section, or beyond it.

**The process**

1. **No "Step 0" commit**: phase 4's audit left no code finding for phase 5 ("Phase 4 — audit": one for phase 6).

**The backend**

2. **The inputs a declaration does not feed are in the layout**, from an attribute slot (`D3D11_BACKEND_ATTRIBUTE_SLOT`, 16) as
   `R32G32B32A32_FLOAT` at `16 × register`, where the spec left them out for phase 6. The backend reads the vertex shader's input
   signature from its bytecode (the `ISGN` chunk: the `TEXCOORD<n>` declared and those read) and adds an element for each declared
   input the device's elements leave out, so `CreateInputLayout` is never handed a layout missing a declared input (which the debug
   layer reports as an error), read or not. A buffer of the sixteen current attribute values at stride 0 in slot 16 (phase 6) gives
   each such input what GL's disabled attribute gives (`state_attribute_value`). The layouts whose shader *reads* such an input are
   counted (`with inputs from the attribute slot`) and the first 16 named in the log (`b6a75592`): one in a10, program 2's `v9`.
3. **`d3d11_backend_shaders_statistics` fills eleven counts** (`D3D11_BACKEND_STATISTICS`), not eight: the spec's nine and the
   microseconds of the reads from the cache and the layouts with inputs from the attribute slot; the times are kept in
   microseconds. **They are printed on a statistics line of their own** (`frame <n>: shaders <c> cached in <ms> ms, <k> compiled
   in <ms> ms, <f> failed; <l> layouts (<u> with inputs from the attribute slot), <b>/<d>/<r>/<s> blend/depth-stencil/rasterizer/
   sampler states; skipped since the start: <n> no shader, <m> no layout`), before the frame's line, which was long already; the
   counts are since the self-tests (the device takes the statistics after them and subtracts), so the self-test's shaders, files and
   objects are not the game's.
4. **The compiler's version** is read with `version.dll`'s functions found at run time (`LoadLibraryA("version.dll")`), not linked:
   the executable links no `version.lib`, and adding it is a change to `port.json` or `tools/windows_build.py`, which `halo.exe`
   shares. The folder is `1-6.2.26100.9168` here.
5. **The file's header is 40 bytes**: `HDXB`, the format's version (1), the generator's version, 4 reserved bytes, the hash, the
   target (8 bytes, `vs_`/`ps_` checked against the stage), the bytecode's size and its CRC-32; a file is checked for its size (at
   most 16 MB), its header, the hash and the CRC, and the log says which failed. The set of names listed at the start keeps a
   deleted file as "gone" (an open-addressed index has no removal).
6. **The self-test's bad shader** is an undeclared name (`return not_declared;`, `error X3004`): HLSL compiles the Vulkan plan's
   `float this` without a word. The compiler's messages name a shader by its cache name (`p<hash>.hlsl`).
7. **The service's self-test is in two parts**: the backend's cases (the spec's: the good pair, the bad shader, the damaged file,
   a layout with an input left out, one state object of each kind found again), and the device's: a layout of every one of the 19
   types of the format table over a vertex shader the generator makes for the declaration's masks, in two declarations (`vs_4_0` has
   sixteen inputs) of two streams, the second with 13 inputs from the attribute slot. The format table is the device's, which the
   backend cannot read. Its files are in the real cache folder (its own hashes, deleted at its start so that it compiles); without a
   cache (no `LOCALAPPDATA`, or a debug setting) the cache's cases are left out and the line says so.
8. **A state description's unused part is fixed** (blending off: `ONE`, `ZERO`, `ADD`; no depth test: `ALWAYS`, no write; no stencil:
   masks `0xff`, `KEEP`, `ALWAYS`; no culling: `FrontCounterClockwise` 0; no `BORDER` address: the border colour 0), so that states that
   draw the same are one object and one hash; the device asks the backend only when a kind's description (or a stage's sampler
   inputs) differs from its last one, as `gl_state` skips a call that changes nothing.

**The device**

9. **The draw's targets are found at every draw** (`render_target_get` for the colour and the depth surface, as `bind_targets` finds
   them, without binding: the depth target's `has_depth` for the depth-stencil state), so the draws make the targets' entries and the
   backend's textures the first time, as `d3d8_gl.c`'s draws do: a10 has 21 targets (phase 4: 4, the clears' and the present's). A
   sampled render target is then found by the key (phase 4's deviation 13 no longer applies to targets only drawn into).
10. **The layout is kept on the declaration by the vertex shader variant and the streams with data** (four, the last met), as
    `d3d8_gl.c` keeps its vertex array by `vertex_array_streams`; one element a register (the last naming it, as GL's layout keeps
    the last), elements of type `NONE` and of streams with no data left out (`setup_streams`' test); immediate mode's layout is kept
    on its variant. The backend's hash makes one object of equal layouts across declarations.
11. **A draw `draw_note` skips does no data work**: the note comes before the data (as `prepare_draw` before `setup_streams`), so a
    draw skipped for a shader or a layout is not counted as a draw and copies nothing.
12. **The sampler of every stage with a texture takes `hires`** from the HUD override found for the texture (any stage, as
    `xbox_textures.c` sets `description->hires`), and the key's coverage and point threshold stay stage 0's; a sampled render target's
    `mipmapped` is `bind_textures`' mip-composite test. The menu art's and the text atlas's `hires` (the texture cache's) are phase 6's,
    with the audit's finding.
13. **The filters**: Direct3D 11's anisotropic filter is linear for the magnification and the mip levels, where GL kept the asked
    ones; a mip filter of `NONE` is `MinLOD` = `MaxLOD` = 0 (the spec's "point with `MaxLOD` 0"); `MipLODBias` is held to Direct3D's
    `[-16, 15.99]`; `MaxAnisotropy` at most 16, and 1 for the other filters; `ComparisonFunc` `NEVER`.
14. **`CONSTANTALPHA` and `INVCONSTANTALPHA`** are `BLEND_FACTOR` and `INV_BLEND_FACTOR` as the spec says, and the first draw that
    uses one is logged once: a10 has one (the finding above). Point fill is solid as the spec says, logged once: not met.
15. **The layouts' line**: `Direct3D 11: <n> layouts made by the draws; the layouts using each vertex type: ...` every 600 frames
    when more were made (with `debug.gpu_stats`); `81ad2bf8` made it count layouts, not elements.

**Testing**

16. **The cold and second starts ran with the layer on**, a10 reached at `game_speed 8` and measured at `game_speed 1` as phase 3's;
    the frame-rate runs without the layer, the cache warm. The first frame-rate run's cache held the earlier runs' shaders; the second
    (`p5_types_a10`) followed WARP's cold start, so its a10 compiled 224 shaders in 3,598 ms before the measurement: the 60 s measured
    are after it.
17. **The main menu only** before a10, not the PC menus' walk of phase 4's corpus (the shaders are phase 4's; this phase's counts
    are the service's): the Xbox menus once, the cache warm (6 compiled in 56 ms, 6 layouts, 9/3/2/8 states).
18. **The run script checks the backup's MD5 before each start** and puts `config.toml` back after it; the cache folder of the
    Adreno's runs was copied to the scratchpad before WARP's cold start removed it.

alone, then `62307379`, `da3b37f4`, `3c370e35`, `b7c93b93`, `b099f2ad`); "Step 0" against phase 3's audit
(the upload ring's offset set to its end before case 3's green write, so `buffer_write` discards with the red

(`diff --strip-trailing-cr`: the originals are CRLF in the working tree), every changed line one of the
dialect's ten items or the Files table's (the header comment, the entry points, the lighting detection not
copied), the instruction decoding, the combiners, fog, the alpha tests, the texture modes, the lighting and the

comparison fails on the one-line change with the summary's diff at line 187, and passes unchanged. **The

`tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`); the symbol difference (exactly the three, 106 against 103, without the `??_C@`

the quit sequence, `HALO_MAX_FPS=120`, `HALO_D3D11_SELF_TEST=1`, exit code 0 in each: the Adreno and WARP
(`renderer: Direct3D 11 on Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU ... driver 31.0.137.0` and `on Microsoft Basic Render
Driver (WARP) ... 10.0.26100.9444`), each without and with the layer: the clears', the memory's ("across its
wrap") and the shader self-test's `ok (36 synthetic shaders compile: ...)` lines in all four; `11 pixel keys, 5
vertex variants met`, `skipped in 60 frames: 0 no program, 0 no buffer`, `0 debug layer messages` in every line;
at exit the layer's 9 live-object lines of phases 2 and 3 and nothing else. The Adreno's run with the layer also
dumped (`shader dump: 5 vertex shaders and 11 pixel shaders written`): its 43 files are the worker's corpus's of

- **The key's high-res flags skip two of the texture cache's checks** (code, for phase 6, not phase 5's "Step

### Phase 4: deviations from the spec

Everything phase 4 did differently from the "Phase 4 — HLSL" section, or beyond it.

**The dialect**

1. **The interface's values are statics in both stages** (`static float4 xD0;` ... from `HLSL_INTERFACE_STATICS`, GLSL's `out` and
   `in` declarations line for line), copied from the input structure at the pixel shader's start (`HLSL_INTERFACE_INPUT`) and into
   the output structure at the vertex shader's end (`HLSL_INTERFACE_OUTPUT`, then `return output;`), so that the bodies, and
   `model_lighting()`, which reads `xWorldNormal` outside `main`, are the GLSL's lines unchanged. The structure is named `vs_out`
   in both stages, the lit members after the nine (`HLSL_INTERFACE_BEGIN` ... `HLSL_INTERFACE_END`, one string for both
   generators). `gl_Position = position;` becomes `vs_out output;` and `output.position = position;` at its place.
2. **A one-argument constructor is `((floatN)(x))`**, with outer parentheses (phase 0's form), not `(floatN)(x)`: a swizzle or an
   operator after a cast binds to its operand otherwise (`(float4)(0.0).xyzw` swizzles the scalar). `float(x)` and `int(x)` are
   `(float)(x)` and `(int)(x)` as the spec says.
3. **`lessThan(A, B)` and `greaterThanEqual(A, B)`** (`slt`, `sge`) are `(A < B)` and `(A >= B)` (componentwise in HLSL): item 3
   did not list them.
4. **`point_size` is `0.0`**: `oPts`, which a program may write and nothing reads, starts at 0.
5. **`textureSize(tex0, 0)`** is `int2 size; tex0.GetDimensions(size.x, size.y);` in the line's place; `texelFetch(t, p, 0)` is
   `t.Load(int3(p, 0))`.
6. **The multisampled alpha test's shader** returns `struct ps_out` (`HLSL_PIXEL_COVERAGE_OUTPUT`, after the interface), declares
   `ps_out output;` after the input's copy, writes `output.coverage = (uint)(...)` for `gl_SampleMask[0]` and ends `output.color =
   ...; return output;`. Its condition is the alpha test's, computed once at the top to choose the target (a `NOTEQUAL` or `EQUAL`
   test with samples stays `ps_4_0`, as the original takes its single-sample path for them; the self-test checks both).
7. **The cbuffer for the lights is named `model_lights`**, as its member: `d3dcompiler_47` accepts it (tried first).

**The header**

8. **`hlsl_shaders.h` includes no key header**: `xgpu.h` is the Xbox SDK's side, which the backend cannot include, so the key and
   the lighting are named by forward declaration (pointers only), and the header is `<stddef.h>` and `<stdint.h>` only. It has four
   structures (the vertex constants too) and the cbuffers' whole texts as string macros made from the `_REGISTER` defines by
   stringizing (no arithmetic, no `%d`).
9. **The generators' signatures take `uint32_t`** (the Files table's), and cast to the original's `const DWORD *` and `unsigned long`
   names in two lines at the top, so the body is the original's.

**The device**

10. **The variant table** is a global one by identity (1,024 buckets), with each program's `[lit][immediate]` remembering the
    variant it last met (a pointer, `[2][2]`, as GL's `shader[2]` and `lit_shader[2]`) for the declaration's masks: the masks come
    from the declaration (`device.vertex_shader`) and the words from `current_program()`, so one program can meet several
    declarations, which GL's per-program cache did not tell apart (it kept the first mask). The spec's `[2 lit][3 variant]` was
    read as this.
11. **A vertex shader's identity's "lit"** is the lighting's light count (1 or 2, 0 unlit), and the words hashed are all the
    program's stored instructions.
12. **The draws are noted at `draw_ready`**, before the data work, not after binding targets (phase 3's draws bind none; the
    Vulkan plan's deviation 2): a draw with no target is noted too.
13. **A sampled render target is found in the renderer's own table** by a static copy of `xgpu_render_target_find`
    (`render_target_find`, not exported, so the symbol difference stays the three). Phase 3's draws do not make entries (only the
    clears and the present do), so a target only drawn into is not found and takes the bitmap's path: the same sampler type (2D),
    and no high-res flags unless its address is a HUD bitmap's. The text atlas's and the menu art's precedence over a HUD override
    (`texture_entry_result`) is not reproduced (they need GL); no key differed for it (above).
14. **The dump**: the manifest is appended (`"a"`, `3c370e35`), so the menus' and a map's runs build one corpus; a shader met again
    by a later run is written again, the same. Text files are written `"w"` as `d3d8_gl.c` writes its dump, so a twin is the
    OpenGL executable's file byte for byte. The manifest also gives each pixel shader's OpenGL dump name (`gl=ps_<hash_words>.glsl`)
    and each vertex shader's instruction count, and the script checks the twins by those names too.
15. **The self-test** compiles 36 shaders, more than the list: every texture mode (the bump, dependent, dot, reflect and clip modes),
    every alpha test function, eight combiner stages with every input and output mapping and both muxes, and a vertex program
    using every MAC and ILU operation, relative addressing and the clip position's capture.

**The script**

16. **`--prove` and `--copies`** are modes of `tools/d3d11_shader_check.py`: the one-line change made in memory, and the classed
    diff of the copies, so the audit runs them as they were run here. The class of a line is found by what it contains, in a fixed
    order of precedence; a brace or a control line takes its change's class (counted apart in the table).
17. **The compile times** are the script's (native Arm64 Python), shown beside phase 0's (the game's emulated x86).

**Testing**

18. **The corpus has an a10 run with `HALO_PER_PIXEL_LIGHTING=1`** (and so does the OpenGL one), so that lit pairs are in it.
19. **The PC main menu is CAMPAIGN, MULTIPLAYER, PROFILES, QUIT** (the settings are the profile's edit, under PROFILES); the walk
    finds each screen by its immediate draws (96, 240, 274) and steps back by them. At the first run the Quick settings flyout had
    the focus and every key was lost: an Escape closed it. The harness finds the game's window by `EnumWindows` when
    `MainWindowHandle` is 0, and waits 6 s in the quit dialog.
20. **halo.exe was run the same way for its dump**, at 3 frames a second in the cryo room (as phase 3 found), 187 s to reach it.

### Phase 3 — audit

The audit (a session of its own, after the five commits) found the record true to the code and nothing
blocking. Reproduced: the function-by-function comparison (`index_extent` and `quad_indices` byte for
byte; `mirror_refresh`, `mirror_range`, `stream_reserve`, `stream_upload`, `index_upload`, `DrawVertices`,
`DrawIndexedVertices` and `End` differ from `d3d8_gl.c` only by the backend's calls in the GL's place, the
Android branches gone, the segment made before its pages are marked, the draws counted where GL drew and
the lines left for phase 6 marked; `stream_data` is `setup_streams`' two loops without the layout's lines),
the GL grep (0), the symbol difference (exactly the three, 103 against 106, once `llvm-nm`'s `??_C@` string
literal symbols are left out), `git diff --name-only d943c612..HEAD` over the shared trees naming
`port_config.c` alone (so phase 1's object check holds without a second mapped rebuild), `d3d11_backend.h`
with `<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only, the commits in the brief's order (the plan's text alone, then
"Step 0" alone), `ninja windows` with no work to do at `af8a31ce`, `config.toml` at the summary's MD5. The
backend's calls were read against the Direct3D 11 documentation: `DYNAMIC` with `CPU_ACCESS_WRITE` and a
bind flag; `NO_OVERWRITE` on vertex and index buffers at 11_0, `DISCARD` at a ring's first map and after an
orphan or the upload ring's wrap, every map unmapped before the buffer is used; `CopySubresourceRegion` from
the `DYNAMIC` upload ring (any usage may be a source) into a `DEFAULT` segment (not `DYNAMIC`, not
`IMMUTABLE`), two resources, the box in bytes; no map waits but the read-back's. The no-overwrite promise
holds in the device: each ring is written at rising offsets since its last orphan, and `stream_data`
reserves all its streams before writing any, so `stream_upload`'s own `stream_reserve` cannot orphan between
them, as in `setup_streams`. Run again on this PC: the self-tests `ok` on the Adreno, with and without the
layer, and on WARP, the `buffers:` line at 2,980 / 2,978 / 3,019 MB free; the main menu's line the
summary's (`7 draws, 138 immediate dropped ... 52 KB mirrored, 0 KB streamed, 0 KB uploaded to 1 segments`,
a discard every 240 frames); the layer's 9 live-object lines at exit; a10 on the Adreno with the layer for
92 s to game_time 2,259 at `HALO_MAX_FPS=120`: 7 segments after the load, 2,400 to 3,700 KB mirrored and
22 to 243 KB streamed a frame through the cinematic (one line 3,096 / 230, the summary's at game_time 593),
`0 KB uploaded` once loaded, `skipped 0 no program, 0 no buffer` and `0 debug layer messages` in all 186
lines, no lost device, no refused write. a10 on WARP was not run, as the user decided. Found, none
blocking, for phase 4's "Step 0" where they are code:

- **The upload ring's wrap is not in the self-test.** Its `DISCARD`, while the copies queued from the old
  storage must still read it, ran at least four times in a10's load (32 MB of segments through the 8 MB
  ring), but nothing drew from the segments, so it is unproven until phase 6 draws. Case 3's green write
  could be made to wrap by setting the ring's offset to its end before it, with the red copy still queued.
- The statistics line's `skipped <q> no program, <b> no buffer` are totals over the 60 frames among
  per-frame averages, and the line does not say; after a lost device every draw is counted "no program",
  as `d3d8_gl.c`'s `gl_ready` guard counted it. Words, not behaviour.
- `target_make`, `target_free` and `read_target` are defined under their short names in `win32_d3d11.c`
  and exported as `d3d11_backend_target_make` and so on by `#define` in `win32_d3d11.h` (deviation 7), so a
  search for the exported name finds no definition. Cosmetic.
- The main menu's 8 immediate draws more than `halo.exe`'s: the line under the version number is
  `d3d8_renderer_description`'s "Direct3D 11" where `halo.exe`'s is "OpenGL", five glyphs more, which is
  most of them if a glyph is an immediate draw; the rest was not chased. Not code.

### Phase 3 — summary

Worked on this PC (Snapdragon X Plus, Adreno X1-45 driver 31.0.137.0, WARP 10.0.26100.9444, Windows 11 on Arm64, the game x86
under emulation), release build, `--portable`, full LTO, PGO off (clang 20). Commits: `7a182a92` (the plan's text and phase 2's
audit), `577d452a` ("Step 0"), `3299ae78` (the device's memory paths, the memory unit, the compiler and targets exported),
`90722582` (the READMEs). **Every draw's data is copied into Direct3D 11 buffers at the draw, on the game's real traffic**, and
the bytes mirrored and streamed a frame are `halo.exe`'s for the same scenes. Settings from the environment as phase 2's
(`HALO_DISPLAY_MODE=windowed`, `HALO_WINDOW_SIZE=1280x960`, `HALO_VOLUME=0.05`, `HALO_D3D11_*`); every a10 run but the one
without a limit at `HALO_MAX_FPS=120` (`config.toml` here has `vsync = false` and `max_fps = -1`, and was not edited; it was
put back at MD5 `85841a6b...` after the game added its two rows). The statistics lines are 60-frame averages; the a10 scenes
are matched between the executables by `game_time`, since `halo.exe` falls to a few frames a second in the cryo room on this
PC and its game time falls behind (below).

| The process's virtual size in a10 | 1.17 to 1.25 GB (`halo.exe`: 1.57 to 2.07 GB, rising) | |

**What the copy changes against `d3d8_gl.c`** (a script extracting the functions, as phase 2's): `index_extent` and
`quad_indices` byte for byte. `mirror_range`: the segment's handle where the buffer's name was. `mirror_refresh`: the Android
branch and its `unused` flag gone; the segment made by the backend, before the run's pages are marked present (deviation 1);
the backend's `d3d11_backend_segment_write` where `buffer_upload` was; the KB uploaded counted. `stream_reserve`,
`stream_upload`, `index_upload`: the GL buffer binds gone, the orphan `d3d11_backend_ring_orphan`, the write
`d3d11_backend_ring_write` (a failed write asks `backend_lost_check`); the rounding, the reservation and the offsets as they
were. `stream_data` is `setup_streams`' first loop whole and its second loop's streaming; the layout, the vertex array, the
buffers' binds and the attributes' values are left out with comments for phase 6. `DrawVertices`, `DrawIndexedVertices` and
`End` are `d3d8_gl.c`'s with `prepare_draw`'s guards (`draw_ready`, with "no buffer" added), the data work as there, and a
comment where GL drew. `Present` no longer orphans the rings each frame (the plan's one change to the design).
`buffer_upload` and its pieces do not come.

**The backend against the documentation**: the rings and the upload ring are `D3D11_USAGE_DYNAMIC` with
`D3D11_CPU_ACCESS_WRITE` and a bind flag (a dynamic buffer needs one; the upload ring's `VERTEX_BUFFER` is never bound).
`Map` with `D3D11_MAP_WRITE_NO_OVERWRITE` is allowed on dynamic vertex and index buffers at feature level 11_0 (on constant
buffers and views only from 11.1, not used); the first map of each buffer, and every map after an orphan or the upload
ring's wrap, is `D3D11_MAP_WRITE_DISCARD`; every `Map` is unmapped before anything uses the buffer. The segments are
`DEFAULT`, with no CPU access, bound `VERTEX_BUFFER | INDEX_BUFFER` (allowed together). `CopySubresourceRegion` copies from
the dynamic upload ring (a source may have any usage) into a `DEFAULT` segment (a destination must not be dynamic or
immutable): two different resources (no overlap), the box `[at, at + size)` by 1 by 1, `DstY` and `DstZ` 0, the source
unmapped. Nothing in the device or the backend waits on the GPU for a resource; the only `Map` that waits is the self-tests'
read-back. `IsBusy`, the locks and `BlockUntilNotBusy` are as before (`d3d8_resources.c` unchanged).

**The failure paths, read** (none can be forced here): rings not made (`d3d11: no buffers (<which>: <hresult>)`, the ones made
released, `buffers_ready` false, every draw counted as `skipped no buffer`, the clears still shown); a write past a ring's
end or a segment's, or larger than the upload ring (refused, logged once); a `Map` that fails (logged once; a lost device
goes through `backend_failed`, then the device's `backend_lost_check` says so once and the game goes on, as phase 2's); a
segment not made (logged; `mirror_refresh` returns FALSE before marking its pages, and the draw streams); the self-test's
objects not made (`FAILED` and why, its objects released).

**The object check**: `git diff --name-only d943c612..HEAD -- port/linux/src source port/windows/src tools` lists
`port/linux/src/port_config.c` alone (phase 2's), so `halo.exe`'s objects are phase 2's; this phase's builds did not relink
it. **The symbol difference**, `llvm-nm -g --defined-only`: exactly `_xgpu_compile_shader`, `_xgpu_link_program`,
`_xgpu_render_target_find` (103 against 106). `grep -E '\bgl[A-Z]\w+\('` on `d3d8_d3d11.c`: 0.

**Not run**: a10 on WARP, with and without the layer, and the window's cases on WARP (the user's decision); the window's
cases with the layer. a10 was left by ending the process (the console's `quit` does not exit), so the live-object report at
exit is the menus' runs'. Not done by the agent in any case: hearing the sound; a gamepad.

### Phase 3: deviations from the spec

Everything phase 3 did differently from the "Phase 3 — the game's memory in Direct3D buffers" section, or beyond it.

**The device**

1. **`mirror_refresh` makes the segment before it marks the run's pages present**, where `d3d8_gl.c` made the buffer after:
   a segment that cannot be made must leave the pages absent, or the next draw would find them present in a segment that
   does not exist.
2. **The rings start full** (`stream_offset` and `index_offset` at the rings' sizes, after the self-tests), so the first write
   to each orphans it through `d3d8_gl.c`'s own path: the self-test's writes are at offsets the device does not know.
3. **A draw is counted where GL drew**, after the guards and the data work, as `d3d8_gl.c` counts it in `prepare_draw`;
   phases 1 and 2 counted every call. `trace_draw` (no GL in it) is not copied: phase 1 has no trace of draws.
4. **The KB uploaded** count includes a run whose write failed (only a lost device fails it, after which the figure means
   nothing).

**The backend**

5. **`d3d11_backend_ring_discards()`**, a function beside the spec's, gives the statistics the rings' discards (the upload
   ring's are not counted: they are the backend's own).
6. **The `buffers:` line also gives the free address space before the buffers** (`(3,017 MB before them)`), so their cost is
   in the line; phase 0's figure was another process's.
7. **`struct target`, `target_make`, `target_free` and `read_target` moved to `win32_d3d11.h`** (as
   `d3d11_backend_target_make` and so on, the short names by macro, as `release` is), and the compiler is exported as
   `d3d11_backend_compile(source, size, name, entry, target, code)`, which loads the DLL itself; the built-in shaders compile
   through it.
8. **The self-test's segment is its own** (made as the mirror's are and released, not in the table, so not one of the
   segments alive), and its discards are taken back out of the count. **Case 2 writes red after case 1's bytes** (at 96, not
   0): a no-overwrite write must not touch bytes an earlier draw read, even in a test. **Case 4's six vertices** (red, then
   green) are written once into the segment, and the two writes to the index ring name the red three, then the green.
9. **The self-test sets every state it draws with** (the clears' blend, depth and scissored rasterizer states, a scissor
   over the target) and unbinds its vertex and index buffers, layout, shaders and target after, so nothing of it stays bound.

**Testing**

10. **The cost runs reached the cryo room at `game_speed 8`** (from the console) and measured at `game_speed 1`, the rate
    checked by `game_time` (+1,836 in 60 s). The console answers with line breaks between its words.
11. **Phase 2's CPU time came from a build of `4992b2c1`** in a worktree (`..\halo-phase2`), its executable copied beside the
    data as `build\windows\halo_d3d11_phase2.exe` for the run and removed after, with the worktree.
12. **The first cost runs were stopped by Claude Code for the system's low memory** (2.3 GB free of 16 GB, not the game's
    doing); the user asked for the rest to run once each, without WARP.
13. **The window's cases ran in one a10 run**; F11 back was confirmed by the user (the agent's key was lost while the window
    was behind the user's work).
14. **The 120 frames a second limit came from `HALO_MAX_FPS`**, not `config.toml`, which was not edited.

### Phase 2 — audit

The audit (a session of its own, after the six commits) found the record true to the code and nothing
blocking. Reproduced without running the game: the symbol difference (exactly the three named, 103 against
106), the GL grep (0), the function-by-function comparison (the six functions the summary names are back
byte for byte; the seven it names as changed are the only ones that differ from `d3d8_gl.c`, and in them
only by the backend's calls, the count moved and the Android guard), that `halo.exe`'s inputs hold no
Direct3D unit and that `port_config.c` is the only one of them changed since `d943c612` (so phase 1's
object check holds without a second mapped rebuild, which was not repeated), `d3d11_backend.h` with
`<stdint.h>` and `<stddef.h>` only, the commits in the brief's order (the plan's text alone, then "Step 0"
alone), and `config.toml` at the summary's MD5. The device's `Clear`, `Present` and render targets were read
against `d3d8_gl.c` line by line, and the backend's calls against the Direct3D 11 and DXGI documentation:
the flip-model rules (the device's own factory, the swap chain's flags given again to `ResizeBuffers`
after the view is released and the context flushed, tearing only at interval 0 and never in DXGI's
fullscreen), the clear calls' disregard of the scissor and the write masks (so only whole all-channel
clears use them), the back buffer unbound from the pixel shader after the present's draw and never a
target while sampled, `Map` on the staging texture by `RowPitch`. The pacing rule holds:
`platform_video_swap` in the no-context mode only paces, so skipping it for a frame the present's interval
paced loses nothing. The testing the user stopped is listed in the summary and accepted as it stands.
Found, none blocking, for phase 3's "Step 0" where they are code:

- **The minimised CPU time misses the acceptance's figure on the Adreno**: 1.95 s over 10 s where it
  asked for under a second (WARP 0.67 s; `halo.exe` 8.2 s). The backend rests 16 ms a frame; the rest is
  the game's own simulation under emulation, not the renderer's. The figure, not the code, was wrong.
- `d3d11_backend_present` after a `ResizeBuffers` that failed (no view) returns 0 without the rest: with
  `display.max_fps = -1` the game spins on that path until the window's size changes. Not met.
- `d3d11_backend_target_create` compares the bound pointers with the old table after `realloc` freed it
  (undefined in the letter, right in practice); the offsets should be taken before the call.
- The brief said `config.toml` held `paths.data` and `display.mode`; it holds neither (the data is found
  beside the executable). The session set the settings from the environment (deviation 11). Phase 3's
  brief says so.
- The Adreno's resets are rightly left open: the same reset, with the same crash of the same app, came
  before this phase's code existed, and never with vsync or the layer. The acceptance's "no lost device"
  is read as "none the renderer caused", which the evidence supports but cannot prove.

### Phase 2 — summary

Worked on this PC (Snapdragon X Plus, Adreno X1-45 driver 31.0.137.0, Windows 11 on Arm64, the game x86
under emulation), release build, `--portable`, full LTO, PGO off (clang 20). Code in commits `7d99677b`
("Step 0"), `f0d4c745` (the two settings rows), `dd229fe3` (the device and the backend) and `aead77f1`
(the READMEs). **The game's clears are on the screen through Direct3D 11 on both drivers**: the swap chain,
the render targets, the clears, the letterboxed present, the marker, screenshots, the self-test and the
lost device all work. **One finding is open**: the Adreno's GPU was reset by Windows twice in a10 with no
frame limit (below, and "What is open"). The testing was stopped by the user before it was complete; what
was not run is listed at the end.

The runs set their settings from the environment (`HALO_DISPLAY_MODE=windowed`, `HALO_WINDOW_SIZE=1280x960`,
`HALO_VOLUME=0.05`, and the `HALO_D3D11_*` variables), because `build\windows\config.toml` did not hold
what the brief said it did (no `display.mode`, no `paths.data`: the data is found beside the executable).
`config.toml` was edited only for `display.vsync = true` in the vsync runs, and put back byte for byte at
the end (MD5 `85841a6b...`), including the two rows the game adds to it as new settings.

| Screenshots (`debug.screenshot_every`) | BMPs at the back buffer's pixel size, one per window size seen (1280x960, 1600x1200, 800x600, 1900x1425, 956x717, 2880x1620), black as the menu clears, without the marker. A uniform colour shows no orientation; the read-back's orientation is the self-test's (its red quarter is read at the top) | BMPs written |
| Debug layer | no `d3d11 debug:` line in the menus (both) or in 300 s of a10 (Adreno); at exit the live-object summary names the device's own objects only: `Live ID3D11Device ... Refcount: 4`, `ID3D11Context`, `ID3DDeviceContextState`, `ID3D11BlendState`, `ID3D11DepthStencilState`, `ID3D11RasterizerState`, `ID3D11Sampler`, `ID3D11Query` and the layer's hint, `9 messages` (after a fix: see the deviations, 6) | the same 9 |

**What is open: the Adreno's resets.** Both resets are logged by Windows as `LiveKernelEvent 117` (a
video timeout) in `qcdxkm8380.sys`, the Qualcomm kernel driver (`LKD_0x117_Tdr:3_TdrVTR:0`), and both
came in the same second as a crash of `MicrosoftSecurityApp.exe` (22:02:21 and 22:12:14). The same reset,
with the same crash of that app, happened on this PC at 16:48 the same day, before any of this phase's
code existed (between phase 0's probe runs, which saw only `S_OK`). The reports Windows kept
(`WER\ReportArchive\Kernel_117_*`, read by the user from an elevated terminal) hold no dump and no process
name; `LiveKernelReports` is the SYSTEM account's alone. So which process's GPU work hung is not known.
What is known: it came only in a10 with no frame limit (about 700 frames a second, tearing presents), in
two of three such runs, never in the menus (which ran at several thousand frames a second for about 15
minutes in all), never with vsync (the game's default) or the layer. The lost-device path is proven by
it. The user decided to stop the investigation there and record it; a later phase (real frames, testers'
GPUs) revisits it.

**The object check.** `main` (`d071462e`, `..\halo-main`) and the branch, each built with
`CCC_OVERRIDE_OPTIONS` mapping the tree to `.`: of `halo.exe`'s 726 objects, the same 8 differ as at phase
1's audit: `port_config.o`; phase 1's own `sdl_platform.o` and `win32_crash.o`; and `d3d8_gl.o`,
`xinput_sdl.o`, `actor_looking.o`, `aim_assist.o` (same sizes, differing only in their 32-digit checksum
strings) and `main.o` (`__TIME__`). Between phase 1's last commit `d943c612` and this phase's, nothing
`halo.exe` compiles changed but `port_config.c` (`git diff --stat`). The tree was rebuilt normally after.
Both trees built in about 51 s after `ninja -t clean windows`.

**The symbol difference**, `llvm-nm -g --defined-only` (the string COMDATs left out): exactly
`_xgpu_compile_shader`, `_xgpu_link_program`, `_xgpu_render_target_find` (106 against 103), as in phase 1.
`grep -E '\bgl[A-Z]\w+\('` on `d3d8_d3d11.c`: 0.

**The device against `d3d8_gl.c`**, function by function (a script that extracts the top-level functions):
against phase 1, `surface_is_shadow_map`, `render_target_remember`, `render_target_bucket`,
`scaled_pixel`, `target_pixel` and `ui_point_from_window_on` are back byte for byte. Changed only by the
backend's calls in the GL's place: `render_target_get` (the GL texture's lines become
`d3d11_backend_target_create` and its log line; `xgpu_gl_state_invalidate` goes), `bind_targets` (no
multisampling; the backend's handles; the target change counted by pair), `D3DDevice_Clear` (the
arithmetic whole; the GL masks become the channel bits, the scissors become the rectangles, row 0 at the
top), `write_screenshot` (`glReadPixels` becomes `d3d11_backend_target_read`; the Android swap goes),
`halo_screen_commit` (the `recent_render_targets` line is back, only the Android guard differs),
`D3DDevice_SetRenderTarget` (phase 1's count of target changes moves to `bind_targets`). `D3DDevice_Present`
is the backend's present in place of the resolve and the blit, the screenshot as before, and the pacing
rule.

**The backend against the documentation**: `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` with the device's own factory
(`IDXGIDevice::GetAdapter`, `GetParent`), flip-discard with two buffers, `B8G8R8A8_UNORM`,
`DXGI_SCALING_NONE`, `ALLOW_TEARING` only when `IDXGIFactory5::CheckFeatureSupport` says so;
`ResizeBuffers` with the swap chain's own flags, after the buffer's view is released and unbound and the
context flushed (no reference may remain); `Present`'s `DXGI_PRESENT_ALLOW_TEARING` only with interval 0
and never in DXGI's fullscreen (never used); `S_OK`, `DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED`, removed and reset handled, the
rest logged once; `ClearRenderTargetView` and `ClearDepthStencilView` ignore the scissor and write masks
(so only whole, all-channel clears go that way); `Map` with `D3D11_MAP_READ` on a staging texture waits
for the GPU, rows by `RowPitch`; `CopyResource` from a target of the same size and format; the back
buffer's view unbound from the pixel shader after the present's draw, and never bound as a target while it
is sampled (`OMSetRenderTargets` would unbind it with a warning). The failure paths read, as none can be
forced here: a lost device from any call (`d3d11_backend_failed`: the reason from
`GetDeviceRemovedReason`, the layer drained, every entry point returns at once, the device's line once,
`destroy` still at exit; met twice for real, above); `d3dcompiler_47.dll` missing or a built-in shader not
compiling (a line, drawn clears counted as 0, the present clears the swap chain's buffer black and
presents it); no swap chain (`d3d11: no swap chain (<call>: <hresult>)`, the window black, paced by the
platform layer); a target not made (a line, the entry skipped).

**Not done (the user stopped the testing)**: a10 on WARP; the four frame-rate runs of phase 1's timeline
(only the Adreno's run without the layer, cut at the reset; the averages above are from the runs that
were made); the window's transitions with the layer; the Xbox-style menus, "original" scaling and
exclusive fullscreen on WARP; `display.d3d11_adapter = "nonesuch"`; `halo.exe` through a10 (it ran the
menus, the quit dialog and a minimised spell, its log as before). Not done by the agent in any case:
hearing the sound; a gamepad.

### Phase 2: deviations from the spec

Everything phase 2 did differently from the "Phase 2 — the backend's skeleton" section, or beyond it.

**The device**

1. **The backend's handle is a member of `struct render_target_entry`**, not of `struct xgpu_render_target`
   (`xgpu.h` is shared and does not change).
2. **`Clear` counts every call before it looks at the backend** (phase 1's rule), then binds and clears; a
   clear of more than 16 rectangles goes to the backend in parts (the game gives one or none).
3. **Each target made is logged** (`Direct3D 11: target <n>: colour 64x64 for the surface at ...`), and
   the first clear that is drawn is logged once with its flags and rectangle, since the statistics' clears
   a frame hide an occasional one in the average.
4. **`display.vsync` and the marker are read when `config_changes()` moves**, and the marker reaches the
   backend through `d3d11_backend_present_marker(int)`, a fifth function beside the four the spec gives
   `present`; the swap chain's resizes reach the statistics through `d3d11_backend_resizes()`, and the
   lost device's reason through `d3d11_backend_lost_reason()`.

**The backend**

5. **A minimised window is found by `IsIconic`**, not by a size of 0: SDL keeps the window's last size,
   and a minimised flip-model window's `Present` answers `S_OK` (phase 0 saw it), so the first run
   presented and spun while minimised (8.2 s of CPU in 10 s). A frame nothing is shown in (minimised, or
   `DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED`) also sleeps 16 ms in the backend, the probe's rest, because with `max_fps = -1`
   the platform layer does not pace.
6. **`destroy` flushes the context after releasing the present's and the targets' objects**, not before:
   flushed first, the live-object report still counted the swap chain, the textures, views, shaders and
   states, which the context had not yet let go.
7. **`ResizeBuffers` is given the window's pixel size**, not 0 and 0, so the size asked for and the
   buffers' agree and a client area SDL reports differently cannot resize every frame; and a size it
   failed at is not asked for again until the window's size changes (the audit of my own code: a
   failure would have been retried and logged every frame).
8. **A depth target of another size than the colour target is left out of the binding** (logged once):
   Direct3D 11 binds the two only at one size, where GL drew into their intersection. Not met in the runs.
9. **The present's sampler, shaders and states are the clears' unit's** (`win32_d3d11.c`), which the
   present unit uses through `win32_d3d11.h`; `destroy` releases the present's view, swap chain and factory
   first, then the targets and these.
10. **The live-object summary's message counts 9 lines as phase 1's**; the layer's hint message carries a
    line break, which shows as an empty log line.

**Testing**

11. **The settings came from the environment** (above), and the game's log was read from its standard
    error, redirected to a file per run (the release build writes `halo.log` only without a console); lines
    were timed as they arrived, which comes in bursts, so the frame rates are averages over each span
    rather than phase 1's per-second minimum, median and maximum.
12. **Keys need their scan codes** (`MapVirtualKey`) for SDL to see them; the PC main menu's quit is QUIT
    (Up from Campaign, or Down from it four times) and its dialog's OK (Left, Enter), not Escape, Left,
    Enter as phase 1 wrote it.
13. **The pacing proof was a temporary count of the frames paced by the platform layer in the statistics
    line**, built for the vsync and `max_fps` runs and taken out before the commit.
14. **The Discord overlay** appears over `halo.exe`'s window (its captures show it); no overlay module is
    loaded in `halo_d3d11.exe`.

### Phase 1 — audit

The audit (a session of its own, after the five commits) found the record true to the code and nothing
blocking. Reproduced: the object check (both trees rebuilt with the path-mapping flag; 718 of 726 objects
byte-identical, the same eight differing for the reasons the summary gives), the symbol difference
(exactly the three named, 103 against 106), the GL grep (0), the function-by-function count (117, 96
byte-identical, 19 changed only by the GL lines or the readiness flag, 2 new), and that every line added
under `port/linux/src` is behind `_WIN32` (checked by script). The backend's calls match the Direct3D 11
and DXGI documentation. Found, none blocking, applied at the start of phase 2 ("Step 0"):

- **The build time does not reproduce**: the summary's 50 s from clean was 163 s (the branch) and 186 s
  (`main`) after `ninja -t clean windows`, with nothing else running; what "clean" covered in the summary
  is not recorded (the generated assets alone are a large part of a true clean build). Not a number a
  later phase compares against.
- `create_device` retries at 10_1 and 10_0 on `E_INVALIDARG` too, which a runtime without Direct3D 11.1
  also answers to a list naming 11_1; an 11_0 adapter there would be reported as below 11_0 (not
  reachable on Windows 10 or 11).
- `choose_adapter`'s loop by name ends only on `DXGI_ERROR_NOT_FOUND`; another failure would loop on an
  unset pointer (the probe has the same loop).
- The `--pgo=use` build (the Direct3D units with a profile flag and no profile) was called harmless
  without being tried; PGO is off here. Untested, and said so.
- The Windows README says the log "starts with" the `renderer:` line; the window's line comes first.
- The vsync stand-in reads the display's refresh rate when a setting changes, not when the window moves
  to another display (phase 2 says what that is worth).
- Phase 1's "What this phase is" forgot `win32_crash.o` in the list of objects that may differ; corrected.

### Phase 1 — summary

Worked on this PC (Snapdragon X Plus, Adreno X1-45, Windows 11 on Arm64, the game x86 under emulation),
release build, `--portable`, full LTO, PGO off (clang 20). Code in commits `2b576bef` (the platform
layer's no-context mode and the two settings rows), `7263de11` (the device, the backend, the stubs, the
build), `525dae63` (the crash line, the CI outputs) and `58026aeb` (the READMEs). **`halo_d3d11.exe` runs
the menus and a10's opening into the cryo room with a black window, on the Adreno and on WARP, with and
without the debug layer, and exits clean.** Nothing crashed in any run.

**The log line**, word for word:

```
renderer: Direct3D 11 on Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU, feature level 11_1, driver 31.0.137.0, debug layer off
renderer: Direct3D 11 on Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU, feature level 11_1, driver 31.0.137.0, debug layer on
renderer: Direct3D 11 on Microsoft Basic Render Driver (WARP), feature level 11_1, driver 10.0.26100.9444, debug layer off
renderer: Direct3D 11 on Microsoft Basic Render Driver (WARP), feature level 11_1, driver 10.0.26100.9444, debug layer on
```

Before it, the platform layer's `window: 1280x960 pixels, no OpenGL context (the renderer presents)`;
after it, `gl stub: glFlush called from 0068F63E (halo_d3d11.exe+0x22f63e)` and `gl stubs: self-test ok`
(the self-test's own call; see the stubs below). `halo.exe` still prints `OpenGL 4.6 (Core Profile)
Mesa 26.3.0-devel (git-e3fc7749c6) on D3D12 (Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU)` and no new line.

**The runs.** Each Direct3D run: the window (`display.mode = "windowed"`, 1280x960, so `screen: 640x480
drawn at 1280x960`), the main menu idle for 185 s, then `map_name a10` from the script console, `look:1`
input, the cinematic, the cryo room, and the window's close button at 770 s. Settings as phase 0's
baseline (`display.vsync = false`, `display.max_fps = -1`, `debug.gpu_stats`, `debug.telnet_console`,
`debug.update_answer = "no"`), with `audio.volume` 0.05 from the environment (`HALO_VOLUME`; the user was
at the PC). Captures of the window (made topmost first) at 100, 400 and 740 s on all four runs: the
title bar there, the client area 100% black.

- Adreno, Adreno with the layer, WARP, WARP with the layer: each `exit 0`, the log ending with `window
  closed` and `d3d11: device released`.
- **The debug layer**: no `d3d11 debug:` line in the menus or the map on either driver, and every
  statistics line says `0 debug layer messages`. At exit, `ReportLiveDeviceObjects(D3D11_RLDO_SUMMARY)`
  gives 9 warnings on both, the device's own objects only, as phase 0 saw: `Live ID3D11Device ...
  Refcount: 4`, then one each of `ID3D11Context`, `ID3DDeviceContextState`, `ID3D11BlendState`,
  `ID3D11DepthStencilState`, `ID3D11RasterizerState`, `ID3D11Sampler`, `ID3D11Query`, and the layer's
  hint about `D3D11_RLDO_DETAIL`; then `d3d11: the live objects at the end, from the debug layer: 9
  messages`.
- **The statistics line**: `frame <n>: Direct3D 11 draws nothing yet; 7 draws, 138 immediate, 1 clears
  asked for; 0 GL stub calls` in the PC main menu (the OpenGL build's line there: 7 draws, 130
  immediate), 94 immediate in the Xbox-style menus, 165 to 285 draws and 21 to 238 immediate a frame in
  a10.
- **The cryo room was reached**, as far as a black window can tell. The game kept real time
  (`game_time` from the console: 30 ticks a second throughout a run of its own), and the statistics show
  the cinematic, then the room: from `map_name` to about 225 s after it the counts swing (0 to 499 draws,
  10 to 954 immediate a frame), and from then to the end they repeat a steady pattern of 180 to 370 draws
  and 180 to 294 immediate, the counts phase 0's OpenGL run had standing in the cryo room (230 to 310
  draws, 230 to 270 immediate). The game's own look test (`player_action_test_look_relative_all_directions`
  after `player_action_test_reset`) was tried as a proof of control and does not tell: it was true during
  the cinematic too.
- **Sound**: the port logs a line about audio only when the device cannot be opened (`dsound_sdl.c`), and
  no such line appeared; the game's `debug.txt` has only what the OpenGL runs of phase 0 have too (`no
  DirectSound for bink`, the Bink stub; and in a10 `too many lens flares submitted to frame`, which phase
  0's OpenGL run logged at the same point, and the `fell outside world` and `object_create` warnings).
  The agent cannot hear it.
- **Input**: `look:1` (the script console's test above), the menus by keys (below), F11.
- **Exit through the menu**: Escape, Left, Enter (the PC main menu's quit dialog, its OK; found on
  `halo.exe`, whose menu can be seen): `window closed`, `d3d11: device released`, `exit 0`. Through the
  close button: every other run.
- **The menus**: the PC menus with Down, Enter and Escape through a few screens, and the Xbox-style
  menus (`display.menus = "xbox"`) idle for 65 s and again with Enter, Down and Escape through a few
  screens: no stub call, no error.
- **F11 and a resize**: F11 to borderless (`screen: 852x480 drawn at 2880x1620`) and back (`640x480
  drawn at 1280x960`); the window resized to 1600x1000, 800x600 and 1900x1200 from outside: the screen's
  size followed each (`802x480 drawn at 1578x944`, ...), no crash.
- **`display.vsync = true`, `display.max_fps = 0`** (the stand-in for vsync): frames 600 to 1,800 in
  9.98 s on the Adreno and 10.04 s on WARP, **120 a second, the display's refresh**. (`config.toml` was
  changed for these two runs and put back byte for byte.)

**The failure paths.** Tried: `display.d3d11_adapter = "nonesuch"` gives `renderer: Direct3D 11 on
Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU, feature level 11_1, driver 31.0.137.0, debug layer off ("nonesuch"
matched no adapter)` and the game runs; `debug.null_renderer = true` gives `Direct3D: running without a
window (nothing is displayed)`, the line `halo.exe` prints, no window and no device (no statistics either,
as in `halo.exe`: the settings are read with the device); `debug.screenshot_every = 300` gives its one
line, `debug.screenshot_every: no screenshots until the Direct3D 11 renderer presents (phase 2)`. Read in
the audit, as none can be forced here: the layer's fallback on `DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING` (the
line then says `debug layer missing (DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING; install the Graphics Tools
feature)`); `unavailable: no adapter (...)` (no factory, or no adapter at all);
`unavailable: D3D11CreateDevice failed (...)`; `unavailable: feature level 10_1 is below 11_0` (asked
again at 10_1 and 10_0 only on `DXGI_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED` or `E_INVALIDARG`); a failed stub self-test (a
loud line, and the device not used); `gl_functions_load` reached (a line, and a failure).

**The object check.** `main` (`d071462e`) in a worktree (`..\halo-main`) and the branch, each built with
`configure.py --portable --release` and `ninja windows`, with clang's `CCC_OVERRIDE_OPTIONS` adding
`-ffile-prefix-map=<the tree>=.`: without it every object differs, since full LTO's bitcode carries the
build folder's path in its debug info. Of `halo.exe`'s 726 objects (`ninja -t inputs`), 718 are
byte-identical. `port_config.o` and `sdl_platform.o` differ, as expected. `win32_crash.o` differs by the
crash line's change the phase makes (its "Files" table lists it; "What this phase is" forgot it). Five
more differ by 13 to 43 bytes at the same size, each only by a 32-digit checksum string of the debug
info, which records an MD5 of every source and header file: `d3d8_gl.o` and `xinput_sdl.o` include
`sdl_platform.h`, which gained the two declarations; `actor_looking.o` and `aim_assist.o` are compiled
through the inline-export wrappers (`build/windows/inline_exports/`), whose `#include` names the tree's
absolute path; and `main.o` holds `__DATE__` and `__TIME__` (`main.c:1788`), the compile's minute. No
code differs.

**The symbol difference**, `llvm-nm -g --defined-only` on `d3d8_gl.o` and `d3d8_d3d11.o` (the
compiler's string-literal COMDATs, `??_C@...`, left out on both sides): exactly `_xgpu_compile_shader`,
`_xgpu_link_program`, `_xgpu_render_target_find`; `d3d8_d3d11.o` defines nothing `d3d8_gl.o` does not
(103 against 106). `xgpu_capabilities` is in neither. `grep -E '\bgl[A-Z]\w+\('` on `d3d8_d3d11.c`: 0.

**The device against `d3d8_gl.c`**, function by function (a script that extracts each top-level
function of both files and compares them): 117 functions in the copy, **96 byte-for-byte** (27 of them
the `COMPLEX_RENDER_STATE` setters), 19 changed, 2 new, 70 of `d3d8_gl.c`'s left out. Changed only by
the Android side or the GL lines taken out: `anti_aliasing_read` (no `anti_aliasing_prepare`),
`screen_mode_choose`, `xgpu_gl_state_invalidate` (empty), `D3DDevice_KickPushBuffer` (empty),
`halo_screen_commit` (no `recent_render_targets` to forget), `d3d8_renderer_description`. Changed by
`gl_ready` becoming `window_ready` or `backend_ready`: `halo_ui_pointer_update`,
`halo_scoreboard_pointer_update`, `D3DDevice_BeginVisibilityTest`, `D3DDevice_EndVisibilityTest`.
`ui_point_from_window_on` takes the back buffer's pixels from the new `back_buffer_pixels` (what
`render_target_get` gave it: the surface's size at the screen's scale). The stubs that keep their state
and count: `DrawVertices`, `DrawIndexedVertices`, `End`, `Clear` (its trace line kept),
`halo_screen_anti_alias` (empty), `GetVisibilityTestResult` (0 samples, `S_OK`, `time_stamp` 0).
`Direct3D_CreateDevice` calls `platform_video_without_gl` and the new `backend_initialize` in place of
`gl_initialize` (what `gl_initialize` did that is not GL: the attributes' w of 1, `memory_watch_initialize`,
the debugging settings, the anti-aliasing setting's first read). `Present` keeps the frame count, the
statistics, `platform_pump_events` and the vertical blank block byte for byte. The copy was made from
`d3d8_gl.c` at `2765d816` (its last change), and its header says so.

**The backend against the documentation** (`D3D11CreateDevice`, `CreateDXGIFactory1`,
`IDXGIFactory6::EnumAdapterByGpuPreference`, `IDXGIAdapter::CheckInterfaceSupport`,
`ID3D11InfoQueue`, `ID3D11Debug::ReportLiveDeviceObjects`): an adapter given with
`D3D_DRIVER_TYPE_UNKNOWN`, WARP with no adapter; 11_1 in the level list (a runtime without 11.1 would
answer `E_INVALIDARG`; Windows 10 and 11 have it); the debug flag's absence answered by
`DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING`; the info queue read with `GetMessage`'s two calls and cleared;
`ClearState` and `Flush` before the context is released; everything released in reverse order.
`CheckInterfaceSupport` is documented for Direct3D 10 interfaces only; with `IID_IDXGIDevice` it gives the
user-mode driver's version on both drivers here, as phase 0 found, and the line says `not given` where it
does not. The debug layer's own run is above.

**The GL stub calls seen: none**, in any run (the PC and Xbox-style menus idle and navigated, a10 to the
cryo room, F11, resizes): every statistics line says `0 GL stub calls`, and no `gl stub:` line but the
self-test's. The self-test's address symbolizes (`llvm-symbolizer --obj=build/windows/halo_d3d11.exe
--relative-address 0x22f63e`) to `rasterizer_preinitialize` at `rasterizer_xbox.c:1935`, the device's
creation, which full LTO inlined there. As in the Vulkan plan, the shared files' GL calls are reached only
through the renderer: `menu_files.c`'s `glDeleteTextures` (a replaced menu art) was not reached by the
menus tried. Phase 6 reads this count again.

**The frame rate with nothing drawn**, frames a second in each second of the window (minimum / median /
maximum), from the statistics lines timed as they reached `halo.log`. The windows are phase 0's, from the
first statistics line, with the map started at 185 s: the menu 120–180 s, the cinematic 385–565 s, the cryo
room 700–760 s.

| Scene | OpenGL, phase 0 (borderless 2880x1620) | OpenGL, windowed, this phase | Adreno | Adreno, layer | WARP | WARP, layer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Main menu, idle | 166 / 232 / 265 | 217 / 287 / 352 | 10,486 / 11,163 / 11,439 | 12,229 / 12,899 / 13,069 | 12,297 / 13,150 / 13,456 | 12,225 / 12,888 / 13,075 |
| a10's opening cinematic | 2.7 / 7.1 / 77 | 1.2 / 1.6 / 4.0 | 1,315 / 1,784 / 2,753 | 1,421 / 1,974 / 3,161 | 1,483 / 1,979 / 3,112 | 1,488 / 1,979 / 3,050 |
| a10, the cryo room, `look:1` | 13.4 / 21.9 / 32.8 | (still in the cinematic at 740 s) | 1,366 / 1,735 / 2,332 | 1,483 / 1,915 / 2,633 | 1,435 / 1,971 / 2,699 | 1,482 / 1,965 / 2,635 |

What the game costs on this PC with nothing drawn: about 0.09 ms a frame in the menu and 0.5 ms in a10
(deko3d's phase 1 found 0.5 ms). With nothing drawn the driver should not matter: three runs agree, and
the Adreno run without the layer was 10 to 15% slower throughout, which is not explained (the user was
working on the PC during the runs). The OpenGL
build's windowed run here was slower than phase 0's in the map: at 1.6 frames a second its game time
fell behind, and it was still in the cinematic (seen) at 740 s.

**The build**: `ninja windows` from clean, both executables under full LTO, **50 s**, the same as `main`'s
`halo.exe` alone (50 s, built the same way; phase 0 measured 58 s): the two links run side by side.
No warning. `halo_d3d11.exe` 18.1 MB, `halo_d3d11.pdb` beside it.

**What is left for the user:** nothing is known to be. Not done by the agent: hearing the sound; a gamepad
(none here); reaching `menu_files.c`'s replaced menu art to see its stub call.

### Phase 1: deviations from the spec

Everything phase 1 did differently from the "Phase 1 — the Direct3D executable" section, or beyond it.

**The device**

1. **The backend is not destroyed from `D3DDevice_Release`**, only by the `atexit` handler. The game
   makes the device, presents once and releases it in `rasterizer_preinitialize`
   (`rasterizer_xbox.c` 1950), then makes it again; a destroy there would leave the game with no device.
   `D3DDevice_Release` is `d3d8_gl.c`'s, byte for byte. Every exit seen (the close button, the menu's
   quit) goes through `exit()`, so the handler runs, and its line is in every log.
2. **The draws are counted as asked for**: `DrawVertices` and `DrawIndexedVertices` count one with
   vertices (and indices), `End` one with vertices, `Clear` every call, whatever the backend's state.
   `d3d8_gl.c` counts a draw only once its program is made. The line says "asked for".
3. **The statistics line** gives the draws a frame as `d3d8_gl.c`'s does (`stats` over the 60 frames),
   the GL stub calls since the self-test (the self-test's own call subtracted), and `; <n> debug layer
   messages` (since the last line) only when `debug.d3d11_debug` is set: the device asks no more of the
   backend than the three functions.
4. **The frame is paced whenever there is a window**, the backend made or not, so a machine without a
   device does not spin. `d3d8_gl.c` swaps only with its context.
5. **The menus' pointer works with the window** (`window_ready`), not with the backend, and takes the back
   buffer's pixels from `back_buffer_pixels` (the surface's size at the screen's scale), which is what
   `render_target_get` gave `ui_point_from_window_on`; `render_target_get` itself is gone with the
   targets.
6. **Anti-aliasing**: `anti_aliasing_prepare` is gone (it built the passes and read the GPU's sample
   count); the setting is still read and logged as before. `maximum_target_size` stays 0, so `ssaa2x`
   draws at the screen's scale until phase 6 asks the backend.
7. **The visibility tests' begin and end** keep only `visibility_test_active`, behind `backend_ready`
   as `d3d8_gl.c`'s are behind `gl_ready`. `trace_draw` is gone (it asks `xgpu_render_target_find`);
   `trace_frame` stays for `SetRenderTarget`'s and `Clear`'s trace lines.
8. **The stub self-test** fails loudly by a log line and by not using the device (`backend_ready` false),
   not by stopping the game.

**The backend**

9. **The info queue is first drained at the first `Present`**, not at creation, so that the `renderer:`
   line, which the device logs, comes first; `d3d11_backend_destroy` drains what is left before the
   live-object report. A second `d3d11_backend_create` returns the device made before, with a line that
   says so.
10. **Below 11_0** the adapter is asked again at 10_1 and 10_0 only when the first answer was
    `DXGI_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED` or `E_INVALIDARG`, so that another failure (a debug layer that fails in
    another way) is not named a feature level.
11. **The backend logs `d3d11: device released`** at the end of `d3d11_backend_destroy`, the "destroy
    line" the testing asks for, and the live objects' count as `d3d11: the live objects at the end, from
    the debug layer: <n> messages`.

**The platform layer, the stubs and the build**

12. **The no-context window is a function of its own**, `platform_video_initialize_without_gl`, which
    `platform_video_initialize` calls first when the flag is set, rather than conditions through the
    OpenGL path: the desktop window's lines are repeated without `SDL_WINDOW_OPENGL` (fullscreen kind,
    event thread, mouse capture). The OpenGL path's lines are untouched.
13. **The two settings rows are behind `#ifdef _WIN32`** (the existing `_platform_windows` row,
    `crash_reports.upload`, is not), so the Linux and Android tables do not change at all.
14. **`gl_stubs.c` also defines `gl_functions_load`**: `sdl_platform.c` references it on its OpenGL path,
    which the link needs, and here it logs a line and fails.
15. **A stub's first caller is logged with its offset into the executable** (`halo_d3d11.exe+0x...`,
    from `__ImageBase`) beside the address, because the executable is relocated (ASLR); the offset is
    what `llvm-symbolizer --relative-address` takes.
16. **`emit()` builds the Direct3D executable itself** when given its path (and returns its objects): the
    Direct3D units need the same flags `emit()` builds, and the training build of `--pgo=train` simply
    does not ask for it.
17. **The object check's method** (above): both trees built with `-ffile-prefix-map` through
    `CCC_OVERRIDE_OPTIONS`, and three more objects than the two named differ, each explained.
    `win32_crash.o` is the phase's own change.

**Testing**

18. **The runs were windowed** (1280x960), where phase 0's baseline was borderless at 2880x1620, because
    the user was using the PC; the window is what the captures and the title bar need. With nothing
    drawn the resolution costs nothing, and the game's width is 640 instead of 852. The sound was at
    `audio.volume` 0.05.
19. **The cryo room was told from the statistics and the game's clock**, not seen: the window is black,
    and the game's look test does not tell control apart. See the summary.
20. **The menus were driven by keys** sent to the window (`keybd_event`, arrow keys with the extended-key
    flag, which SDL otherwise reads as the keypad's), the quit's sequence first found on `halo.exe`.
    Partway through the work the session locked: captures taken while it was locked are black in the
    title bar too, and were not counted. The four main runs' captures were made before it.

### Phase 0 — the `present` step's picture seen

The user asked the agent to run the tester's script instead of a person. The two earlier sets of
captures had failed only because other windows were on top of the probe's, so this time the key
script makes the probe's window topmost before each screen capture, and the captures show what the
display shows. The probe was run with `all --quiet`, the debug layer on, sync 1, on the Adreno and
on WARP (the reports are like the debug-layer ones and are not kept: 11 keys each, all the
script's, every Present `S_OK`, no layer message, exit 0).

The agent looked at each capture: windowed, after the resize by the edge, borderless, SDL's
exclusive fullscreen, restored after minimising, and after Alt+Enter. On both drivers the clear
colour cycles (red, orange, teal, yellow and purple at the six captures) and the white triangle is
at the top left, pointing up. Borderless and exclusive fill the whole 2880x1620 display, and the
window stays a window after Alt+Enter. The captures are not committed, because they include other
windows on the user's desktop.

Nothing about presenting is left for a person. A gamepad was not tried (there is none here), and
the user judged it unnecessary: gamepads are SDL's, not Direct3D's, and that path is the game's own,
well tested; the keyboard's events already show SDL's input working beside a Direct3D window.

### Phase 0 — the runs with the debug layer

The user installed the Graphics Tools feature and restarted the PC, and the four runs were made
again: `all`, Adreno and WARP, sync 1 and sync 0, with `present` driven by the same key script.
This time no key of the user's reached the window: each run has the script's 11 keys only. The
reports are `snapdragon_x_plus_adreno_x1_45_{hardware,warp}_sync{1,0}_debug_layer.txt`. They say
`build.git: d071462e+changes`, because the executable was not rebuilt after the commit, but its code
is that of `2e3188ea`.

- `debug_layer: yes (0x00000000 S_OK)` on both drivers.
- **No message from the layer in any step**, on either driver: no error, no warning and no
  information, in `caps`, `compile`, `memory`, `queries` and `present`. That includes the
  swap chain, every resize, fullscreen both ways, minimise and Alt+Enter.
- At the end, `ReportLiveDeviceObjects(D3D11_RLDO_SUMMARY)` gives 9 warnings, the same as in a run
  of `caps` alone: the device (refcount 4: the probe's reference, the info queue's and the
  runtime's own) and the immediate context's own objects (`ID3D11Context`,
  `ID3DDeviceContextState`, one default blend, depth-stencil, rasterizer and sampler state, and one
  query). The probe leaves none of its own objects alive.
- The rewrite test passes on all four paths on both drivers. All runs exit 0, and no guard fired.
- `present` with the layer: every Present `S_OK` (8,501 to 330,156 presents). Sync 1 is 120 Hz
  (median 8.33 ms) on both drivers. Every transition presents again within 37 ms. **With sync 0 the
  layer slows the Adreno** to a median of 1.0–1.5 ms a present (about 1,000 a second, against
  about 9,000 without it), and WARP not at all (0.17–0.20 ms).

The phase's acceptance item "with and without the debug layer" is met. The probe's
live-object report and its handling of the layer's messages are now exercised (deviation 15 below
no longer holds).

### Phase 0 — summary

**The first finding: the emulated x86 process gets the Adreno.** With `--adapter hardware` the probe
takes the first adapter that `IDXGIFactory6::EnumAdapterByGpuPreference` lists and that is not a
software adapter. It makes the device on that adapter at feature level 11_1:

```
machine.is_wow64_process2: process x86 (0x014c), native arm64 (0xaa64)
adapter.0.description: Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU
adapter.0.driver_version: 31.0.137.0
device.feature_level: 11_1
device.adapter: Qualcomm(R) Adreno(TM) X1-45 GPU (vendor 0x4d4f4351, device 0x37314430)
device.on_qualcomm: yes
```

The vendor ID is the older `0x4D4F4351` ("QCOM"), not `0x5143`. `GetNativeSystemInfo` says x64 in
this process, because of the emulation. `IsWow64Process2` gives the true machine. Microsoft Basic
Render Driver is the second adapter in the list.

Probe build `d071462e+changes` (the probe, uncommitted when the reports were made, on top of
`d071462e`). The reports are in `port/windows/probe/reports/`, one for each driver and sync
interval: `snapdragon_x_plus_adreno_x1_45_{hardware,warp}_sync{1,0}.txt`. None of them has the
debug layer: `debug_layer: no (0x887a002d DXGI_ERROR_SDK_COMPONENT_MISSING)`, because the Graphics
Tools feature was not installed yet. The runs with it are in the entry above. No step crashed, so no guard fired. Each
run exited 0.

`D3DCompile` on the Adreno run, sync 1 (wall time: the median of runs 2 to 10; the first run of
the process is separate):

| Shader | Source | `_4_0`, level 3 | bytecode | `_5_0`, level 3 | bytecode | `_4_0`, level 1 | `_4_0`, skip optimisation (bytecode) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vs_large` (vs058_0, the largest of 19) | 11.8 KB | **146 ms** | 4,960 B | 169 ms | 5,116 B | 144 ms | 48 ms (10,588 B) |
| `vs_typical` (vs004_1, the median) | 7.4 KB | **24 ms** | 2,712 B | 27 ms | 2,892 B | 26 ms | 14 ms (5,344 B) |
